# TESTS_AUDIT — test completeness and sufficiency audit

> **Provenance.** Audited 2026-07-25 against `master` at `885f55c` plus the then-in-flight Codex
> hardening work (since merged). Verified again against `3492b74` before publication. Line numbers
> are accurate as of those trees and will drift; every finding names a symbol as well as a line, so
> `grep` for the symbol rather than trusting the line. Nothing in this document was applied as a
> code change — it is a findings register, not a changelog.
>
> **Method.** 27 agents: 2 establishing the system map and the real behaviour of the deploy gate,
> 12 domain auditors reading source against tests, 12 adversarial verifiers whose instruction was to
> *refute* each auditor's claim by finding an existing test that already covered it, and 1
> completeness critic looking for what all of them missed. 141 findings survived verification
> (52 CONFIRMED, 86 ADJUSTED — narrowed or downgraded, 3 UNVERIFIED, 0 REFUTED). Claims that a
> behaviour is untested were checked repo-wide across languages, because a Rust behaviour may be
> covered by a shell e2e script and a TypeScript behaviour by another package's suite.

**How to read a finding.** Each carries an id, a severity, a verifier verdict, the source symbol at
risk, what is *not* covered today, the production consequence, and a concrete test to add. Severity
is blast radius, not line count: `critical` means money moves wrongly or an authorization boundary
is unenforced, and nothing in the suite would notice.

---

## 1. Executive summary

The test suite is not thin — ~200 Rust `#[test]`s, 318 TypeScript `it()`/`test()` blocks across 69
files, and 711 lines of bash test — and the deploy gate that runs it is stronger than most projects'
CI. The problem is not volume, it is **placement**: test density correlates *negatively* with blast
radius. The most densely tested crate (`metering`, 51 LOC/test) is the one whose worst failure is a
wrong number in a table a human reviews; the least densely tested (`server`, 328 LOC/test) contains
the `/admin/*` money control plane, and `admin.rs` has zero tests across 908 lines.

Three structural facts explain most of the 141 findings:

1. **There is no HTTP test harness anywhere in the Rust workspace.** No crate declares
   `[dev-dependencies]`; `crates/pool/tests/` and `crates/registry/tests/` are empty directories.
   `forward::proxy::forward` — the ~730-line handler holding the retry budget, blame classification,
   capacity lease, RAII guards, header stripping and rotation — has never been invoked by a test.
   Streaming and mid-stream failover are untested end to end.
2. **There is no local feedback loop.** 16 `describe.runIf(TEST_DATABASE_URL)` suites and
   `registry::pg::tests::stage2_fault_matrix` silently no-op to green on a developer machine. The
   entire live-database money surface — the best-tested part of the commerce layer — is invisible
   until after `git push`.
3. **The gate does not protect itself, and it self-installs.** `watchdog-infrastructure.sh` runs the
   candidate's own `install-watchdog.sh`, and `watchdog-lib.test.sh` pins only 2 of the ~10 steps in
   `prepare_and_test_candidate`. A commit deleting the `cargo test` line passes the current gate,
   installs itself, and every subsequent commit ships with no Rust tests.

The audit also surfaced two defects that are live rather than hypothetical — see §4. One is a test
that has been silently disabled by a lost newline; the other is an anti-abuse billing control that
cannot fire in production because an untested caller filters away the input its tested parser needs.

---

## 2. What the gate actually runs

Recorded because the audit's severity calls depend on it, and because it is easy to under-estimate
from `.github/` alone.

`deploy/watchdog.sh` (`prepare_and_test_candidate`) validates each candidate SHA in an isolated
clone, as an unprivileged CI user:

| Step | Command |
|---|---|
| frozen install | `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` |
| build | `pnpm build` |
| types | `pnpm typecheck` |
| commerce schema | `node packages/db/dist/migrate.js` against disposable PostgreSQL |
| sales schema | `node packages/sales-db/dist/migrate.js` against a second disposable database |
| TypeScript tests | `pnpm -r --workspace-concurrency=1 --if-present test` with `TEST_DATABASE_URL` **and** `TEST_SALES_DATABASE_URL` exported |
| Rust tests | `cargo test --locked --workspace` with `CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL` exported |
| whitespace | `git diff --check` across the processed range |
| shell syntax | `bash -n` over every `deploy/*.sh` |
| bash suites | `watchdog-lib.test.sh`, `monitoring-config.test.sh` |
| cleanliness | fails if the tests modified tracked files |

The integration suites really do run against real PostgreSQL on every push, and `stage2_fault_matrix`
really does execute. This is the single biggest reason most findings below are gaps rather than
outages. Two structural caveats, developed in §6: every gated database starts **empty**, and
`--if-present` means a package with no `test` script passes silently.

Outside this gate there is no CI: `.github/` contains one workflow, an SEO ping. No clippy, rustfmt,
eslint, `cargo audit` or `cargo deny` runs anywhere.

---

## 3. Coverage density vs blast radius

Measured 2026-07-25 (`wc -l` over `src`, `grep -c '#\[test\]|#\[tokio::test\]'`).

| Crate | LOC | Tests | LOC/test | Worst realistic failure |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `metering` | 1286 | 25 | **51** | a wrong rate in a table a human reviews |
| `pool` | 2779 | 49 | **57** | suboptimal routing; no I/O, no money |
| `forward` | 13833 | 83 | 167 | wrong charge, leaked stream, failed rotation |
| `registry` | 6052 | 29 | 209 | **the money authority**: double/lost charge, stranded hold |
| `server` | 4922 | 15 | **328** | **the `/admin/*` money control plane** |

At file level the inversion is sharper:

| File | LOC | Tests | Role |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| `crates/forward/src/meter.rs` | 428 | **0** | closes the money loop on every request |
| `crates/server/src/admin.rs` | 908 | **0** | 13 handlers that mint balance and issue live keys |
| `crates/server/src/main.rs` | 1261 | **0** | CLI, startup, epoch claim, drain |
| `crates/forward/src/billing.rs` | 1417 | **1** | owns the writer connection to the authority |
| `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs` | 1939 | 11 | all 11 cover pure helpers; `forward()` itself is untested |

The same shape repeats in TypeScript: `packages/payments` and `packages/engine-client` (pure,
reviewable) carry 27 tests, while `apps/worker`'s four background loops — ~730 LOC of transactional
money logic — carry none.

---

## 4. Live defects found during the audit

Both re-verified against `3492b74`. Unlike the rest of this document these are not coverage gaps;
they are current behaviour.

### 4.1 A test has been silently disabled since a lost newline

`crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2646`:

```rust
/// settle пишет usage_event В ТОЙ ЖЕ операции (один коммит); при actual=0 usage НЕ пишется.    #[test]
fn settle_writes_usage_event_in_same_tx() {
```

The `#[test]` attribute was absorbed into the doc comment, so the function is never registered as a
test. `cargo test` reports it only as a `dead_code` warning. Fix: move `#[test]` to its own line.
The compiler warning is the only current signal, which is itself an argument for denying warnings in
the gate.

### 4.2 The anti-abuse billing fallback cannot fire in production

`metering::usage_from_sse` (`crates/metering/src/lib.rs:476-546`) accumulates `delta_bytes` from
`content_block_delta` events and, when no terminal `message_delta` arrived
(`have_output_usage == false`, line 545), charges
`output_tokens = max(message_start_value, delta_bytes)`. This is deliberate and documented in the
source as an anti-abuse control, and it is tested at `lib.rs:926` and `lib.rs:945`.

`crates/forward/src/meter.rs:166-169` accumulates only lines whose `type` is `message_start`,
`message_delta` or `error`. `content_block_delta` never reaches the parser, so in production
`delta_bytes` is always 0 and the fallback cannot fire. A client that streams a long generation and
disconnects before the terminal `message_delta` is charged the `message_start` value — typically
**1 output token**. Complete streams are unaffected, because `message_delta` carries authoritative
usage. The same filter zeroes `observed_web_search_requests` from `content_block_start`, which
matters only on the same truncated path.

The filter landed 2026-07-22 (`dcd6459`), six days after the fallback (`b9c681d`), and every
metering test stayed green — because they call `usage_from_sse` directly with a full transcript that
production never produces. This is the canonical failure mode of a well-tested pure function behind
an untested caller, and it is the concrete argument for finding `forward-tee-meter-sse` below.

Fix: retain `content_block_start` and `content_block_delta` in the meter's accumulator (respecting
`SSE_USAGE_CAP`), or track delivered text bytes separately in `TeeMeter`. Either way the regression
test belongs at the `TeeMeter` level, not in `metering`.

---

## 5. What is good

Recorded deliberately: several of these are patterns worth copying into the areas that lack tests.

- **Money invariants tested as properties, not literals.** `proxy.rs:1806
  cap_to_balance_enforces_budget` sweeps 4 multipliers × 4 balances and asserts `hold ≤ balance+$1`
  and worst-case-charge bounds rather than three hardcoded numbers.
- **Price tables encode the traps that cost money.** `metering/src/lib.rs:710` pins that
  `claude-opus-4-20250514` bills $75/M rather than falling into the $25/M substring default — a 3×
  error someone had already been bitten by. `:746` loops every known model asserting the fail-safe
  ceiling dominates it, so an unknown model overcharges rather than undercharges.
- **Adversarial inputs chosen to fail under float math.** `engine-client/src/index.test.ts:7-24` and
  `web/src/lib/api.test.ts:18-24` both use 2^53+1 to prove money survives serialization as exact
  integers — a value that cannot pass by accident.
- **Real subprocesses and real sockets where mocks would lie.** `codex/runner.rs:783` spawns an
  actual child that the production spawn path digest- and version-verifies; `nodetls.rs:81` binds a
  TCP listener and asserts raw on-wire header case and ordering; `codex/process.rs:885` feeds
  32 MiB + 1 bytes and asserts the cap held *before* allocation, not after.
- **Concurrency tested for real.** `payments.integration.test.ts:137` and `email.integration.test.ts:38`
  fire two claims via `Promise.all` against live PostgreSQL and assert exactly one winner —
  `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` semantics no mock reproduces.
- **Invariants asserted at both layers.** `content-studio.integration.test.ts:88` proves the
  blog-first rule through the service, then bypasses the service with a raw `INSERT` to prove the
  database `CHECK` rejects it too.
- **Boundaries asserted at the boundary.** `refund-eligibility.integration.test.ts:74` asserts 5 days
  eligible and 5 days + 1 ms not; `sales-db/periods.test.ts:72` asserts `phaseOf` at the exact
  instant of every transition rather than at comfortable midpoints.
- **Structural tests that cannot rot silently.** `metering`'s `ALL_LOCAL_ERRS` array forces a
  compile-visible decision when an error variant is added, and the test then sweeps every variant
  against a forbidden-substring list (`proxy.rs:1857`, and its Codex twin at `codex/api.rs:2154`).
- **The bash suite is better than most.** `watchdog-lib.test.sh:56-98` copies the real migrations
  tree three times to prove append-only-ness against actual repo content; `:334-380` proves the
  ERR/EXIT quarantine trap behaviourally, including the subshell case.
- **`crates/pool` (49 tests) and `crates/metering` (25) are genuinely well covered**, including
  cooling survival across deploys, calibration rejecting implausible samples, and the Sonnet-5
  intro-pricing cutoff asserted at the exact boundary second.

One architectural invariant needs no test at all and should not acquire one: the layer direction
`registry ← pool ← forward ← server` is enforced by Cargo itself — a cycle does not compile.

---

## 6. Cross-cutting gaps

These are properties of the suite as a whole; no per-file audit can see them.

### 6.1 CLAUDE.md invariants: enforced or not

| Invariant | Status |
|---|---|
| Layer direction `registry ← pool ← forward ← server` | **Enforced by Cargo.** No test needed |
| `registry`/`pool` do no network/HTTP | **Unenforced.** Cargo prevents cycles, not new dependencies; adding `reqwest` to `pool` compiles green |
| env read only in `crates/server/src/config.rs` | **Holds today, unenforced.** The only `std::env` outside it are `metrics_store.rs` (`temp_dir`), the `pg.rs` test gate, and `temp_dir()` in test modules |
| Integer-only money end to end | **Partially enforced.** One confirmed hole: `apps/sales-web` does `Number(BigInt(x) / 1_000_000_000n)` in two admin/promo pages — and that package has no `test` script at all |
| Browser API never trusts client `user_id` | **Enforced by types, guard untested.** No controller binds a user id from `@Body()`/`@Query()`/`@Param()`; ownership always arrives via `@CurrentAuth()`. But every controller test constructs the class with `new`, so `SessionAuthGuard` and `OriginGuard` are bypassed in every test |
| Secrets never logged | **Structurally unenforced.** The two leak tests check *error text*, not logs; `mask_proxy`, `mask_key` and `read_secret_file` have no tests |
| Expand-only migrations | **Enforced for `packages/db` only** (`wd_manifest_is_append_only`). `packages/sales-db/migrations` and `crates/registry/migrations_pg` are outside the manifest |
| Byte-transparent Anthropic protocol | **Structurally unenforced.** No test anywhere compares an upstream body to a downstream body |

The cheapest high-value item on this list is the env-locality check: one `grep` in
`watchdog-lib.test.sh` allowlisting `temp_dir` and `#[cfg(test)]`.

### 6.2 Absent test levels

- **Migrations are only ever tested against an empty database.** `watchdog-test-db.sh` does
  `docker rm -f` then a `--tmpfs` run on every gate execution. A migration that is valid empty but
  fatal populated — `SET NOT NULL` without backfill, a unique index over existing duplicates, a long
  `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock on a live table — passes the gate at 100% and then executes irreversibly on
  production. This is the highest-consequence untested surface in the system, and it is invisible to
  per-file review because it is a property of the fixture.
- **Restore has never been rehearsed.** `watchdog-backup.sh` validates the dump with
  `pg_restore --file=/dev/null`, which proves the archive decompresses, not that it loads. Since
  migrations are expand-only and irreversible, restore-from-dump *is* the rollback plan for a bad
  migration.
- **Rollback restores code, not schema.** `apply_migrations_before_deploy` runs before
  `deploy_engine` and `deploy_backend`, so a rollback runs release N-1 against schema N. The
  expand-only policy is exactly the assumption that makes this safe, and no test runs the previous
  release's code against the new schema.
- **No contract test between `packages/engine-client` and `crates/server/src/admin.rs`.** The TypeScript
  side asserts against response fixtures it authors itself; nothing runs the real Rust handler and
  validates the result against the `packages/contracts` zod schemas.
- **No property-based testing** (`proptest`/`quickcheck`/`fast-check` appear in no lockfile), no
  failure injection, no fuzzing of the SSE parser, no load or concurrency test above the unit level.

### 6.3 The gate does not protect itself

`watchdog-infrastructure.sh` executes the candidate's own `install-watchdog.sh` — the tested
candidate installs itself as the next gate. The only defence is grep assertions in
`watchdog-lib.test.sh`, and they cover 2 of ~10 steps:

| Gate step | Pinned by `watchdog-lib.test.sh`? |
|---|---|
| `monitoring-config.test.sh` invoked | yes |
| `TEST_SALES_DATABASE_URL` exported | yes |
| `cargo test` runs at all | **no** |
| `CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL` exported | **no** |
| `pnpm … --if-present test` runs | **no** |
| `pnpm typecheck` runs | **no** |
| `bash -n` over `deploy/*.sh` | **no** |
| `git diff --check` | **no** |
| `watchdog-lib.test.sh` itself invoked | **no** |

Related: `--if-present` makes deletion invisible. `apps/sales-web` (~5k LOC, including the
irreversible on-chain USDT payout console) and `packages/contracts` (the money types) have no `test`
script and pass silently, and nothing asserts which packages must have one.

### 6.4 Operational

- **No local reproduction of the gate.** `CONTRIBUTING.md` prescribes a command that runs a strict
  subset of what the gate runs; the first real signal on the database-backed money surface arrives
  after a push to a production trigger.
- **Infrastructure flakiness is indistinguishable from test failure.** `watchdog-test-db.sh` carries
  a comment recording that port 55432 sat inside the kernel ephemeral range and quarantined a SHA
  twice on 2026-07-25. There is no retry-on-infra-failure path and no flake budget; recovery is a
  manual `watchdog-control.sh` intervention.
- **No cross-authority reconciliation exists at all.** None of the 19 exported `apitoken_*` metric
  families reconciles engine `balance_nano` against commerce's `payments + bonus − spend`. Given that
  `debitAccount`, `revokeSignupBonus`, `reconcileTopupTier` and `ledger_prune` are all untested, a
  silent divergence between the two money authorities is the most likely way real money goes missing,
  and it would be discovered by a customer.

### 6.5 A note on defence in depth

Many findings were downgraded by verifiers because a different, tested layer catches the failure —
almost always the PostgreSQL authority in `crates/registry/src/pg.rs`. That is the correct call each
time, but it has a corollary worth stating: `pg.rs` is load-bearing for most of the downgraded
severities in this document, and its coverage is effectively one 585-line monolithic test
(`stage2_fault_matrix`) gated on an environment variable. The compensating control is itself a
single point of failure.

---

## 7. Prioritized remediation plan

Ordered by risk reduced per unit of effort, not by severity of the individual finding.

1. **Protect the gate from itself (S, ~20 min).** Add ~10 `grep -Fq` assertions to
   `watchdog-lib.test.sh` pinning `cargo test --locked --workspace`, all three test DSN exports,
   `--if-present test`, `typecheck`, `bash -n`, `git diff --check`, its own invocation, and the
   required set of packages carrying a `test` script. The pattern already exists in that file.
   Everything below is worth less without this.
2. **Make the gate reproducible locally (M).** A `scripts/dev-db.sh` mirroring `watchdog-test-db.sh`,
   a `pnpm gate` script exporting all three DSNs, and one `expect(ranIntegrationSuites).toBe(true)`
   per package that fails when the DSN is unset unless `ALLOW_SKIP=1`. This turns 16 silently-skipped
   suites and `stage2_fault_matrix` into something a developer can see, and it is a prerequisite for
   anyone actually writing the tests requested below.
3. **Replay migrations against production-shaped data in the gate (M).** Restore a scrubbed
   structural dump, seeded with the constraint-violating shapes (NULLs in columns about to go
   NOT NULL, duplicates under a new unique index), into a fourth database; run the migrators against
   it; assert success and bounded lock time. Covers the only irreversible production write.
4. **One HTTP harness for `forward`, wired into `cargo test` (M–L).** The workspace's first
   `[dev-dependencies]` (`tower`, `http-body-util`, `wiremock`) plus a `crates/forward/tests/` target:
   401 on unauthorized `/v1`, control-key-must-not-forward, request/response header strip lists, one
   SSE body through `stream_back` + `TeeMeter` asserting the exact charged nano (this catches §4.2),
   the 401→rotate→response path, and a client-abort case. Establishing the convention makes
   `crates/server/tests/` for `/admin/*` a follow-on rather than a project.
5. **A cross-authority reconciliation invariant, as a test and a metric (S–M).** Extend
   `stage2_fault_matrix`'s closing aggregate sweep to assert `Σ charges + Σ holds + balance = Σ topups`
   per account, and add an `apitoken_balance_divergence_nano` gauge with a critical alert. One
   detector covering six separately-reported untested money paths — and the only proposed change that
   could catch a defect already in production.

Independent one-liners, worth doing immediately: fix the swallowed `#[test]` (§4.1) and close the
SSE accumulator filter (§4.2).

Deliberately **not** in the top five: the Codex `settle` gap (critical, but a smaller revenue surface
than the Claude path, and `runner.rs` is genuinely well tested), and adding a `test` script to
`apps/sales-web` (real, but a script with no tests buys nothing before item 2).

---
## 8. Findings by domain

141 findings that survived adversarial verification — 9 critical, 40 high, 55 medium, 37 low. `CONFIRMED` means a verifier searched the whole repository and found nothing covering the behaviour. `ADJUSTED` means the original claim was narrowed, or its severity corrected, because another layer partly covers it — the text below is the corrected claim. `UNVERIFIED` means verification did not reach it; treat those as unaudited leads.

3 entries are the same defect reached by two auditors from different files. Both are kept, because independent convergence is evidence, but each weaker entry links to the fuller one — triage the defect once, at the higher severity.

### 8.2 `rust-registry` — Rust registry crate (engine authority store)

`crates/registry` is the engine's money and fencing authority: PostgreSQL (`pg.rs`, 2518 loc) owns accounts/api_keys/reservations/settlement_outbox/ledger/usage_events/pool_state/capacity+leader leases, `lib.rs` (3080 loc) holds the legacy-but-still-live SQLite fallback and importer source, `authority.rs` (454 loc) dispatches between them. Every reserve/settle/cancel/reconcile in production goes through `pg.rs`, and its failure modes are irreversible: stranded holds, double charges, deleted unbilled ledger rows, or a stale engine slot admitting traffic it no longer owns. Coverage is starkly bimodal. The single test `pg::tests::stage2_fault_matrix` (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1933) is genuinely excellent where it reaches — real threads against real PostgreSQL, exactly-once charging, an overdraft boundary triple, epoch fencing, CAS, a crash-boundary drain, and a closing global aggregate-vs-rows invariant sweep — but it is one 585-line monolith covering roughly the happy path plus the reserve/settle race surface, and it `return`s silently as a PASS whenever `CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is unset (pg.rs:1934-1937), which is every environment except the prod watchdog (deploy/watchdog.sh:333). `crates/registry/tests/` is empty, `authority.rs` has zero tests, and about half of `pg.rs`'s public surface — the outbox permanent-failure path, `ledger_prune`/`usage_prune`/`maintenance_prune`, `classify_failure`, `renew_stream_leases`, the `add`/`add_file` ON CONFLICT health-reset branch, the capacity util/cooling admission gate, and all analytics reads — is never executed by any test. The well-tested SQLite twins in `lib.rs` (28 tests) create a false sense of safety: they cover functions whose *authoritative* PostgreSQL implementations are independently written SQL with no coverage at all.

**Working well here:**

- **Exactly-once charging pinned at the storage layer, not the return value** — `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2321-2342` — Settles twice expecting the same balance, then asserts a third settle with a *different* actual (251 vs 250) errors rather than silently re-charging, then drops to raw SQL for COUNT(*) FROM ledger WHERE kind='charge' AND request_id='req-1' == 1. Asserting the ledger row count directly means a regression that returned the right balance while writing a second charge row still fails. This would catch a real double-billing bug.
- **Overdraft floor tested as a boundary table, not a single point** — `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2383-2414` — Four cases in sequence on the same account: a hold that lands just inside the buffer (-999_999_000), one that lands exactly on the -$1 floor (-1_000_000_000, inclusive), one nano more rejected at the floor, then a fresh account rejecting hold > balance+$1 and accepting the in-range hold. This is exactly the on/at/over-boundary discipline the most dangerous money predicate in the codebase deserves, and the exact expected balances make an off-by-one in the `balance_nano + $3 >= $1` gate (pg.rs:315) impossible to miss.
- **Crash boundary exercised through a real dropped connection** — `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2354-2374` — Reserves, marks delivering, commits the outbox intent, then `drop(pg)` and reconnects on a fresh PgStore before draining. Draining twice asserts 1 then 0, and the account is checked to (650, 350, 0). This tests the actual reason the outbox exists — durable intent surviving process death — rather than simulating it with a flag.
- **Two real multi-threaded races against live PostgreSQL with post-race invariant checks** — `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2180-2227 and crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2252-2298` — Barrier-synchronised threads on independent connections: two 400-nano reservations against a 700-nano key cap must yield exactly one winner, and a reservation racing a policy tightening must yield exactly one winner (assert_ne! on the two booleans). Crucially both then assert the *resulting state*, not just the winner count — spent_nano + reserved_nano <= limit, and account.reserved_nano == key.reserved_nano. Testing serialization against the real engine instead of asserting on a mocked lock is rare and is the right call here.
- **Capacity oversell proven by contention, not by arithmetic** — `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2469-2503` — Eight concurrent contenders against max_inflight=3 must produce exactly 3 leases and pool_inflight == 3, then all three release successfully, then all three release again returning false, then inflight is back to 0. The double-release-is-false assertion is what stops a retry from silently under-counting inflight and overselling a subscription's quota.
- **Closing global invariant sweep that cross-checks the denormalized aggregate against source rows** — `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2510-2516` — After ~15 scenarios the test asserts billing_totals().reserved_nano == 0 AND, via raw SQL, SUM(hold_nano) over all non-terminal reservations == 0. Any accounting leak introduced anywhere earlier in the test — a hold that was never released, a reserved_nano that drifted from its reservations — surfaces here even if the scenario that caused it had no assertion of its own. This is the single highest-leverage assertion in the file.
- **Recovery preserves the money-safe asymmetry between undelivered and delivered work** — `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2435-2456` — Sets up two requests under a separate 'dead-engine' owner, forces both the instance lease and the reservation leases to 0, then asserts reconcile refunds exactly one (never delivered) and charges the full hold on exactly one (delivered, usage lost). This pins the deliberate over-charge policy at pg.rs:699-707 so a well-meaning 'fix' that refunds delivered inference cannot land silently.
- **Irreversible cutover fails closed on unattributable money** — `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1993-2000` — After a successful import, the test adds an anonymous aggregate hold to the SQLite source and asserts the second import errors. That is the correct property for a one-way migration: refuse rather than guess. The SQLite-side analogue at lib.rs:2293-2302 asserts the same fail-closed stance for reconcile.
- **Security regressions pinned as named tests on the SQLite path** — `crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2246-2273 and crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2220-2244` — legacy_keys_with_same_suffix_migrate_to_distinct_accounts constructs two keys sharing a 12-char suffix and asserts they migrate to distinct accounts with their own balances — a direct regression test for the AUDIT(C39) fix documented at lib.rs:262. open_fails_closed_when_legacy_topup_references_are_duplicated builds a genuinely corrupt on-disk ledger and asserts open() refuses rather than proceeding. Both encode a specific past incident class.

<a id="rust-registry--outbox-failed-strands-customer-funds"></a>
#### `outbox-failed-strands-customer-funds` — A permanently-failing settlement moves the outbox row to a terminal 'failed' state that nothing ever retries, stranding the customer's hold forever

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:659`

*Not covered today.* The drain_outbox permanent-failure branch (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:656-673) is genuinely unexecuted by any test, and no test ever writes settlement_outbox.state='failed', so migration 0004's relaxed CHECK (crates/registry/migrations_pg/0004_audit_hardening.sql:17-19) is never proven against a freshly migrated DB. But the claim overstates the blast radius: a 'failed' row is NOT invisible — deploy/collect-monitoring-metrics.sh:85 counts state <> 'done' and observability/prometheus/rules/application.yml:205-214 raises a *critical* EngineSettlementBacklog alert after 5m with runbook MONITORING.md:277-280 ('Protect money integrity first'). It is also not structurally unreachable: process_outbox_request (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:527-540) filters only on outbox_state=='done', not on 'pending', so any direct settle_request/cancel_request for that request_id would still apply it. The accurate claim is 'permanent settlement failures are operator-repair-only and that branch has zero test coverage', not 'the customer silently and permanently loses the hold'.

*Production risk.* reserve_request already debited balance_nano and moved the amount into reserved_nano (pg.rs:314). If settlement hits any Permanent error — the invariant bails at pg.rs:559/571, a payload-conflict bail at pg.rs:482, or a CHECK violation such as accounts_reserved_nano_check (SQLSTATE 23514) — drain_outbox marks the row 'failed' with next_attempt_ts=0. drain_outbox only selects state='pending' (pg.rs:648), reconcile_expired only re-enqueues 'reserved'/'delivering' and enqueue_outbox is ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING (pg.rs:444), and maintenance_prune only reaps 'done'. The money is neither refunded nor converted to spend: the customer permanently loses the held amount and their usable balance is silently reduced, with no automated repair path — only an eprintln (pg.rs:671) and the apitoken_engine_settlement_pending gauge (deploy/collect-monitoring-metrics.sh:85).

*Suggested test.* Integration test in the same #[cfg(test)] module against CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL, ideally as its own #[test] fn outbox_permanent_failure_is_repairable. Reserve a request, enqueue settlement, then force a Permanent error deterministically (e.g. UPDATE api_keys SET reserved_nano=0 for a key with a non-null spend_limit_nano so the pg.rs:566 branch bails while the key still exists). Assert: drain_outbox returns 0 and the row is state='failed' with attempts=1 (proving the CHECK accepts 'failed'); then assert the invariant that must hold afterwards — either a subsequent reconcile_expired/drain recovers the row, or, if 'failed' is intentionally terminal, assert that accounts.reserved_nano is still non-zero and add an explicit operator-repair entry point and test it. Also add a pure unit test that classify_failure maps a bare anyhow::anyhow! message to Permanent and a synthetic 40001/40P01 postgres::Error to Transient.

<a id="rust-registry--ledger-prune-acked-boundary"></a>
#### `ledger-prune-acked-boundary` — Neither ledger_prune implementation is tested at the watermark boundary, so nothing stops the automatic pruner from deleting charge rows commerce has not yet billed

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1377`

*Not covered today.* pg.ledger_prune has zero tests. The SQLite twin has one test (crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2853-2867) that only asserts prune returns 0 when no ledger_consumer_checkpoints row exists — which passes for any query whose JOIN yields nothing, including a query whose id comparison is inverted. Neither implementation has a test that inserts a 'pricing' checkpoint and asserts that charge rows with id <= last_ledger_id are deleted while rows with id > last_ledger_id survive. ledger_ack (pg.rs:1360) — the function that advances the watermark, including its GREATEST monotonicity guard against a regressing consumer — has no PostgreSQL test either.

*Production risk.* crates/server/src/poller.rs:100 calls ledger_prune on a timer in up to 20 batches of 5000 per pass, unattended, against the live authority. B2C pricing derives only from consumed engine charge-ledger rows (CLAUDE.md, commercial workspace section), read through ledger_after (crates/server/src/admin.rs:297). If the checkpoint JOIN or the `l.id <= c.last_ledger_id` predicate regresses — a LEFT JOIN, a dropped consumer filter, an inverted comparison — the engine deletes charge rows before commerce has billed them. The deletion is irreversible and silent: balances stay correct, so the only symptom is revenue that is never invoiced. A regressing ledger_ack watermark would do the same.

*Suggested test.* Unit tests on both paths. PostgreSQL: in the stage2 module, settle three requests, capture their ledger ids via ledger_after, ledger_ack("pricing", acct, second_id), backdate all ts below the cutoff, call ledger_prune, and assert exactly the first two charge rows are gone, the third survives, and every 'topup'/'adjust' row survives regardless of ts. Add a second case asserting a checkpoint for a *different* consumer name prunes nothing, and that ledger_ack with a lower id does not lower the watermark. Mirror the same three assertions on the SQLite path by extending crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2853.

*Verified absent by.* Searched repo-wide: rg -n "ledger_consumer_checkpoints" --glob '!target' returns exactly 7 hits — crates/registry/src/lib.rs:158,1182,1194, crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1370,1372,1380, crates/registry/migrations_pg/0004_audit_hardening.sql:45. Not one is inside a test. rg -n "ledger_prune|ledger_ack" confirms the only test touching either is crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2852-2867 (ledger_prune_is_disabled_without_consumer_watermarks), which inserts no checkpoint row and asserts prune==0 — exactly the vacuous-JOIN case the auditor describes; it does add an unclaimed extra assertion (ledger_after returns 2 rows, lib.rs:2861-2867) but still never exercises the id<=watermark predicate. crates/registry/src/pg.rs has zero ledger_prune/ledger_ack calls in its #[cfg(test)] mod (verified by awk 'NR>=1925' + rg). Commerce-side coverage does not transfer: packages/engine-client/src/index.test.ts:205 only asserts the HTTP URL '/admin/account/acct_test/ledger/ack'. Risk is live and unattended: crates/server/src/main.rs:33 hardcodes LEDGER_RETENTION_DAYS=30 and main.rs:1080-1085 always spawns poller::ledger_prune_loop when billing is on; crates/server/src/poller.rs:99-112 loops 20x5000 per pass. Severity adjusted critical->high only because the ts<cutoff term means a regression additionally requires charge rows older than 30 days to be unacked, which has its own upstream signal.

<a id="rust-registry--capacity-admission-gate-untested"></a>
#### `capacity-admission-gate-untested` — Every rejection predicate and the crash-recovery reclaim in acquire_capacity are unexecuted

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:753`

*Not covered today.* All four rejection predicates and the reclaim are indeed unexecuted (the only test, crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2469-2495, runs against a pool_state row at schema defaults cooling_until/util5/util7/reset5/reset7 = 0 per migrations_pg/0001_engine_authority.sql:139-155, so only `inflight >= max_inflight` can reject). But the 'second independent implementation whose divergence risks an Anthropic ban' framing overstates it: the routing decision that actually protects a paid account is the in-memory pool gate, which is heavily covered — crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1763 window_rollover_resets_util, :1786 all_cooling_detected_for_fast_reject, :2247 route_rebinds_on_deep_cooling, :2215 route_placement_respects_concurrency_envelope, :2494 retry_selection_respects_concurrency_envelope — and crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1162-1192 treats the PG lease as a post-hoc confirmation of an already-chosen candidate. The genuinely unique, genuinely uncovered piece is the expired-lease reclaim at pg.rs:753-763: it is the ONLY code anywhere that returns a leaked pool_state.inflight to reality (maintenance_prune at pg.rs:1487-1493 only deletes leases already in 'released'/'expired'), and if it breaks, proxy.rs:1192 silently `continue`s past that subscription forever.

*Production risk.* These SQL predicates are a second, independent implementation of admission logic that also lives in-memory in crates/pool. Divergence in either direction is expensive: too permissive (a rollover that fails to reset, or a cooling window ignored) keeps pushing traffic at a rate-limited or cooling subscription and risks an Anthropic ban on a real paid account; too strict silently blackholes capacity. The unreclaimed-inflight case is worse: if an engine slot dies mid-request during a blue-green cutover, only the pg.rs:753 path returns that subscription's inflight counter to reality. If it is broken, inflight stays pinned at max_inflight forever and that subscription is permanently unroutable — capacity lost with no error, only 429s to customers.

*Suggested test.* Table-style integration cases against the real database. (1) UPDATE pool_state SET cooling_until=now+60 and assert acquire_capacity returns None. (2) SET util5=0.99, reset5=0 and assert None at util_cap 0.95; then SET reset5=now-1 and assert Some (rollover zeroes the window). (3) Same for util7/reset7. (4) Reclaim: acquire a lease, UPDATE capacity_leases SET lease_until=0 for it, then acquire a *different* lease_id for the same email and assert it succeeds, the stale lease row is state='expired', and pool_inflight is 1 rather than 2.

<a id="rust-registry--pg-add-conflict-health-reset"></a>
#### `pg-add-conflict-health-reset` — The PostgreSQL auth-health auto-revive branch never executes in any test, while its SQLite twin is fully covered

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:894`

*Not covered today.* pg.add's ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE health-reset arm (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:894-901) is genuinely never evaluated — the only call is on an empty subs table at crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2009 — and that IS a live production path, since `claude-api sub add-file` re-add lands there. But the add_file half of the claim describes dead code, not a production risk: crates/server/src/main.rs:715-726 shows the AddFile CLI reads the file contents and calls db.add(), and rg -n "add_file" repo-wide finds NO caller of Authority::add_file (crates/registry/src/authority.rs:83) or pg.add_file (pg.rs:912) anywhere. The 'primary authbot path' framing is also wrong here: tools/authbot does not exist in this repository (ls tools/ -> codex-app-server, refresh-fingerprint.sh).

*Production risk.* Re-adding a subscription is the primary authbot path: `claude-api sub add-file <email> --token-file ...` runs on every token re-issue. If the PostgreSQL condition regresses toward always-reset, a subscription Anthropic has banned is silently auto-revived to 'healthy' and the pool routes paying-customer traffic straight back into it, producing a 401/403 storm and rotation churn. If it regresses toward never-reset, a genuinely re-authenticated subscription stays 'dead' and is permanently excluded from the pool — paid capacity lost with no operator signal. Neither failure is visible until traffic hits it.

*Suggested test.* Add a dedicated #[test] (or a clearly delimited section of the stage2 test) that mirrors lib.rs:2442 against PostgreSQL: pg.add(email, "token-a"), save_sub_health with auth_state='dead', streak=3, fp='old'; pg.add(email, "token-a", changed proxy) and assert load_sub_health still reports 'dead' with streak 3; pg.add(email, "token-b") and assert ('healthy', 0) with an empty auth_token_fp and empty dead_reason; then pg.add_file(email, "/tmp/token") and assert (token, token_file) == (NULL, '/tmp/token') and health reset again; finally pg.add_file with the same path and assert the verdict is preserved.

<a id="rust-registry--engine-migration-not-gated"></a>
#### `engine-migration-not-gated` — Engine migrations are neither replay-tested nor protected against edits, and nothing asserts a new migration file is actually wired in

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:196`

*Not covered today.* The 'never re-applied, never applied over populated tables' half is factually wrong: deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh applies migrate() four times, three of them over a populated schema — once via `db migrate-postgres` (script line 68 -> crates/server/src/main.rs:312) before the import, then again on each engine boot at script lines 114-117 -> crates/server/src/main.rs:881, with CLAUDE_API_DATABASE_URL inherited from the provisioned env file (script lines 44-48, deploy/engine-postgres-provision.sh:50-57). schema_version() is likewise reached, via `db verify-postgres` (script line 69 -> main.rs:325-331). What survives is narrower but still real: (a) that E2E is manual, root+Docker-only (DEPLOYMENT.md:157-161, docs/STAGE2_POSTGRES_AUTHORITY.md:126) and is NOT in the automated gate, which runs only cargo test + two shell test files (deploy/watchdog.sh:332-344); (b) verify-postgres only *prints* the version, it never asserts schema==5, and the E2E never inspects that output; (c) there is genuinely no test tying migrations_pg/*.sql file count to the MIGRATION_0001..0005 consts; (d) crates/registry/migrations_pg is genuinely outside the append-only manifest.

*Production risk.* crates/server/src/main.rs:881 runs migrate() on every engine boot, so in blue-green each release applies the full set at least twice (new slot, then again on any restart or rollback to the old binary). A future migration containing one non-idempotent statement — a plain ADD CONSTRAINT without the DO-block guard used in 0002/0003, or a CREATE INDEX without IF NOT EXISTS — passes the gate green on the empty test database and then hard-fails the second engine boot, which is a failed cutover or a failed rollback on a live money service. Separately, adding 0006_*.sql without touching pg.rs leaves the file inert: the repo shows a schema the production database does not have, and any code depending on it fails at runtime. And because migrations_pg is outside the append-only manifest, editing an already-applied engine migration is undetected.

*Suggested test.* Three cheap tests. (1) In pg.rs tests: call pg.migrate() a second time immediately after the first and assert Ok, then assert schema_version() == 5 — this pins both replay-idempotency and the fact that every migration registers its version. (2) A pure #[test] with no database that reads std::fs::read_dir("migrations_pg") and asserts the file count equals the number of MIGRATION_ consts and that the highest filename prefix equals the expected schema version, so a new unwired file fails the build. (3) Extend deploy/watchdog-lib.sh's manifest (and its assertions in deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh) to hash crates/registry/migrations_pg/*.sql under the same append-only rule as packages/db/migrations.

<a id="rust-registry--authority-silently-falls-back-to-sqlite"></a>
#### `authority-silently-falls-back-to-sqlite` — AuthorityConfig has zero tests, including the blank-URL fallback that boots a production engine slot on a non-authoritative local SQLite file

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/registry/src/authority.rs:18`

*Not covered today.* The zero-coverage part is fully confirmed (crates/registry/tests/ is empty, authority.rs has no #[cfg(test)], Cargo.toml declares no [dev-dependencies], and neither owner-required guard nor AuthorityConfig::new is asserted anywhere). Two corrections reduce the risk: (1) the cited config.rs:36-41 is the generic `ev()` helper — the actual read is crates/server/src/config.rs:356 — so the empty/whitespace->None mapping is shared with ~40 other settings, not a bespoke widening; (2) the fail-open is not unguarded end-to-end: deploy/engine-bluegreen.sh:151 aborts the cutover unless the PostgreSQL env file is non-empty, and crates/server/src/main.rs:860-862 hard-bails if the authority is PostgreSQL while billing is off. A *truncated to zero bytes* env file therefore fails the deploy; only a non-empty file carrying an empty/whitespace CLAUDE_API_DATABASE_URL value reaches the SQLite fallback. That residual hole is real and untested.

*Production risk.* A truncated or whitespace-only CLAUDE_API_DATABASE_URL in /etc/apitoken engine env does not fail the boot — the slot starts and serves traffic against a local SQLite file whose balances are stale or absent. In blue-green that means two engine slots with two different money authorities admitting the same customers concurrently: overdraft, double-spend, and a ledger the commerce side will never reconcile. It is a fail-open on the single most important configuration value in the system, and nothing in the test suite would notice a refactor that widened it further.

*Suggested test.* Pure unit tests in a new #[cfg(test)] mod in authority.rs, no database needed: assert AuthorityConfig::new(path, Some("postgresql://x")).is_postgres(), and assert new(path, Some(" ")) and new(path, None) are Sqlite with label()==path. Then assert Authority::Sqlite(open(":memory:")).save_pool_state(None, &rows) succeeds while Authority::Sqlite(...).postgres() errors and the Postgres arm of save_sub_health/save_pool_state with owner: None returns the 'requires owner epoch' error. Separately, in crates/server, assert that a serve config with billing enabled and no database_url is rejected rather than silently degraded.

<a id="rust-registry--renew-stream-leases-untested"></a>
#### `renew-stream-leases-untested` — The streaming lease heartbeat has no test in either direction

**MEDIUM** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:377`

*Not covered today.* pg.renew_stream_leases is never called by any test in the repo (only by crates/forward/src/meter.rs:114 and crates/forward/src/billing.rs:701 in production). Untested: that renewing a 'reserved'/'delivering'/'settlement_pending' reservation returns true and actually extends lease_until; that a wrong owner_epoch returns false rather than erroring; that a settled reservation returns false; that passing only one of the two Option ids does not spuriously fail the other; and that the assert_owner fencing at pg.rs:387 fires for a stale owner.

*Production risk.* A long stream renews its reservation and capacity lease every STREAM_LEASE_RENEW_SECS. If renew returns a spurious false, the heartbeat task exits (meter.rs:122-124) and the reservation lease expires mid-stream, so reconcile_expired charges the customer the *full hold* for a request whose actual usage would have been lower (pg.rs:699-707) — a systematic overcharge on exactly the long streaming requests where hold and actual diverge most. If renew spuriously succeeds against a fenced epoch, a stale slot keeps a dead subscription's inflight counter alive and blocks capacity.

*Suggested test.* Integration cases beside the existing lifecycle test: reserve + acquire_capacity, then assert renew_stream_leases(Some(req), Some(lease), 120) is Ok(true) and that reservations.lease_until and capacity_leases.lease_until both advanced by reading them back; assert renew with only the request id and with only the lease id each return true; settle the request and assert renew now returns Ok(false); claim a new epoch for the same instance and assert renew with the old Owner is Err.

*Verified absent by.* rg -n "renew_stream_leases|sqlite_renew_reservation_lease|STREAM_LEASE_RENEW" --glob '!target' . returns exactly 6 hits and not one is a test: definitions at crates/registry/src/pg.rs:377 and crates/registry/src/lib.rs:1343, wrappers at crates/forward/src/billing.rs:701,1087, prod callers at crates/forward/src/billing.rs:507 and crates/forward/src/meter.rs:110-124. crates/forward/src/meter.rs has no #[test]/#[cfg(test)] at all (rg over the file returns nothing). The pg #[cfg(test)] mod never calls it (awk 'NR>=1925' crates/registry/src/pg.rs | rg 'renew' -> no hits). assert_owner fencing at pg.rs:387 and the three-state WHERE at pg.rs:391-395 are therefore unexecuted, as is the SQLite twin. Two mitigating facts worth noting but not sufficient to refute: the heartbeat-exit consequence path IS covered on the PG side — crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2404-2422 drives a 'delivering' reservation past lease expiry and asserts recovered.charged_after_delivery==1 — so the full-hold charge mechanism is tested, just not the heartbeat that prevents it; and meter.rs:122-124 does log before returning. Severity medium is appropriate as stated.

<a id="rust-registry--pg-read-and-retention-surface-untested"></a>
#### `pg-read-and-retention-surface-untested` — The whole PostgreSQL read/aggregation surface and maintenance_prune have no coverage, only SQLite twins

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1388`

*Not covered today.* The inventory is accurate — none of the 14 listed PostgreSQL methods is called by any test (verified by awk over the pg #[cfg(test)] mod). But two of the three risk framings misread the code. (1) key_account is NOT 'the only thing that stops a disabled account's keys from continuing to spend': pg.reserve_request independently gates `WHERE id=$2 AND status='active'` on accounts (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:314) and `status='active'` plus expiry/limit on api_keys (pg.rs:323-327), and that gate IS covered — the disabled-key case at pg.rs:2113-2123 and the expired-key case at pg.rs:2088-2100 both assert reserve_request returns None. A regressed key_account.active would produce a wrong error code, not a spend. (2) subs_admin is not a PostgreSQL-specific masking implementation: pg.rs:1034 calls the SAME shared registry::mask_proxy imported at pg.rs:7 that SQLite uses at lib.rs:584, so a PG-only regression cannot leak credentials; the real (shared) gap is that mask_proxy at lib.rs:471 has no test in either backend. (3) The maintenance_prune concern stands as written and is the strongest part of the claim.

*Production risk.* key_account is the authorisation lookup for every customer request (crates/forward/src/billing.rs:852): its `(k.status='active' AND a.status='active')` conjunction is the only thing that stops a disabled account's keys from continuing to spend, and the PostgreSQL version of that expression has never been executed by a test. subs_admin is a secret-exposure boundary — it masks the proxy string via mask_proxy (pg.rs:1034) and returns only a has_token boolean; a regression there leaks proxy credentials into the admin panel. maintenance_prune deletes reservations and capacity_leases and depends on a subtle intra-transaction ordering against the ON DELETE RESTRICT FK at 0001_engine_authority.sql:79; it runs unattended from crates/server/src/poller.rs:102.

*Suggested test.* One compact integration test per cluster, reusing the stage2 fixture. Auth: issue a key, assert key_account(...).active is true, account_set_status(acct,'disabled'), assert active is false, re-enable and assert key_set_status(key,'disabled') also yields false. Secrets: pg.add with a proxy containing a password, assert subs_admin()[0].proxy_host is masked and contains neither the password nor the token, and list_subs()[0].has_token is true. Retention: settle a request, backdate reservations.settled_ts and settlement_outbox.committed_ts, call maintenance_prune and assert the outbox row and reservation are both removed and the counts in MaintenanceReport are exact, while the ledger charge row and the engine_instances row still holding a non-terminal reservation both survive.

<a id="rust-registry--import-drops-pricing-audit-columns"></a>
#### `import-drops-pricing-audit-columns` — import_sqlite silently drops ten usage_events columns during the irreversible cutover, and the import assertions are too shallow to notice

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1799`

*Not covered today.* The technical finding is correct and I reproduced it: the usage_events SELECT at crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1799-1802 and INSERT at pg.rs:1839-1843 both carry 14 columns and omit speed, inference_geo, input_nano, output_nano, cache_read_nano, cache_write_5m_nano, cache_write_1h_nano, web_search_nano, priced_ts and provider, all of which exist on both sides. The test blindness is also confirmed. What is wrong is the production risk: this is now a closed, one-way path, not a pending hazard. DEPLOYMENT.md:143 states 'The one-time SQLite-to-PostgreSQL cutover is complete. Do not rerun it for a normal release', docs/STAGE2_POSTGRES_AUTHORITY.md:3 confirms Stage 2 is deployed, and import_sqlite additionally refuses to run whenever any reservation or capacity lease is active (pg.rs:1904-1906), so it cannot fire against a live engine. Any audit loss it could cause has already either happened or not; no future release re-enters this path. The accurate claim is 'the completed cutover's importer silently drops ten audit columns and the regression tests would not catch it if the path were ever reused'.

*Production risk.* The cutover is one-way and re-running it re-truncates the target (pg.rs:1606-1616). Every historical request loses its immutable pricing breakdown and its provider attribution, defaulting to 'anthropic' — which is precisely what migration 0005 exists to prevent ('without an explicit column, which provider earned this can only be guessed'). Post-cutover, spend_by_provider (pg.rs:1439) misattributes all Codex traffic to Anthropic and usage_by_model's per-bucket nano sums read zero for all pre-cutover history, so margin analysis over the migration boundary is silently wrong. Charge and real_nano totals survive, so money is not lost, but the audit trail the 0004 migration comment calls a requirement is.

*Suggested test.* Extend the import fixture at crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1952 to write at least one usage_events row with every column populated (call account_settle with a fully-populated UsageEventInput, including provider="codex" and non-zero input_nano/priced_ts) and one extra ledger row, then assert report.usage_rows/ledger_rows/pool_rows exactly, and read the imported usage_events row back from PostgreSQL asserting every column round-tripped. Also assert the imported pool_state row's cooling_until == 123 survives (the fixture already writes it at pg.rs:1969-1978 but never checks it).

<a id="rust-registry--connect-tls-enforcement-untested"></a>
#### `connect-tls-enforcement-untested` — The remote-PostgreSQL TLS requirement is a security control with no test, despite needing no database

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:177`

*Not covered today.* The coverage statement is accurate — crates/registry/src/pg.rs:167-179 is reachable without a socket and no test ever calls PgStore::connect with a non-loopback DSN (all six call sites in the test mod pass the loopback CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL). But the production risk is theoretical, not live: every DSN this system generates is loopback by construction (deploy/engine-postgres-provision.sh:52 writes 127.0.0.1:5433, config.env.example:5 documents 127.0.0.1:5433), so there is no configuration in which the remote branch currently fires, and the transport choice it protects is separately pinned by deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:492-497. The accurate claim is 'a cheap, socket-free unit test of a defense-in-depth predicate is missing', and the same applies to the session-hardening batch at pg.rs:188-191.

*Production risk.* If this predicate regresses, the engine will happily open a plaintext TCP connection to a remote PostgreSQL carrying OAuth subscription tokens, customer api_keys and the full money ledger. The session-hardening batch that follows (statement_timeout, lock_timeout, idle_in_transaction_session_timeout, synchronous_commit=on at pg.rs:188-191) is equally load-bearing for money durability and equally unasserted — a change to synchronous_commit would silently make committed charges losable on crash.

*Suggested test.* Pure #[test] with no database: assert connect("postgresql://user:p@db.example.com/engine") is Err with a message containing 'sslmode=require'; assert connect("postgresql://...@db.example.com/engine?sslmode=require") fails only with a connection error, not the sslmode error; assert loopback DSNs (127.0.0.1, ::1, localhost, unix socket) never produce the sslmode error. Separately, in the stage2 test, query `SHOW synchronous_commit`, `SHOW statement_timeout` and `SHOW lock_timeout` on the live session and assert on/15s/5s so the durability settings cannot be weakened silently.

### 8.3 `rust-pool` — Rust pool crate (subscription pool + rotation)

crates/pool is the in-memory scheduler that decides which subscription serves each request: cache-first affinity (pin/spill/rebind), capacity-weighted placement, the Reserve headroom envelope, the concurrency (in-flight) envelope, 429 cooling, the durable Healthy→Suspect→Dead auth-ban detector, and the ΔUSD/Δutil window-capacity calibration that everything else is scaled by. It is pure logic (no net/DB/env), so it is genuinely testable, and with 49 inline tests it is the best-covered Rust module in the repo — the auth state machine (injected clock), the persistence/CAS rebase paths, and the incident-derived calibration-quarantine test are real, regression-catching work. But the suite has three systemic holes. (1) The calibration tests are numerically degenerate: every one of them feeds an observation exactly equal to the plan prior, so the EMA blend, the jump clamp and the baseline choice are all unobservable — the very fix their comments describe could be reverted with the suite green. (2) Coverage is concentrated on `Pool::route(u64)`, which has zero production callers anywhere in the repo, while the production entry point `route_affinity` has two dedicated tests and leaves its money-relevant policies (continuation hard-cap vs new-placement Reserve, dead home mid-conversation, short-burst cooling spill) unpinned. (3) `now()` is a free function over SystemTime and no test sleeps, so every recovery transition — cooling expiring, an affinity binding going stale, the debounce window closing — is untested; the pool could stay cooled forever and all 49 tests would still pass. There is also no multithreaded test at all, despite this being the shared scheduler behind every request, and `mark_healthy` — the function on the success path of every request, which can erase a ban verdict in memory — has zero tests anywhere in the repository.

**Working well here:**

- **Durable auth-ban detector (Healthy→Suspect→Dead)** — `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2618 rapid_fails_within_window_do_not_kill and crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2635 corroborated_fails_over_window_go_dead` — Both sides of the corroboration rule are pinned: three rapid 401s stay Suspect, two 401s spread over DEAD_MIN_SECS go Dead with the exact dead_since_ts asserted. Because apply_probe takes `now` as a parameter, this is deterministic and instant. Loosening the streak or dropping the time window — which would start quarantining healthy subscriptions on transient blips, i.e. self-inflicted capacity loss — fails immediately.
- **Incident-derived calibration quarantine end-to-end** — `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1968 import_repairs_implausible_capacity_and_restores_placement` — Written from a real production shape ($0.0315 cap5h). It asserts the repair count (1), the fallen-back prior (300.0), the resulting routing decision (peek_affinity_home returns the repaired subscription), and then that a stale CAS reload carrying the same poisoned value cannot get back into memory or back out through export_state. One test spanning import_state, merge_persisted_state, capacity and place_best — a genuine invariant, not a getter.
- **Ban durability across roster churn and restart** — `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2806 cooling_survives_roster_flap and crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2555 import_retains_inactive_future_cooling` — These encode the failure the module exists to prevent: a subscription that drops out of the roster (or is dormant at boot) must not have its multi-day 429 ban silently forgotten, or live traffic returns to a rate-limited account. The second test goes further and asserts the dormant row survives the safety-flush export and that reactivation still keeps it out of rotation.
- **Persistence lost-update protection** — `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2363 persistence_ack_preserves_spend_recorded_during_write` — Models the exact race — spend recorded after export_state but before the database write is acknowledged — and asserts exact numbers (total 3.0, remaining delta 2.0). This is the class of bug that silently drifts every subscription's spend accounting across blue-green slots, and the test would catch an ack that cleared the whole counter.
- **Affinity state sequence with dispositions asserted** — `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2165 shared_affinity_waits_once_then_spills_without_rebind` — Drives a three-step sequence (Wait{50} → Spilled to another sub → Pinned back home once slots free) and asserts the AffinityDisposition each time, not just the email. That distinction is what separates 'spilled one request, kept the warm cache' from 'rebound and lost the prompt cache', which is the crate's core economic behaviour.
- **Cross-crate wiring of the real production path** — `crates/forward/src/affinity.rs:1436 growing_classic_api_history_stays_on_its_subscription` — Uses a real Pool with the real AffinityStore (infer → claim → route_affinity → resolve → route_affinity) and asserts the continuation comes back Pinned to the same subscription. This is the only test in the repo that exercises the actual production pairing rather than pool internals in isolation.
- **Write-amplification contract of the health poller** — `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2768 record_probe_persists_only_on_change` — Pins that a repeated healthy probe returns None so the poller does not issue a fenced PostgreSQL write per subscription per cycle. An operational invariant that is easy to regress and invisible until the authority database is hot.

<a id="rust-pool--calib-ema-degenerate"></a>
#### `calib-ema-degenerate` — Every calibration test uses an observation equal to the prior, so the EMA blend and jump clamp are unobservable

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:230`

*Not covered today.* The degeneracy is real — verified pool() seeds prior5h=50.0 (crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1736-1743), so obs==prior in all three calibrating tests — but the claim overstates the blast radius. The blend at lib.rs:230 is the SECOND line of defence; the first (plausible_cap, lib.rs:148-155, applied at lib.rs:225 before the blend) rejects any observation outside [0.25x, 4x] of the plan prior and IS pinned by calibration_rejects_implausible_or_concurrent_bootstrap_samples (lib.rs:1881-1932, which replays the exact $0.0315-vs-$300 production incident), plus import_repairs_implausible_capacity_and_restores_placement (lib.rs:1968-2037) quarantines a poisoned persisted cap on restart and on CAS reload. A regression to `cap = obs` therefore cannot inflate a window 'several-fold' unbounded — it is capped at 4x prior, and because EMA converges to obs over repeated samples the blend only changes the transient, not the fixpoint. What is genuinely unpinned: the CALIB_ALPHA weighting and the CALIB_MAX_JUMP clamp at lib.rs:227 (which is a no-op whenever baseline==prior, i.e. exactly the case all three tests hit).

*Production risk.* Window capacity scales everything downstream: live_util (lib.rs:519), placement_free (lib.rs:658), the Reserve gate and the /capacity numbers. Reverting to `cap = obs` (the shape of the incident the guard was written for) or breaking the clamp lets one noisy quantised header inflate a subscription's estimated window several-fold; the scheduler then reads its utilization as low and floods it until Anthropic rate-limits or bans the account. Lost subscriptions are lost resale capacity and real money, and the suite stays green.

*Suggested test.* Unit, in the existing tests mod of crates/pool/src/lib.rs. Call calib_window directly with obs deliberately != prior — e.g. seeded anchor 0.20/spent 0, then util 0.30 with spent_total 60.0 and prior_cap 300.0 (obs = 600, plausible, > prior): assert cap == 300*0.7 + 600*0.3 = 390.0 exactly, not 600. Add a second case with obs beyond CALIB_MAX_JUMP (prior 300, du chosen so obs = 1500) and assert the clamp pins the pre-blend value at 1200, giving cap == 570.0. Add a third case exercising CALIB_MIN_SHARE alone: a numerically plausible obs (within 0.25x-4x of prior) whose du is below `d * baseline * CALIB_MIN_SHARE`, and assert cap stays 0 while the anchor still advances.

<a id="rust-pool--mark-healthy-untested"></a>
#### `mark-healthy-untested` — mark_healthy has zero tests anywhere, yet it runs on every successful request and can erase a ban verdict

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1163`

*Not covered today.* Correct that Pool::mark_healthy has no test (only the definition at crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1163 and a doc mention at :1295 exist repo-wide), but both halves of the stated risk are mitigated by tested code elsewhere. (a) The concurrency envelope is enforced AUTHORITATIVELY in PostgreSQL on every request — crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1163-1191 acquires a lease and crates/registry/src/pg.rs:783-793 rejects when `inflight >= max_inflight` inside a FOR UPDATE transaction — and that gate has a real 8-thread barrier test asserting exactly 3 of 8 contenders win and pool_state.inflight returns to 0 (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2470-2503), which runs in the CI gate (deploy/watchdog.sh:333-334 exports CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL). So an in-memory double-release cannot produce 'unbounded concurrent streams onto single accounts'. (b) reset_auth_healthy's semantics (the thing mark_healthy delegates to) are pinned via apply_probe at crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2667-2678 and via revive at :2716-2728. The genuine, narrow gap is mark_healthy's own wiring: expired-cooling clear (:1166-1168), last_used, and the 'in-flight is not touched' contract.

*Production risk.* Two distinct regressions ship silently. (a) If mark_healthy ever decrements in-flight, every successful request double-releases its slot alongside InflightGuard/end_stream, in-flight underflows to the clamp at 0, the MAX_INFLIGHT envelope stops constraining anything, and the fleet piles unbounded concurrent streams onto single accounts — precisely the automation fingerprint and 429/ban risk the envelope exists to prevent. (b) If the reset stops (or over-reaches), a corroborated Dead subscription either never returns to service on live traffic, or a banned token keeps being un-deaded in memory and served to clients as guaranteed 401s.

*Suggested test.* Unit in crates/pool/src/lib.rs tests mod: pick a sub (in-flight 1), call mark_healthy, assert snapshot in-flight is still 1 and last_used advanced; then kill(email), assert is_auth_dead, call mark_healthy and assert the in-memory verdict is cleared while a subsequent record_probe(401) restarts at Suspect with streak 1. Add a case where cooling_until is in the future and assert mark_healthy does NOT clear it.

<a id="rust-pool--affinity-continuation-cap-policy"></a>
#### `affinity-continuation-cap-policy` — The continuation hard-cap vs new-placement Reserve split in route_affinity is untested

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:954`

*Not covered today.* Coverage claim verified exactly: every route_affinity call in the repo's tests runs at utilization 0.05/0.30 or 0 — crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2086,:2097,:2107 (fixture at :2080-2081), :2170,:2179,:2182,:2194 (fixture at :2167-2168), and crates/forward/src/affinity.rs:1455,:1473 (fixture at :1440-1441) — against caps of 0.95 (Reserve::new(0.05,0.05,0.0)), so the newly_claimed-vs-continuation cap split at lib.rs:954-958 is never observable. Worth noting the same split is duplicated and equally untested one layer up (crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1166-1170 sends util_cap=1.0 to the PostgreSQL authority for Pinned dispositions). Severity lowered from high to medium: a regression only affects lineages whose home is already above the ~0.90-0.95 reserve line (the tail of the fleet) and its worst outcome is prompt-cache loss / a rebind to a healthy sub — no outage, no 401, and the reciprocal failure ('fresh sessions placed with no headroom') is separately blocked by placement_eligible (lib.rs:673-690) and by the PostgreSQL util_cap gate (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:786-790).

*Production risk.* If the soft Reserve is ever applied to continuations, every long conversation is rebound the moment its home crosses ~90% — mass prompt-cache invalidation. Cached prefix reads cost roughly a tenth of fresh input against the subscription window, so cache loss multiplies window burn per request, shrinking sellable capacity and pushing subscriptions into 429/ban territory. If the hard cap leaks into new placements instead, fresh sessions get placed onto subscriptions with no headroom left and 429 immediately.

*Suggested test.* Unit in crates/pool/src/lib.rs: pool with subs a,b and Reserve::new(0.10, 0.03, 0.0). set_util(a, 0.93, 0.10). Call route_affinity("opaque-a", 0, false, identify) and assert Selected{ sub: a, disposition: Pinned } (continuation ignores the reserve). Then call route_affinity("opaque-a", 0, true, identify) and assert disposition == Rebound onto b. Add a boundary case at util 1.0 asserting the continuation is also rebound.

<a id="rust-pool--affinity-dead-home-midsession"></a>
#### `affinity-dead-home-midsession` — A subscription banned mid-conversation is never tested on the production affinity path

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:961`

*Not covered today.* The coverage gap is real — no test kills or import_health-deads a subscription and then calls route_affinity for a lineage pinned to it; the dead-exclusion tests at crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2697-2714 and :2716-2728 go through pick/route, and the only route_affinity unavailability case is cooling (:2106). But the stated consequence is wrong: the pinned-to-dead-token request does NOT return a hard 401 to the customer. crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1327-1351 treats the first 401/403 as possibly request-caused, rotates to a DIFFERENT subscription (auth_tries < 2) and fires request_probe; the real upstream error is only returned when it repeats on the second subscription or no alternative exists. So the actual cost of an is_dead regression on the affinity path is one wasted upstream round-trip plus latency and probe churn per request, not a customer-visible outage.

*Production risk.* Claude bans pooled subscriptions — that is the stated reason the whole AuthState machine exists. The scenario is therefore routine: a live conversation is pinned to a home that goes Dead. If the is_dead term regresses, route_affinity keeps pinning the lineage to a revoked token and every request in that conversation returns a hard 401 to a paying customer instead of a transparent rotation — the exact failure the crate doc calls out as worse than an Overloaded.

*Suggested test.* Unit in crates/pool/src/lib.rs: place a lineage on a via route_affinity(newly_claimed=true), mark_done, then p.kill("a", "permission_error"); assert route_affinity("opaque-a", 0, false, identify) returns Selected{ sub: b, disposition: Rebound }. Add the exhaustion case: kill b as well and assert AffinityRoute::Exhausted rather than a Selected pointing at a dead subscription.

<a id="rust-pool--no-clock-injection-recovery-paths"></a>
#### `no-clock-injection-recovery-paths` — No injectable clock: every recovery transition (cooling expiry, stale binding, debounce close) is untested

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:70`

*Not covered today.* 'No injectable clock, therefore no recovery transition is tested' is too broad on two counts. First, the time-sensitive auth-health machine IS tested with an injected clock — apply_probe takes `now` explicitly and the tests drive it across DEAD_MIN_SECS boundaries (crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2618-2633, :2635-2663, :2667-2678). Second, a recovery transition IS proven without sleeping by feeding a past timestamp: window_rollover_resets_util (:1763-1772) sets reset5h to now()-10 and asserts the 0.99-utilized subscription becomes the preferred pick again, i.e. the suite does prove subscriptions come back. The accurate residual gap is narrower: cooling expiry re-entry (is_cooling at :489-494), mark_healthy's expired-cooling clear (:1166-1168), AFFINITY_TTL staleness rebinding (:1061), the 15s request_probe debounce reopening (:1286, whose closed half IS asserted at :2581-2605), and import_state dropping a dormant expired cooling row (:1606-1610). All of these are reachable with past/absolute timestamps in the same style as :1765, so the missing tests are cheap.

*Production risk.* The suite is asymmetric: it proves subscriptions get taken out of rotation and never proves they come back. A regression that makes cooling permanent (an off-by-one in the comparison, a cooling_until that only ever extends and is never re-evaluated) silently drains the fleet to zero routable subscriptions after the first 429 wave, and the operator sees a total outage with every test green. The mirror risk — a debounce that never closes — permanently blinds the ban detector.

*Suggested test.* Refactor toward the pattern apply_probe already uses: add crate-internal `*_at(now: i64)` variants of the stateful methods (or a test-only clock override on Pool), then assert: (a) mark_cooling(a, 60) then evaluate at now+61 → a is routable and is_cooling is false; (b) route a session, then evaluate at now+AFFINITY_TTL+1 → the binding is treated as stale and re-placed; (c) request_probe twice 16 s apart → the second call is honoured. Until the refactor lands, at minimum one `mark_cooling(a, 1)` + `std::thread::sleep(1100ms)` test to pin the expiry direction.

<a id="rust-pool--coverage-on-unreachable-route"></a>

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1049`
