But 'the equivalents on route_affinity mostly do not exist' is overstated: placement (crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2086), pin (:2097), deep-cooling rebind (:2107) and wait-then-spill-keeping-home-then-return-home (:2165-2205) are all asserted on route_affinity, and the pin is additionally covered end-to-end through the production composition in crates/forward/src/affinity.rs:1455-1480 and through real HTTP in tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh:53-58. Genuinely route-only: envelope-gated placement (:2215-2226), fleet spread (:2266 — partly duplicated by tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh:39-43 and :44-51) and the no-false-exhaustion spill (:2533-2553). The strongest surviving point is the auditor's own last one: nothing asserts AffinityRoute::Exhausted at all (grep: Exhausted appears only at lib.rs:486,1008,1022,1035 and proxy.rs:1129), so the Exhausted -> pick fallback composition is untested on both sides. The bindings-eviction sub-claim (:1116-1129) carries no production risk since it lives inside the unreachable route().

*Production risk.* The invariants look covered but are not. Most acutely, 'internal concurrency pressure must never become a false fleet-exhaustion Retry-After' is pinned only for `route` (lib.rs:2533); route_affinity handles the same situation differently — it returns Exhausted at lib.rs:1008 and depends on the caller falling back to pick — and no test covers that composition, so a change on either side turns a busy home into a spurious 429 + Retry-After for a customer whose fleet is actually healthy.

*Suggested test.* Port each legacy-route assertion to route_affinity as a paired test in crates/pool/src/lib.rs (busy-home-with-only-cooling-alternatives must not return Exhausted-then-cooling; short-burst cooling spills and keeps the home; placement respects the envelope). Then either delete Pool::route/bindings as dead code or mark it #[cfg(test)] so the coverage split is visible.

<a id="rust-pool--merge-spend-unasserted"></a>
#### `merge-spend-unasserted` — The CAS-rebase spend merge is never asserted, though the fixture already has diverging values

**MEDIUM** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1706`

*Not covered today.* `persisted_local = spent_total - unpersisted; spent_total = persisted_local.max(remote) + unpersisted` is the reconciliation between two engine slots writing pool_state. lib.rs:2309 feeds a remote row with spent_total_usd 12.0 against a local 10.0 and then asserts version, caps, utils, reset and cooling — but never spent_total_usd. lib.rs:2363 covers the ack path only, not the conflict path.

*Production risk.* Spend feeds live_util (lib.rs:524) between provider headers and the calibration anchors. Losing the local unpersisted delta on every CAS conflict makes each subscription look cheaper than it is, so the scheduler keeps routing to an account that is actually near its window ceiling; double-adding it does the opposite and strands paid-for capacity. During blue-green cutover — when two slots overlap and CAS conflicts are most likely — this drift is systematic, not random.

*Suggested test.* Extend lib.rs:2309 or add a sibling: import_state with spent_total 10.0, record_spend of $3 (unpersisted 3.0, total 13.0), then merge_persisted_state with remote spent_total 12.0; assert exported spent_total_usd == 15.0 and spent_delta_usd == 3.0. Add the reverse case (remote lower than persisted_local) asserting the local value wins and the delta is untouched.

*Verified absent by.* Read the merge at crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1700-1712 (persisted_local / spent_total reconciliation) and both merge_persisted_state tests: :2309-2360 asserts version, cap5h, cap7d, util5h, util7d, reset7d and cooling_until but never spent_total_usd, and :2017-2037 asserts only cap5h_usd/calib_n. Neither test calls record_spend before merging, so live.unpersisted_spend_usd is 0.0 at both call sites and the `+ unpersisted` term — the whole point of the rebase — is never non-zero on this path. grep -rn 'merge_persisted_state' over crates/ shows the only production caller is crates/server/src/poller.rs:458 (the CAS-conflict reload), so the path is live in blue-green cutovers exactly as described. The neighbouring test at :2363-2387 covers accept_persist_versions (the ack path), not the conflict path, confirming the auditor's distinction. No coverage in TypeScript or shell either: grep for spent_total across apps/, packages/ and deploy/ finds no engine pool_state merge assertions.

<a id="rust-pool--saturation-tier-untested"></a>
#### `saturation-tier-untested` — Fleet-saturation ordering (all candidates over the concurrency envelope) has no test

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:576`

*Not covered today.* 'Never reached by a test' is factually wrong. route_spreads_sessions_across_fleet (crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2266-2286) issues 200 routes over 4 subs and adds 2 in-flight per iteration (route increments at :1140-ish plus the explicit mark_used at :2275) with MAX_INFLIGHT=6 (:92), so from roughly iteration 12 onward EVERY candidate is over the envelope: place_best's fallback at :713 and the `if a_over && b_over` branch at :576-607 execute on ~185 iterations, and the test does pin the load-bearing no-stall property (p.route(s).unwrap() at :2273 would panic if saturated selection returned None). What is genuinely unasserted is only the ORDER inside that branch (in-flight -> over_cap -> 7d -> 5h -> warn -> LRU). Severity dropped to low because the '20+ concurrent streams on one subscription' outcome cannot occur: the authoritative PostgreSQL gate rejects any lease once inflight >= max_inflight (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:786-793, called per attempt at crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1173-1191), and that gate has a gated multithreaded test (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2470-2503). A pure ordering regression degrades fairness/retry churn, not account safety.

*Production risk.* This is the peak-load path — the only moment when routing choice actually matters for stability. If saturation ordering regresses to plain utilization comparison, the emptiest account keeps winning while already over the envelope and absorbs the entire burst: 20+ concurrent streams on one consumer subscription is both an immediate 429 source and the clearest automation fingerprint in the system, which risks a ban rather than a slowdown.

*Suggested test.* Unit in crates/pool/src/lib.rs: three subs, drive a and b to exactly max_inflight() and c to max_inflight()+3 via mark_used, give c the lowest utilization, then assert pick() returns the lowest in-flight candidate (a or b by LRU), not c. A second case: push every sub over the envelope AND over the reserve cap, then assert place_best still returns a subscription (no stall) and that it is the least-loaded one.

<a id="rust-pool--capacity-availability-math"></a>
#### `capacity-availability-math` — capacity() availability horizons, reset normalisation and cooling proration have no assertions

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1483`

*Not covered today.* The coverage statement is accurate — no test asserts avail_1h/5h/1d/7d, so the resets() closure (crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1483-1497), the past-reset normalisation and the cooling proration (:1505-1506) are unverified; the only related test feeds hand-built Cap values to the consumer aggregation (crates/server/src/http.rs:1114-1126). Severity drops to low because these fields are reporting-only: their sole consumers are /capacity (crates/server/src/http.rs:522-561) and /overview (:755-770), and the hourly snapshot writer takes cap5h/cap7d/util only, not avail (crates/server/src/poller.rs:169-174). No routing, admission, billing or pricing path reads avail_* — routing uses live_util/placement_free (:506-525, :658-690), and admission is gated by the PostgreSQL capacity lease. An off-by-one here misleads an operator reading a dashboard; it does not itself oversell quota.

*Production risk.* These numbers are the fleet's capacity picture: /capacity, /overview and the hourly metrics snapshots operators and commercial planning read. An off-by-one that counts one extra window reset overstates available quota by a full 5h window per subscription per horizon — the fleet looks like it has headroom it does not, admission and top-up decisions are made against a fiction, and the correction only surfaces as customer-visible 429s.

*Suggested test.* Unit in crates/pool/src/lib.rs with a single max20 sub and pinned resets: set reset5h = now + 3600 and assert avail_1h_usd == rem5h + 1*cap5h while avail_1h with reset5h = now + 3601 excludes the reset. Assert reset exactly == now is counted zero times (the double-count guard). Assert an idle subscription whose reset is 3 windows in the past normalises to the next future reset rather than counting past resets. Finally, cool the sub for h/2 and assert avail_1h is halved.

<a id="rust-pool--no-concurrency-test"></a>
#### `no-concurrency-test` — No multithreaded test exists for the shared scheduler behind every request

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:717`

*Not covered today.* 'No multithreaded test exists for the shared scheduler' is refuted at the layer that actually enforces the invariant. crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2470-2503 spawns 8 threads on a Barrier, has them race acquire_capacity for the same subscription with max_inflight=3, and asserts exactly 3 leases are granted, pool_state.inflight == 3, and 0 after release — i.e. 'N concurrent acquisitions produce exactly N slot increments and never oversell' is pinned, and it runs inside the CI gate (deploy/watchdog.sh:333-334 passes CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL). Additionally tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh:44-51 fires 60 genuinely parallel HTTP requests through the real server against a mock upstream and asserts fan-out and a per-subscription share ceiling (though this script is a manual guard — it is not invoked by deploy/watchdog.sh). Narrower true claim: the Pool's in-memory choose-then-increment critical section (lib.rs:717-719) has no direct multithreaded test; its production consequence is bounded because PostgreSQL re-validates the same envelope per request (crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1163-1191).

*Production risk.* A thundering herd of new sessions all read the same emptiest home (crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1084 peek under a read lock, then an await on Redis, then route_affinity under the write lock) — nothing pins that the concurrent revalidation actually keeps in-flight bounded, or that N concurrent picks produce exactly N slot increments. A refactor that split the choose-then-increment critical section, or a leaked/double release, is invisible until in-flight drifts and the human-envelope protection quietly stops working in production.

*Suggested test.* Unit in crates/pool/src/lib.rs: spawn 64 threads against an Arc<Pool> with 4 subscriptions, each calling pick()/route_affinity then mark_done in a loop; join and assert gauges().0 == 0 (no leaked or negative slots). A second thread-based test: hold all subscriptions at max_inflight()-1 and let 32 threads race pick(), asserting no subscription ever exceeds max_inflight() while an under-envelope candidate existed.

<a id="rust-pool--burst-cooling-keeps-home"></a>
#### `burst-cooling-keeps-home` — Short burst cooling must spill and keep the home — never tested on either routing path

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:965`

*Not covered today.* The specific branch is indeed unexercised — every mark_cooling value in the test module is 300, 3600, 60, 10 or REBIND_AFTER+1 and none of them puts a route/route_affinity home into short-burst cooling (crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1788,1808,1822,2106,2254,2294,2430,2484-2486,2541,2557) — and the branch is production-reachable (a 429 without Retry-After cools 20s: BURST_COOL_SECS at crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1530, used at :1570, vs REBIND_AFTER=60 at pool/src/lib.rs:86). Severity drops to low for two reasons. (1) The 'spill one request, keep the binding' semantics ARE pinned, just through the other busy trigger: crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2165-2205 (affinity: Wait -> Spilled -> back to Pinned) and :2228-2245 (legacy route). (2) The wait arithmetic at :967-971 is not meaningfully untested math — with the shipped default CLAUDE_API_AFFINITY_WAIT_MS=250 (crates/server/src/config.rs:476-481) cooling_for*1000 always saturates to max_wait_ms, so the branch reduces to a constant. The residual gap is the burst-vs-deep threshold itself, whose deep side is already pinned at :2106 (REBIND_AFTER+1 rebinds) and :2254.

Both outcomes convert a transient blip into either sustained cost inflation or a ban-signal loop.

Add the deep case at REBIND_AFTER seconds exactly to pin the boundary.

<a id="rust-pool--nonfinite-util-latches-calibration"></a>
#### `nonfinite-util-latches-calibration` — A non-finite utilization value permanently latches a subscription's calibration anchor; nothing tests or guards it

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:202`

*Not covered today.* The permanence — the core of this finding — is wrong, and I verified it by reading the control flow rather than trusting the summary. calib_window re-anchors UNCONDITIONALLY at crates/pool/src/lib.rs:236-237 for any sample that gets past the two early returns, and with a NaN anchor both guards are false (:202 `new_util + 1e-9 < NaN` is false, :209 `NaN < CALIB_MIN_DELTA` is false), so control reaches :236 and the very next well-formed header writes a finite anchor back. A NaN therefore costs one skipped calibration interval, not a permanent latch, and cap itself can never be poisoned because plausible_cap requires is_finite (:149-150) before the write at :230. Also the cited sourceFile line is mislabelled: lib.rs:202 is calib_window's re-anchor comparison; set_util is at lib.rs:1358 (it does indeed lack a finite check, and no test feeds non-finite/negative/>1 utilization anywhere — that part of the claim stands). Remaining real risk: util5h/util7d hold NaN until the next header, which makes live_util NaN and excludes the subscription from placement (placement_eligible, :673-690) — and reaching it requires Anthropic or an intermediary to emit a literal 'NaN'/'nan' header (crates/forward/src/upstream.rs:212-215).

*Production risk.* One malformed provider header silently and permanently disables capacity measurement for that subscription; live_util also returns NaN until the next good header, and placement_eligible (which requires `live_util < cap`) excludes the subscription from all new placements in the meantime. The NaN can then be exported into the durable pool_state row and re-imported as the seeded anchor after restart. The failure is invisible — no error, no metric, and calibrated simply stays false.

*Suggested test.* Unit in crates/pool/src/lib.rs: call set_util with Some(f64::NAN), then with a normal 0.10/0.20 sequence plus record_spend, and assert capacity()[0].calibrated becomes true (i.e. NaN was rejected rather than latched). Add table cases for -1.0 and 5.0 asserting the value is ignored or clamped and the anchor stays finite. Pair with a guard in set_util/calib_window rejecting non-finite samples.

### 8.4 `rust-forward-proxy` — Rust forward crate — proxy/transport core

This crate is the product: it takes an ordinary Anthropic request, authorizes the caller into one of three privilege classes, reserves money, picks a pooled subscription, injects the Claude Code identity + OAuth headers, forwards to api.anthropic.com through that subscription's egress proxy, classifies upstream failures (429 → cooling, 401/403 → probe, 5xx → circuit breaker), rotates before the stream starts, and streams the response back byte-for-byte while a tee meter closes the money loop. The test suite is 33 `#[test]`/`#[tokio::test]` across the in-scope files, and it is sharply bimodal: the pure, side-effect-free helpers are tested unusually well (the balance-cap budget test at proxy.rs:1806 is close to a property test; the endpoint allowlist, LocalErr leak guard, breaker distinct-subscription semantics, and affinity's native-session isolation boundary are all real regression guards), while everything that touches HTTP is tested not at all. `pub async fn forward` (proxy.rs:731), the ~730-line handler holding the retry budget, blame classification, capacity lease, RAII guards, header stripping and rotation, is never invoked by any test — no test in the workspace constructs an `AppState`, and no crate in `crates/` has a `[dev-dependencies]` section, so there is no HTTP test harness at all. Streaming and mid-stream failover are therefore untested end-to-end: `stream_back` (proxy.rs:1590), `KeyGuardStream` (proxy.rs:147) and `TeeMeter` are never driven, and no fixture anywhere emits an SSE body. The only artifact that drives the real HTTP surface — `tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh` against `tests/mock_upstream.py` — is manual-only (nothing in `deploy/watchdog.sh` invokes it), runs with `CLAUDE_API_BILLING=0`, and its mock always returns 200, so even if it were gated it would cover fan-out and cache pinning but never rotation, error classification, or streaming.

**Working well here:**

- **Balance-cap budget enforcement (the 'client never gets a token or cent beyond balance' invariant)** — `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1806` — This is the strongest test in the crate and close to a property test: nested loops over four multipliers (including the 0.09x and 3.33x extremes) and four balance magnitudes assert three separate invariants — hold <= bal + OVERDRAFT_NANO, worst-case charge <= hold, and, when truncation occurred, that eff_mt+1 output tokens WOULD have breached bal+$1. That last assertion is what makes it valuable: it pins the tightness of the cap, not just its safety, so an over-conservative refactor that silently starts refusing funded users would fail. It then pins the i64 clamp with `cap_to_balance(i64::MAX as i128, ...)` and the zero-multiplier free-key path. A regression in the reservation ceiling math is the most expensive bug this codebase could ship, and this test would catch it.
- **Endpoint allowlist / path traversal on the subscription-quota surface** — `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1723` — A proper table test that covers both directions. Beyond the obvious allow/deny pairs it pins the security-relevant cases: `/v1/models/%2e%2e/%2e%2e/api/oauth/profile`, `/v1/models/%2Fapi%2Foauth%2Fprofile`, and a backslash variant — i.e. it defends the specific attack of routing a crafted path onto a subscription's OAuth token to reach /api/oauth/profile. It also pins method sensitivity (`GET /v1/messages` must fail). This would catch a real regression if someone loosened the `%`/`\`/`//` rejection in is_supported_endpoint.
- **Synthetic-error sanitizer — no internal architecture leaks to clients** — `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1857 (ALL_LOCAL_ERRS) and crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1870` — The `ALL_LOCAL_ERRS` const array is the right design: it forces a compile-time-visible decision when a variant is added, and the test then sweeps every variant against a forbidden-term list (subscription, pool, upstream, authority, cooling, rotat, persona, fleet, oauth, quota). Paired with proxy.rs:1901, which pins each variant's exact (status, error.type) against authentic Anthropic triples including the unusual 529 overloaded_error and the 402 LowBalance product contract, this genuinely protects the 'indistinguishable from api.anthropic.com' invariant against a careless new error variant.
- **Byte-exact on-wire header case and ordering (fleet anti-fingerprinting)** — `crates/forward/src/nodetls.rs:81` — Rare and well-executed: it binds a real TcpListener, sends a request through a client built with `bun_emulation()`, reads the raw request bytes, and asserts on them. It catches the exact class of subtle regression a header-map refactor causes — `X-Stainless-OS` silently becoming `X-Stainless-Os`, `anthropic-beta` becoming Title-Case — and additionally asserts positional ordering of the Connection/Host/Accept-Encoding/Content-Length transport tail relative to application headers. This is the only test in the crate that inspects actual wire bytes.
- **Native session ID as a hard isolation boundary in cache affinity** — `crates/forward/src/affinity.rs:1192` — Tests the property that actually matters for correctness rather than the happy path: two DIFFERENT sessions sending byte-identical bodies with an identical cacheable system root must not resolve to each other's binding (`resolve` returns None), yet may still see the other's home as a warm placement hint. It then confirms the second session gets a distinct session_id and its own home. This is the invariant that prevents cross-session cache lineage confusion, and it is asserted from both sides.
- **Affinity → pool integration under real capacity state** — `crates/forward/src/affinity.rs:1427` — Not a mock: it constructs a real `pool::Pool` with two subscriptions and asymmetric utilization, does a real `peek_affinity_home` → `claim` → `route_affinity` cycle, then feeds a genuinely grown transcript through `infer`/`resolve` and asserts the continuation routes back to the SAME subscription with `AffinityDisposition::Pinned`. It crosses a crate boundary with no test doubles, so it would catch a digest-chaining change in affinity or a placement change in pool.
- **Circuit breaker distinct-subscription semantics** — `crates/forward/src/breaker.rs:97 and crates/forward/src/breaker.rs:128` — The tests encode the design intent, not the implementation: 20 failures from one email must not open the breaker (one flaky proxy is not an outage), a strict majority of distinct emails must, and cooldown expiry must re-close. `threshold_tracks_roster_reload` covers the easily-missed case where a hot roster reload changes the denominator and previously-collected samples become incomparable. For 90 lines of source this is the right amount of test, aimed at the right places.
- **Redis affinity fails open rather than failing requests** — `crates/forward/src/affinity.rs:1404` — Points the store at a guaranteed-dead port (127.0.0.1:1) and asserts that claim/resolve still succeed locally. This pins the load-bearing architectural promise that the L2 cache can never gate a request — a Redis outage must degrade cache hit rate, never availability. Cheap, deterministic, and it protects a real production failure mode.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--forward-handler-never-driven"></a>
#### `forward-handler-never-driven` — The entire `forward` handler is never executed by any test — no HTTP test harness exists in the workspace

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:731`

*Not covered today.* No test in the *gated* suite drives the handler. The factual assertion 'no test constructs AppState or invokes proxy::forward' is false: deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:148-176 runs the real binary and issues two concurrent POST /v1/messages with a metered `sk-pool-…` key and CLAUDE_API_BILLING=1 (line 103), asserting the Anthropic envelope (:162) and the closed money loop (2 settled reservations, 2 charge ledger rows, 2 done outbox rows, 0 active capacity leases, 0 pool inflight, reserved_nano=0 — line 173). tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh:29-52 drives 122 requests through the handler on the admin path. Neither is invoked by watchdog.sh's prepare_and_test_candidate (deploy/watchdog.sh:291-360, which runs only `cargo test` at :334 plus watchdog-lib.test.sh/monitoring-config.test.sh at :343-344); the e2e is documented as a manual root-only command (DEPLOYMENT.md:161). Also, the three 'concrete' regressions cited do NOT all survive as claimed: the e2e's exactly-once ledger assertion tolerates a lost hold_guard.disarm() (registry enforces exactly-once settle — crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2491 double_settle_no_overcredit), so that specific example is right, but it is right for a different reason than 'nothing executes the handler'. Accurate gap: the handler IS executed by two ungated harnesses, but only on the 200 happy path, so the concurrency semaphore overflow, the ?beta=true merge, the 4-attempt re-reserve loop, and disconnect-time RAII remain unasserted anywhere.

*Production risk.* Any refactor of the ~730-line handler ships with a green build and a green `cargo test --locked --workspace` in deploy/watchdog.sh:334. Concretely: dropping the `hold_guard.disarm()` at proxy.rs:1430 double-refunds every successful request; moving it before the `mark_delivering` check charges every cancelled request twice; removing the `guard.disarm()` at 1429 decrements pool in-flight twice per streamed request and lets the fleet over-admit until restart. None of these produce a test failure today.

*Suggested test.* Integration level, in a new `crates/forward/tests/forward_handler.rs` with `tower` + `http-body-util` as dev-dependencies. Add a `#[cfg(test)] pub fn test_state(upstream: &str) -> AppState` constructor in crates/forward/src/state.rs (billing: None, a 2-subscription pool, affinity with no Redis). Point `cfg.upstream` at a `tokio::net::TcpListener`-backed stub. Assert, per request: exact status/body/content-type passthrough, that a client-supplied `x-api-key` never appears in the bytes the stub received, and that `pool` in-flight returns to zero after the response body is fully consumed AND after the response future is dropped mid-body.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--rotation-blame-classification"></a>
#### `rotation-blame-classification` — Upstream failure classification and rotation (429 / 401 / 403 / 5xx) has zero coverage of any kind

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1318`

*Not covered today.* The three blame branches at proxy.rs:1318 (429 → mark_cooling with cool_secs_429), proxy.rs:1327-1364 (401/403 → request_probe + probe_poke, rotate at most once, then return the real upstream response byte-for-byte) and proxy.rs:1365-1383 (5xx/408/409/425 → breaker.record_fail at most once per request, consume the backend budget) are untested. The documented budget asymmetry — subscription-blame errors must NOT consume `max_tries` while backend-blame errors must — is asserted nowhere. The `backend_fail_recorded` anti-DoS flag (proxy.rs:1052, 1369) that stops a single poison request from tripping the global breaker by itself is asserted nowhere. `tests/mock_upstream.py:47` always returns 200, so even the manual smoke script cannot reach any of these branches.

*Production risk.* Two specific, plausible regressions ship silently. (a) If the 401/403 branch loses its `auth_tries < 2` bound, one crafted request with an out-of-scope model rotates through and probe-flags the entire fleet, and Anthropic sees a burst of auth failures across every pooled account — the exact ban signal the design avoids. (b) If 429 handling starts consuming the backend budget, a single rate-limited subscription short-circuits rotation and the client receives a 429 while healthy subscriptions sit idle, converting a routine cooldown into a customer-visible outage.

*Suggested test.* Integration level, same harness as `forward-handler-never-driven`, in crates/forward/tests/. Drive a scripted stub upstream that returns a caller-supplied status sequence. Table cases: [429, 200] must succeed on the second subscription with the first marked cooling and `max_tries` unspent; [401, 401] must return the second upstream response verbatim (status, body bytes, request-id header) with no cooling applied to either; [500, 500, 500] with max_tries=2 must stop after 2 upstream sends and record exactly ONE breaker failure; [429, 429, ...] across the whole fleet must yield 429 with a Retry-After equal to `pool.soonest_ready()`.

*Verified absent by.* Searched: `grep -rn 'backend_fail_recorded' --include=*.rs .` → only crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1052,1369,1371 (definition + two uses, no test). `grep -rn 'auth_tries|upstream_auth|upstream_429|upstream_5xx' crates/` → production only. The proxy test mod (crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1619-1960) contains 11 tests, none touching status classification. The downstream *effects* are unit-tested — crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1818 cooling_excludes_while_alternative_exists, :2247 route_rebinds_on_deep_cooling, crates/forward/src/breaker.rs:97 trips_only_on_distinct_subs_and_recovers — but the decision of WHEN to cool/record-fail, the max_tries budget asymmetry (proxy.rs:1321-1325 vs :1377-1382), and the `auth_tries < 2` bound (proxy.rs:1343) are asserted nowhere. Minor correction to the claim: tests/mock_upstream.py does not 'always return 200' — do_GET returns 403 (tests/mock_upstream.py:50-51); it returns 200 only for POST (:49), which is all the smoke issues, so the substance holds — no harness in the repo can reach the 429/401/403/5xx branches. Severity adjusted from critical to high: the two most damaging consequences (fleet-wide cooling, breaker trip) run through primitives that do have unit tests, so a regression is one step removed from the untested code rather than directly in it.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--auth-class-ladder"></a>
#### `auth-class-ladder` — The three-class authorization ladder (admin ⊂ control ⊂ readonly) is untested — a control key gaining /v1 forwarding would not fail any test

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:286`

*Not covered today.* Two of the four sub-claims are wrong or overstated. (a) The trust_loopback invariant IS exercised: deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:104 starts the engine with CLAUDE_API_TRUST_LOOPBACK=0 and no CLAUDE_API_KEYS, then issues a loopback request with only a metered sk-pool key (:153); if `authed` regressed to trusting any 127.0.0.1 peer the request would take the Admin path, no reservation would be created, and the '2:2:2:0:0:0' assertion at :173/:176 would fail. Ungated, but a real assertion. (b) The available_nano derivation at proxy.rs:379-388 is a non-authoritative pre-check — the binding spend-limit gate is the SQL/SQLite reserve, which is well tested at crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2931 per_key_policy_gates_reservations_and_releases_allowance (limit, expiry, disabled key, release-on-settle) and crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2019-2100 inside stage2_fault_matrix. A regression in the proxy derivation cannot overspend a key. Genuinely uncovered: (1) the class boundary itself — nothing pins that a control key must not forward /v1; (2) the Authz::Unavailable → Overloaded mapping at proxy.rs:759.

*Production risk.* If `authorize` were changed to call `control_authed` instead of `authed` at proxy.rs:368 — a plausible one-word edit during a refactor that unifies the auth helpers — every commerce control key would gain free, unmetered, unbilled forwarding of /v1/messages on subscription quota. The build stays green and all 33 tests pass. Symmetrically, if `trust_loopback` handling regressed, the public bind behind Caddy would grant anonymous admin-equivalent forwarding to anyone who can reach the reverse proxy.

*Suggested test.* Unit level, in the existing `mod tests` in crates/forward/src/proxy.rs (these functions need only a `ProxyConfig` + `HeaderMap` + `SocketAddr`, no I/O). Table test over the cartesian product of {admin key, control key, panel key, unknown key, no key} × {loopback peer, remote peer} × {api_keys empty/non-empty, trust_loopback true/false}, asserting the expected (authed, control_authed, readonly_authed) triple for each cell. Add a separate case asserting a control key produces `Authz::Unauthorized` from `authorize` when billing is None, and a case asserting `available_nano` for a key with spend_limit_nano set and non-zero spent+reserved.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--streaming-transparency-untested"></a>
#### `streaming-transparency-untested` — SSE streaming and the response-body guards are never exercised — no fixture in the repo emits a `text/event-stream` body

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1590`

*Not covered today.* `stream_back` (1590), `KeyGuardStream` (147-186) and `is_event_stream` (1507) have no tests, and neither does `TeeMeter` (crates/forward/src/meter.rs has 0 `#[test]`). `tests/mock_upstream.py` returns only `application/json`, so no fixture anywhere in the repo produces an SSE response. The specific contract that is unverified: `KeyGuardStream::poll_next` (172) releases the fair-share slot and the global concurrency permit on `Ready(None)` and on `Ready(Some(Err))`, but a client that simply stops polling (disconnect mid-stream) relies on `Drop` instead — and the interaction between that Drop, the TeeMeter finalize, and `pool.end_stream` is what keeps in-flight accounting and the money loop consistent for aborted streams.

*Production risk.* The product's #1 invariant is byte-for-byte protocol transparency including SSE. A regression that buffers the body, re-encodes it, or drops the final `message_stop` event breaks every streaming client — this is the single most customer-visible failure the system can produce, and nothing detects it before production. Separately, if the slot/permit release were lost on the mid-stream-abort path, long-lived aborted SSE connections leak both fair-share slots and global semaphore permits, driving the instance to blanket 529s until restart.

*Suggested test.* Integration level, in crates/forward/tests/. Stub upstream emits a canned multi-chunk SSE body (message_start / content_block_delta ×N / message_delta with usage / message_stop) with `content-type: text/event-stream`. Assert: (1) the client-side concatenated bytes equal the stub's bytes exactly, including chunk framing across at least two TCP writes; (2) `is_event_stream` selected the SSE parse path; (3) after full consumption, the global semaphore has all permits back and key-limiter in-flight is 0; (4) a second case where the response body is dropped after the first chunk also returns all permits and calls `pool.end_stream` exactly once.

*Verified absent by.* `grep -rn 'text/event-stream' --include=*.rs --include=*.sh --include=*.py --include=*.ts .` (excluding node_modules/target) returns exactly three hits, all production code: crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1511, crates/forward/src/codex/chat.rs:942, crates/forward/src/codex/api.rs:1822. No fixture emits an SSE body — tests/mock_upstream.py:39-48 and deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:74-92 both send only application/json. crates/forward/src/meter.rs is 428 lines with zero `#[test]` (verified per-file count), so TeeMeter::poll_next, retain_sse_usage and the bounded background drain in Drop (meter.rs:385-420) — the code that decides how much a streamed request is actually charged — are entirely unexercised; the claim understates this by framing meter.rs only as a transparency concern. KeyGuardStream (proxy.rs:147-186) and stream_back (proxy.rs:1590) likewise have no test. Partial mitigation worth noting: the Codex adapter has its own downstream-disconnect SSE test (crates/forward/src/codex/api.rs:2575 sse_send_stops_immediately_after_downstream_disconnect), but that is a separate streaming implementation and shares no code with stream_back/TeeMeter.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--delivery-marker-charges-full-hold"></a>
#### `delivery-marker-charges-full-hold` — A fenced delivery marker charges the customer the entire reserved ceiling while returning them an error, and nothing tests it

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1404`

*Not covered today.* When upstream returns 2xx but `billing.mark_delivering` does not return `Ok(true)` (fencing, authority blip), proxy.rs:1410-1418 calls `settle_detached(request_id, account_id, key, hold, hold, Some("delivery-marker-failed"), None)` — actual equals hold, i.e. the full reserved ceiling is charged — then disarms the hold guard and returns `LocalErr::Overloaded` (529) to the client. The reserved ceiling is deliberately a worst case: `cap_to_balance` (proxy.rs:565) prices the entire request body at the `cache_write_1h` rate and adds `eff_mt × output`, so for a typical request it is far larger than actual usage. There is no test for this branch, and no test asserts the relationship between the charged amount and any plausible real usage on this path.

*Production risk.* During an authority failover or fencing event, a burst of customers are each debited the full worst-case ceiling for requests that returned them a 529 and zero tokens — potentially several dollars per request against prepaid balances, with a ledger row that reads `delivery-marker-failed`. This is a real-money, customer-visible overcharge that is currently invisible to the test suite; a refactor could also easily invert the argument order (hold, actual) here and refund instead, giving away delivered inference, with equally no signal.

*Suggested test.* Integration level in crates/forward/tests/ with a fake `AsyncBilling` whose `mark_delivering` returns `Ok(false)`. Assert the client receives 529 `overloaded_error`, that exactly one settlement was dispatched, that it carries reason `delivery-marker-failed`, and pin the charged amount explicitly (a snapshot assertion on `actual == hold`) so that the policy is a deliberate, reviewable decision rather than an accident. Pair it with a unit assertion in crates/forward/src/proxy.rs tests that `HoldGuard::disarm` was called so the guard cannot double-settle on the same request_id.

*Verified absent by.* Code reading verified: crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1407 `if !matches!(billing.mark_delivering(request_id, 3600).await, Ok(true))` → :1410-1417 `settle_detached(request_id, account_id, key, *hold, *hold, Some("delivery-marker-failed"), None)` (actual == hold) → :1418-1420 hold_guard.disarm() → :1421 `return local_err(LocalErr::Overloaded, Some(2))`. The ceiling is the worst case: cap_to_balance prices the whole body at p.cache_write_1h (proxy.rs:565). `grep -rn 'delivery-marker-failed' --include=*.rs --include=*.sh --include=*.ts .` → a single hit, proxy.rs:1416, i.e. no test. The registry primitive is tested (mark_delivering true/false and lease expiry at crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2313, :2359, :2440 and crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2364, :2400, :2424), but nothing tests the proxy's reaction to a false return, and no test relates the charged amount to plausible usage on this path.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--header-strip-lists-unpinned-and-drifted"></a>
#### `header-strip-lists-unpinned-and-drifted` — Neither request nor response header strip list is tested, and the request list has already drifted out of sync with the affinity header list

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:211`

*Not covered today.* `skip_req_header` (211) and `skip_resp_header` (235) have no tests. They enforce two stated invariants: client `authorization`/`x-api-key` must never reach Anthropic (replaced by the subscription Bearer), and `anthropic-ratelimit-*` / `anthropic-organization*` / `anthropic-account-id` must never reach the client (they would disclose that this is a pool and would jump around on rotation). Concrete evidence of drift: `AffinityStore::infer` reads six session headers (crates/forward/src/affinity.rs:227-234 — `x-claude-code-session-id`, `x-conversation-id`, `x-session-id`, `session-id`, `x-thread-id`, `x-session-affinity`) while `skip_req_header` strips only the first three (proxy.rs:222), so `session-id`, `x-thread-id` and `x-session-affinity` are forwarded verbatim to api.anthropic.com. Also unstripped and unverified: `x-stainless-*` is handled by prefix match at proxy.rs:213 but nothing pins that a client-supplied `x-stainless-lang: python` cannot coexist with the synthesized `claude-cli` UA.

*Production risk.* A leaked client `authorization` header upstream would send a customer's own API key to Anthropic alongside a pooled OAuth token — a credential disclosure. Leaked `anthropic-ratelimit-*` downstream tells any customer they are on a shared pool and exposes another account's utilization. The already-present drift means requests carrying `x-thread-id`/`x-session-affinity` reach Anthropic with headers no genuine Claude Code client ever sends, which is a fleet fingerprint — and it happened precisely because nothing pins the list.

*Suggested test.* Unit level, in the existing `mod tests` in crates/forward/src/proxy.rs — both functions are pure `&str → bool`. Two table tests. For `skip_req_header`, assert true for every entry in an explicit list (authorization, x-api-key, user-agent, anthropic-beta, anthropic-version, accept, accept-encoding, host, content-length, all hop-by-hop, all forwarding headers, `x-stainless-anything`) and false for a representative passthrough header. Critically, add an assertion that iterates the SAME six-header array used by `AffinityStore::infer` and requires `skip_req_header` to be true for each, so the two lists can never drift again. For `skip_resp_header`, assert true for `anthropic-ratelimit-unified-5h-utilization`, `anthropic-organization-id`, `anthropic-account-id`, `content-encoding`, `content-length`, and false for `request-id`, `anthropic-version`, `retry-after`.

*Verified absent by.* `grep -rn 'skip_req_header|skip_resp_header' --include=*.rs .` → crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:211 (def), :1238 (use), :235 (def), :1599 (use) — no test. The drift is real and I verified both sides: crates/forward/src/affinity.rs:228-234 iterates six headers (x-claude-code-session-id, x-conversation-id, x-session-id, session-id, x-thread-id, x-session-affinity) while crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:222 strips only the first three; the upstream request builder at crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1235-1240 is blacklist-based (`for (name, value) in parts.headers.iter() { if !skip_req_header(n) { rb = rb.header(...) } }`), not a whitelist, so `session-id`, `x-thread-id` and `x-session-affinity` do reach api.anthropic.com verbatim. One narrowing: the credential-disclosure half is a regression-only risk, not a present defect — `authorization` and `x-api-key` are in the list at proxy.rs:221.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--identity-injection-untested"></a>
#### `identity-injection-untested` — `inject_identity` — the single mechanism that makes subscription OAuth tokens work — has no test

**HIGH** · verdict `UNVERIFIED` · `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:594`

*Not covered today.* `inject_identity` (594) and its guard `is_cc_marker` (588) are referenced only at their definitions and at the call site (proxy.rs:884-885); there is no test. Untested branches: absent `system` → create a one-element array; `system` as a String → wrap as `[identity, {text: original}]` preserving the client's prompt as the second block; `system` as an Array whose first block already carries a Claude Code marker → do not double-inject; non-object body or a body without `messages` → leave untouched. The sibling function `set_billing_block` IS tested (proxy.rs:1671), which makes the omission look accidental rather than deliberate.

*Production risk.* If identity injection silently stops firing or emits a malformed shape, Anthropic rejects every OAuth-token request and the entire product returns 401 — total outage across the whole fleet at once. If it double-injects on rotation (the exact bug `set_billing_block` is tested against), every rotated request carries two identity blocks, which is both a wire-level fingerprint and a prompt-cache-busting prefix change that silently multiplies token cost on every rotation.

*Suggested test.* Unit level, in the existing `mod tests` in crates/forward/src/proxy.rs — it is a pure `&mut Value` function. Table test over five bodies: no system; system as String; system as String already starting with 'You are Claude Code'; system as Array with a non-CC first block; system as Array whose first block is a CC marker. Assert the resulting `system` array length, that index 0 is the identity text, that the client's original content survives at index 1 unmodified, that no `cache_control` key is added to the identity block (proxy.rs:602-606 documents this as fingerprint-critical), and that a body without `messages` is returned byte-identical.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--redis-l2-only-covered-by-ignored-test"></a>
#### `redis-l2-only-covered-by-ignored-test` — The Redis L2 affinity layer (~350 lines) is covered only by an `#[ignore]`d test that the gate never runs

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/affinity.rs:1483`

*Not covered today.* `redis_shares_opaque_affinity_across_processes` (affinity.rs:1483) is the only test covering `redis_claim` (839), `redis_remember` (920), `redis_rebind` (984), `redis_resolve` (717), `redis_warm_homes` (766) and `redis_mark_cache_warm` (800) — roughly affinity.rs:668-1020. It is annotated `#[ignore = "requires Redis at redis://default:test-affinity@127.0.0.1:16379/15"]`. The gate runs `cargo test --locked --workspace` (deploy/watchdog.sh:334) with no `--ignored` flag, and `deploy/watchdog-test-db.sh` provisions only PostgreSQL — no Redis service exists anywhere in the deploy tooling. So this test has never run in CI. It is also the only test asserting the privacy invariant that Redis contains no account names, raw session IDs, prompt text or subscription emails (affinity.rs:1604-1612).

*Production risk.* Production runs with Redis configured to share affinity across blue-green slots. A regression in the claim script's single-winner semantics (affinity.rs:1520-1523 is the only assertion of it) causes two slots to bind the same lineage to different homes, splitting the prompt cache and silently raising per-request cost on the most expensive long-context conversations. A regression that starts writing raw values into Redis keys leaks tenant account scopes and session IDs into an unencrypted shared store, and no test would notice.

*Suggested test.* Add a Redis container to the gate alongside the existing disposable PostgreSQL in deploy/watchdog-test-db.sh, export its URL, and convert the `#[ignore]` into the same env-gated pattern used elsewhere — but with a hard failure in CI rather than a silent skip (assert the env var is present when a `CI`-style marker is set, so it can never regress to silently-skipped). Keep the existing assertions; they are good. Additionally add a fast non-Redis unit test for `digest_parts`/`redis_alias_key`/`redis_session_key`/`redis_root_key` (affinity.rs:668-680, 1021) asserting the key strings contain only the keyed digest and the constant prefix — that pins the privacy invariant with zero infrastructure.

*Verified absent by.* `grep -rn '#\[ignore' crates/ --include=*.rs` returns exactly one hit workspace-wide: crates/forward/src/affinity.rs:1484 `#[ignore = "requires Redis at redis://default:test-affinity@127.0.0.1:16379/15"]` on redis_shares_opaque_affinity_across_processes (:1485). The gate runs `cargo test --locked --workspace` with no `--ignored` (deploy/watchdog.sh:334). `grep -c redis deploy/watchdog-test-db.sh` → 0, so the disposable test infrastructure provisions PostgreSQL only. deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:239-264 does assert things about affinity-redis.compose.yaml, but those are infra-config assertions (image digest, appendonly, port binding, unit ordering) that never execute redis_claim/redis_remember/redis_rebind/redis_resolve. The privacy assertions at affinity.rs:1604-1612 (no account names, session IDs, prompt text or subscription emails in the keyspace dump) therefore have never run in CI. Production does configure Redis (deploy/install-watchdog.sh:117-127 generates CLAUDE_API_REDIS_URL/PASSWORD), so the code is live.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--only-real-http-harness-is-ungated"></a>
#### `only-real-http-harness-is-ungated` — The one harness that drives real HTTP against `forward` is not wired into any gate, and its mock cannot produce failures

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh:1`

*Not covered today.* The core claim — nothing in any gate runs a harness that drives real HTTP against `forward` — is correct, but the survey is incomplete and two supporting statements are wrong. There are TWO such harnesses, not one: tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh and deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:148-176. The latter runs with CLAUDE_API_BILLING=1 (line 103) and a metered sk-pool key, and asserts the full money loop, so the framing that the only harness 'excludes the money path by construction' is false — that applies only to the fanout smoke. Also, tests/mock_upstream.py does not always return 200: do_GET returns 403 (mock_upstream.py:50-51). Correct residual gap: neither harness can produce an upstream error on the POST path or an SSE body, and neither is invoked by deploy/watchdog.sh, so fan-out/pinning and the money loop are both protected only by manual runs.

*Production risk.* The repo's documentation asserts that fan-out and cache pinning are protected by a regression guard; in practice they are protected by nothing, because nothing runs it. A placement or affinity change that reintroduces clustering — every session landing on one subscription, which is the ban-triggering pattern the design exists to avoid — ships green.

*Suggested test.* e2e level. Wire the script into `prepare_and_test_candidate` in deploy/watchdog.sh after the `cargo test` step (it already needs a built binary; the gate has one). Simultaneously extend `tests/mock_upstream.py` with a scripted-status mode (env-driven sequence, e.g. 429 then 200) and an SSE mode returning `text/event-stream`, then add smoke assertions for: rotation to a second subscription on 429, byte-identical SSE passthrough, and a client `x-api-key` never appearing in the mock's recorded request headers. Run it with `CLAUDE_API_BILLING` enabled against the disposable engine PostgreSQL the gate already provisions.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--limits-from-headers-untested"></a>
#### `limits-from-headers-untested` — The passive rate-limit harvest that drives the entire fleet capacity model is untested

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/upstream.rs:292`

*Not covered today.* No *unit* test and no gated test covers limits_from_headers, but the exact header names ARE exercised end-to-end by the ungated smoke harness: tests/mock_upstream.py:41-45 emits the five real unified-ratelimit headers with growing util, and tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh:41-45 asserts a fan-out (≥4/5 subscriptions, max share ≤18/50) that only holds if that util is harvested and fed to placement — AUDIT.md:288-292 states this dependency explicitly. So the headline failure mode the claim describes (a typo in a header name making every subscription read util 0.0) would be caught by an existing, runnable harness; it just isn't run by any gate. What is untested anywhere, in any harness: the util() >1.0 percent-format fallback and [0,1] clamp (upstream.rs:212-215), the numeric-vs-RFC3339 dispatch in reset_ts (upstream.rs:267-274), and parsing of `anthropic-ratelimit-unified-representative-claim` (upstream.rs:302-305) which window_cool treats as authoritative.

*Production risk.* A typo in a header name, or Anthropic switching from fraction to percent, makes every subscription read as util 0.0. The pool then routes maximum traffic at every subscription simultaneously, driving the whole fleet into hard 429s and ban territory — the failure mode the entire cooling design exists to prevent. The inverse (misparsing as 1.0) idles the whole paid fleet and returns 429 to every paying customer. Neither is detected before production.

*Suggested test.* Unit level, in the existing `mod tests` in crates/forward/src/upstream.rs — `wreq::header::HeaderMap` is trivially constructible. Build a HeaderMap from the exact header set the mock already documents (tests/mock_upstream.py:38-42) and assert every field of the resulting `Limits`. Add cases for: percent-format values (`"22"` → 0.22 via the >1.0 branch), out-of-range values clamping to [0,1], garbage values yielding None (so `has_util()` is false and pool state is left untouched), an RFC3339 reset falling back correctly, and the `anthropic-ratelimit-unified-representative-claim: five_hour` header reaching `Limits::claim` so the tested `window_cool` logic is actually reachable from real headers.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--smooth-wait-terminal-selection"></a>
#### `smooth-wait-terminal-selection` — The smooth-wait retry loop and its terminal-outcome selection are untested

**MEDIUM** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1470`

*Not covered today.* Only the leaf helper `smooth_step` is tested (proxy.rs:1635). The loop that uses it — `'smooth` at proxy.rs:1045 — and the terminal decision at proxy.rs:1470-1502 are untested. That block computes `backend_exhausted`, derives `is_transient` from a three-way disjunction (explicit hint / no upstream response but subscriptions were tried / upstream returned 429), and then chooses between three mutually exclusive outcomes: replay the last real upstream response byte-for-byte (1494), return the local synthetic error (1498), or synthesize a 429 with `Retry-After = pool.soonest_ready()` (1500-1502). The `last_upstream: Option<(StatusCode, wreq::Response)>` is held live across rotation attempts, which also means an unconsumed upstream connection is kept open for the duration of the round.

*Production risk.* If the priority order inverts, a real Anthropic 400/429 with its diagnostic body and request-id is replaced by our synthetic 'Overloaded', destroying SDK-side diagnosability for paying customers and masking client-side bugs as our outage. If `is_transient` becomes too permissive, requests silently burn the full `smooth_wait_ms` budget on genuinely dead pools, converting a fast 429 into a multi-second TTFB on every request during an outage.

*Suggested test.* Refactor the terminal decision into a pure helper (inputs: backend_exhausted, transient_hint, tried_is_empty, last_upstream_status, remaining_budget_ms; output: an enum of ReplayUpstream / LocalErr / SyntheticRateLimit / RetryAfterSleep) and table-test it in the existing `mod tests` in crates/forward/src/proxy.rs. That extracts the branching logic from the `wreq::Response` ownership that currently makes it untestable, and is a small, low-risk refactor.

*Verified absent by.* crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1635 smooth_step_bounds is the only test touching this area and covers only the leaf helper (proxy.rs:1578 fn smooth_step). The `'smooth: loop` at proxy.rs:1045, the backend_exhausted/is_transient derivation at :1470-1479 and the three-way terminal at :1493-1502 have no test; verified by reading the whole test mod (proxy.rs:1619-1960) and by `grep -rn 'is_transient|backend_exhausted|last_upstream|transient_hint' --include=*.rs .` returning production-only hits in proxy.rs. Severity medium is right: the failure is degraded diagnosability and latency, not money or bans.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--cool-secs-clamp-unpinned"></a>
#### `cool-secs-clamp-unpinned` — `cool_secs_429` precedence and the 8-day cooling clamp are untested, so a hostile Retry-After could park a healthy subscription

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1567`

*Not covered today.* cool_secs_429 (proxy.rs:1567-1572) and retry_after_header (proxy.rs:1524) are indeed untested, but both substantive halves of the risk are pinned elsewhere. The inference half — window_cool — is well tested including the authoritative-claim precedence (crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1646 window_cool_prefers_authoritative_claim). The hostile-reset half — the identical 8-day clamp — is explicitly tested on the poller's sibling implementation: crates/server/src/poller.rs:713 probe_429_rejects_stale_and_bounds_hostile_resets asserts probe_cool_secs_429(None, Some(1.0), None, Some(i64::MAX), now, 300) == MAX_PROBE_COOL_SECS (8*24*3600, poller.rs:280), and poller.rs:284-285 documents it as the same defence pending unification into forward. What remains genuinely unpinned is a 5-line wrapper: Retry-After > window_cool > BURST_COOL_SECS precedence, plus the forward-side clamp constant.

*Production risk.* A compromised or misbehaving egress proxy returning `Retry-After: 999999999` on a 429 would cool a paid subscription for decades if the clamp regressed. With a modest fleet, a handful of such responses removes a meaningful fraction of purchased capacity semi-permanently — and because cooling state survives deploys via export_state/import_state, a restart does not clear it.

*Suggested test.* Unit level. Split the header-reading from the policy: extract `fn cool_secs_from(retry_after: Option<i64>, lim: &Limits, now: i64) -> i64` and have `cool_secs_429` be a thin adapter that pulls the header. Then table-test in crates/forward/src/proxy.rs tests: explicit Retry-After beats a window that says otherwise; absent Retry-After falls through to window_cool; neither present yields exactly BURST_COOL_SECS; `Retry-After: 999999999` and a reset timestamp years in the future both clamp to exactly MAX_COOL_SECS; a negative or non-numeric Retry-After is ignored rather than producing a negative cooldown.

<a id="rust-forward-proxy--tls-emulation-constants-unpinned"></a>
#### `tls-emulation-constants-unpinned` — The TLS ClientHello emulation — the fleet's primary anti-fingerprinting defence — is asserted nowhere

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/nodetls.rs:53`

*Not covered today.* The description of coverage is accurate — the sole test connects over plain http://127.0.0.1 and validates only header name case/order (crates/forward/src/nodetls.rs:78-120) — but the stated production risk overstates how a regression could ship. 'A dependency bump of wreq could change defaults with no signal' is not how this repo builds: the gate runs `cargo test --locked` (deploy/watchdog.sh:334), and wreq is pinned to 6.0.0-rc.29 in Cargo.lock:2700-2702, so a transitive default change cannot arrive without an explicit committed lockfile diff. Additionally, no in-process unit test can validate a JA3/JA4 hash — that requires an external fingerprinting endpoint. The accurate, actionable gap is narrow and cheap: BUN_CIPHERS (nodetls.rs:42-50), the curves string, ALPN=HTTP1 and HDR_ORDER's 22 entries (nodetls.rs:15-38) are not pinned by a snapshot assertion, so a hand edit to those constants would ship silently.

*Production risk.* The whole business depends on pooled subscription traffic being indistinguishable from genuine Claude Code. A silent ALPN change to h2, or a reordered cipher list after a wreq upgrade, changes JA3/JA4 across the entire fleet simultaneously — which is exactly the correlated signal that would get every pooled account banned at once. Recovery means re-acquiring subscriptions, not shipping a fix.

*Suggested test.* Unit level in crates/forward/src/nodetls.rs tests, two parts. (1) A cheap pinning test on the constants: assert `HDR_ORDER.len() == 22` and its exact contents, and assert `BUN_CIPHERS` equals the captured string — a deliberate snapshot, since the whole point is that these must not drift accidentally. (2) Extend the existing socket test to TLS: terminate with a self-signed cert on the listener and parse the raw ClientHello bytes off the wire, asserting no GREASE values in the cipher list, that the first three ciphers are the TLS1.3 suites in order, and that the ALPN extension advertises exactly `http/1.1`.

### 8.5 `rust-billing-meter` — Rust billing/metering (money path)

This domain turns an upstream response into an integer-nanoUSD charge: `metering` holds the effective-dated Anthropic and Codex price tables and the SSE/JSON usage parsers, `forward/meter.rs` tees the response body and settles the reservation, `forward/billing.rs` is the actor that owns the only writer connection to the money authority, and `forward/codex/billing.rs` does the same for the OpenAI-compatible pool. Coverage is inverted relative to risk: the pure price tables and parsers are genuinely well tested (25 passing tests in `metering`, exact per-million rates, the "opus-4-20250514 must not fall into the cheap default" trap, effective-dated Sonnet 5 and Codex schedules), and `proxy.rs:1806 cap_to_balance_enforces_budget` is a real property-style money test — but the two functions that actually move money have almost nothing. `crates/forward/src/meter.rs` (428 lines) has no `#[cfg(test)]` module at all and `TeeMeter` is never constructed outside `proxy.rs:1611`; `crates/forward/src/billing.rs` (1417 lines) has one test that covers one state of the SQLite handoff machine and zero coverage of the PostgreSQL writer that is the production authority. Worse, the untested caller has already silently invalidated tested behaviour: `meter.rs:166` retains only `message_start`/`message_delta`/`error` SSE lines, so the byte-bound output fallback and the web-search counter that `metering` tests assert on a full transcript (lib.rs:926, lib.rs:944) can never fire in production — the filter landed 2026-07-22 (dcd6459), six days after the fallback (b9c681d), and every test stayed green. The only end-to-end money test, `deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh`, asserts row counts rather than amounts, covers only the non-streaming path, and is not run by the watchdog gate (deploy/watchdog.sh:316-344).

**Working well here:**

- **Reservation budget invariant (the strongest money test in the repo)** — `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1806` — `cap_to_balance_enforces_budget` is table-driven over 4 multipliers × 4 balances and asserts three real invariants rather than three literals: hold ≤ balance+$1, worst-case charge ≤ hold, and tightness (if max_tokens was truncated, one more output token must breach balance+$1). It also pins the free-key (mult_bp=0) and i64-overflow edges. A regression in the truncation math would fail this immediately.
- **Price-table traps encoded as tests** — `crates/metering/src/lib.rs:710` — `model_matcher_covers_real_ids` asserts the specific substring traps that cost money — `claude-opus-4-20250514` and bare `claude-opus-4` must bill $75/M, not the $25/M default (a 3× undercharge), and `claude-3-5-haiku-…` must not fall through the `haiku-3-5` branch. These are exactly the regressions a future model-id edit would cause.
- **Fail-safe pricing for unknown models, asserted structurally** — `crates/metering/src/lib.rs:746` — `reserve_prices_cover_any_served_model` does not just check a constant; it loops over every known model and asserts MAX_PRICES ≥ that model's output/input/cache_write_1h rate. Adding a model more expensive than MAX (which would let a charge blow past the hold) fails the test automatically.
- **Codex effective-dated catalog** — `crates/metering/src/codex.rs:229` — `schedule_resolves_the_newest_effective_entry` asserts the exact boundary second (1_999 → launch, 2_000 → later, i64::MAX → later) and a companion test pins the clock-behind-first-epoch case. This makes historical settlements replayable, which is the stated reason the schedule exists.
- **Config drift cannot change money (Codex)** — `crates/forward/src/codex/billing.rs:389` — `pricing_comes_from_the_audited_catalog_not_the_config_copy` deliberately corrupts the config-embedded rate and asserts the catalog rate is still billed. It tests the architectural rule (metering is the single audited source of money) rather than an arithmetic result, so it survives price changes and would catch a refactor that reintroduced config-sourced pricing.
- **Codex usage extraction against a real app-server** — `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs:1047` — `closed_downstream_update_channel_still_drains_authoritative_usage` runs a real subprocess speaking the app-server JSON-RPC protocol (fake_gateway, runner.rs:774) and asserts that a client disconnect still yields the authoritative token counts used for settlement. This is the exact failure mode the Claude path (meter.rs Drop drain) leaves untested.
- **Provider attribution incl. legacy rows (uncommitted work)** — `crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2588` — `spend_is_attributed_to_the_serving_provider` covers the new column on SQLite end-to-end, plus the two backward-compatibility cases that actually break in a blue-green promotion: an empty-string provider coalescing to `anthropic` (lib.rs:2632) and a settlement payload serialized without the field deserializing to `anthropic` (lib.rs:2640).

<a id="rust-billing-meter--sse-retention-kills-byte-fallback"></a>
#### `sse-retention-kills-byte-fallback` — The SSE tee drops the very events the truncation-undercharge protections read, and no test covers the pair

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/meter.rs:166`

*Not covered today.* The tee's SSE retention filter (crates/forward/src/meter.rs:166-174) keeps only message_start/message_delta/error lines, so `metering::usage_from_sse`'s truncation fallback (`delta_bytes`, crates/metering/src/lib.rs:502-512 + :545-547) is unreachable from production: an upstream stream that dies before the final message_delta settles output at whatever message_start reported. No test feeds anything through TeeMeter. The web-search half of the claim is overstated: `web_search_requests` also arrives on message_start (crates/metering/src/lib.rs:521-523) and on the final message_delta (`usage.server_tool_use`, :532-538), both of which ARE retained, so the content_block_start counter (:492-501) is only load-bearing in the same truncated-stream case, not 'whenever the count arrives only on content_block_start'.

*Production risk.* A stream that ends before the final `message_delta` — upstream/egress-proxy reset mid-generation, an `error` event after HTTP 200 (which the code explicitly expects, meter.rs:196), or the 1 MiB SSE_USAGE_CAP filling — settles output at whatever `message_start` reported, typically 1 token, while the customer already received the whole generation byte-for-byte. Web-search calls ($0.01 each, reserved for via `web_buf` in proxy.rs:948) are billed 0 whenever the count arrives only on `content_block_start`. Both are pure revenue loss on the highest-volume path, and the existing tests actively assert the opposite behaviour.

*Suggested test.* Unit test in a new `#[cfg(test)]` module in crates/forward/src/meter.rs: drive `TeeMeter` (via `futures_util::stream::iter`) with an SSE transcript split across chunk boundaries mid-line — message_start, a web_search content_block_start, N content_block_delta, then EOF with no final message_delta — against a stub billing sink (extract a small trait or assert on an mpsc receiver), and assert the settled charge covers the delivered output and the web-search request. Add a second case where message_delta does arrive, asserting the authoritative count wins over the byte bound.

<a id="rust-billing-meter--meter-finalize-zero-tests"></a>
#### `meter-finalize-zero-tests` — `TeeMeter::finalize` — the only function that converts a served response into a charge — has no test

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/meter.rs:178`

*Not covered today.* `TeeMeter::finalize` (crates/forward/src/meter.rs:178-355) has zero unit tests and zero coverage in the automated deploy gate. It is not wholly unexercised: deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh drives two real non-SSE metered requests through it and asserts the observable side effects of :188 (end_stream), :189-191 (release_capacity) and :328 (settle_detached) — settled reservations, charge ledger rows, zero active capacity leases, zero pool inflight, reserved_nano back to 0. What is genuinely untested anywhere is every *decision* inside finalize: the response-model-vs-request-model price basis (:226-229), the US-geo 1.1x (:246-250), `incomplete_non_sse → 0` (:264-269), the clamp to hold+OVERDRAFT_NANO (:283-295), the `charge_i64 > 0 && real > 0` usage_event gate (:298), and the whole SSE branch (:192-208).

*Production risk.* Every metered Anthropic request settles here. A regression in the clamp charges up to hold+$1 on a zero-usage 200 (count_tokens-shaped traffic), an inverted `is_zero` check refunds real usage, a missing `end_stream` leaks pool concurrency until restart, and dropping the usage_event blanks the customer-facing per-bucket dashboard while money still moves. deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:165-176 touches this path once but asserts only counts (`2:2:2:0:0:0`), never an amount, only non-SSE, and is not run by the watchdog gate (deploy/watchdog.sh:316-344).

*Suggested test.* Table-driven unit tests in crates/forward/src/meter.rs over a fake billing sink and a real `pool::Pool`, one row per outcome: 200 without usage (charge 0, hold released, no usage_event), normal SSE, normal non-SSE, usage priced above the hold (assert exactly hold+$1), truncated non-SSE JSON (assert the documented policy explicitly), and response-model-overrides-request-model. Each row must also assert `end_stream` and `release_capacity` fire exactly once.

<a id="rust-billing-meter--pg-billing-writer-untested"></a>
#### `pg-billing-writer-untested` — The PostgreSQL billing writer — the production authority path — has zero tests, including a duplicated handoff state machine and a retry wrapper that can re-run non-idempotent writes

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/billing.rs:589`

*Not covered today.* The PostgreSQL writer thread `start_postgres` (crates/forward/src/billing.rs:589-884) — including its second copy of the reserve-handoff CAS machine (:630-659) and `run_pg_with_retry` (:149-195) — has zero tests, and nothing in the file is gated on CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL, so the watchdog's Rust phase covers none of it. The retry-double-credit sub-claim is refuted for real money in: `WriteCmd::Topup` reaches the writer from exactly one production caller, crates/server/src/admin.rs:391, and any positive amount is rejected at admin.rs:384-389 unless it carries a provider-qualified `<provider>:<txid>` ref (admin.rs:330-339). The reachable variant is narrower — a retried *referenceless negative* adjustment (amount ≤ 0, where the ref is optional) would double-debit. The CLI top-up path does not use the retry wrapper at all.

*Production risk.* This code decides whether a customer's hold is refunded when a request is cancelled between the DB commit and the caller taking ownership. A divergence between the two copies leaks holds (customer money frozen) or double-refunds (revenue lost). Separately, `run_pg_with_retry` re-invokes its closure after a transient failure: `reserve`/`settle` are idempotent by `request_id`, but `Topup` (:728) passes `reference` straight through, and `pg.account_topup` only deduplicates when a reference is present (registry/src/pg.rs:1154) — a retried referenceless top-up double-credits real money.

*Suggested test.* Add a `CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL`-gated `#[tokio::test]` in crates/forward/src/billing.rs that starts `start_postgres` against the disposable engine DB and drives: reserve→claim, reserve with the receiver dropped (assert refund), reserve with the guard dropped pre-commit, and a settle above hold. Separately, unit-test `run_pg_with_retry` with an injectable operation that fails transiently once, asserting it is not retried for operations lacking an idempotency reference.

<a id="rust-billing-meter--drop-drain-can-lose-the-settlement"></a>
#### `drop-drain-can-lose-the-settlement` — Client-disconnect drain is a spawned task whose loss silently converts to a full-hold charge later

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/meter.rs:395`

*Not covered today.* Neither branch of `impl Drop for TeeMeter` (crates/forward/src/meter.rs:385-427) has a test — no test anywhere constructs or drops a TeeMeter. The framing of the risk is off, though: a lost drain task and a killed in-flight stream produce the same durable state (reservation left in `delivering` until the lease expires), and the resulting policy — charge the approved hold as expired-delivery rather than refund — is deliberate and IS pinned at the authority layer, so the untested surface is the forward-side drain/accumulator handoff, not the money outcome.

*Production risk.* Blue-green cutovers routinely drop in-flight streams. A lost drain leaves the reservation in `delivering` until the 3600 s lease expires, at which point `reconcile_expired` charges the FULL hold as expired-delivery — and `cap_to_balance` sizes that hold to nearly the customer's whole balance for a large `max_tokens`. That is a customer overcharge produced by an engine restart, plus a leaked pool concurrency slot per orphaned stream.

*Suggested test.* tokio test: build a `TeeMeter` over a stream that yields one chunk then stays pending, drop it inside a runtime, then shut the runtime down with `shutdown_timeout(0)`; assert the settle command reached the writer channel or that a synchronous fallback settle ran. Add a second test asserting `finalize` runs exactly once when both the poll path and the drop path are reachable.

<a id="rust-billing-meter--reserve-vs-settle-price-basis"></a>
#### `reserve-vs-settle-price-basis` — Fast-Mode and US-geo premiums are reserved from the request but charged from the response, and every function on both sides is untested

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/metering/src/lib.rs:172`

*Not covered today.* Partially covered. The reserve-vs-settle price-basis invariant IS pinned for the model dimension (reserve prices == settle prices for every recognized id, and MAX ≥ every real tariff for unrecognized ids) and for the fast dimension when the requested flag matches the served speed. What is untested is (a) the mismatch case — a response reporting speed=fast or inference_geo=us that the client did not request, where the settle basis exceeds the reserve basis and the excess above hold+$1 is written off at meter.rs:295 — and (b) the four helpers on the settle side, which have zero test references. The `basis_points <= 0` collapse in `premium_prices_ceil` is dead-code-theoretical: the sole caller passes the constant 11_000.

*Production risk.* If a response reports `speed: fast` or `inference_geo: us` that the client did not request, the real cost is 2-3× (fast tariff) or 1.1× (geo) the reserved hold, and everything above hold+$1 is silently written off by the clamp at meter.rs:295. A rounding error in the opposite direction overcharges, and nothing pins reserve ≥ settle for these two dimensions. `premium_prices_ceil` also collapses every price to 0 for `basis_points ≤ 0` (`bp.max(0)`), which would produce a zero hold if it ever gained a second caller.

*Suggested test.* Property test in crates/metering/src/lib.rs asserting, for every recognized model and both flag values, that the reserve-side per-token rates (`premium_prices_ceil(model_prices_reserve_for_speed_at(m, t, fast), 11_000)`) are ≥ the settle-side effective rates (`model_prices_for_speed_at(m, t, fast)` after `apply_token_multiplier(11_000)`), plus a meter-level test that a response reporting fast/us produces the premium charge and one reporting neither does not.

<a id="rust-billing-meter--stream-lease-heartbeat-untested"></a>
#### `stream-lease-heartbeat-untested` — Stream lease heartbeat has no test and its failure branch silently stops renewing while the stream keeps running

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/meter.rs:107`

*Not covered today.* Confirmed uncovered on the forward side: nothing tests the 300s renewal cadence, the abort on finalize, the both-ids-None case, the fail-and-stop-renewing branch, or the SQLite arms that ignore the capacity lease id and fabricate a lease. The severity is narrower than stated because the trigger requires a stream living past 300s AND a writer failure outlasting run_pg_with_retry's deadline, and both downstream consequences the claim describes are already pinned at the authority layer.

*Production risk.* A long stream whose renewal failed keeps delivering while its reservation lease expires; `reconcile_expired` then charges the full hold as expired-delivery, and the tee's later real settle for a different actual is rejected as an idempotency mismatch (registry/src/pg.rs:2331 shows a differing actual returns Err). Net result: the customer pays the reserved ceiling instead of actual usage, with no test anywhere asserting otherwise.

*Suggested test.* tokio test with `time::pause()` asserting `renew_stream_leases` is called at the expected cadence with both ids while the stream is live, that `finalize` aborts the heartbeat exactly once, and that a renewal returning `Ok(false)` produces the documented outcome (terminate the response or keep retrying) rather than a silent stop.

<a id="rust-billing-meter--codex-settle-untested"></a>
#### `codex-settle-untested` — Codex settlement and admission are untested; a failed turn refunds 100% after provider tokens were consumed

**MEDIUM** · verdict `UNVERIFIED` · `crates/forward/src/codex/billing.rs:122`

> Same defect as [`codex-settle-untested`](#rust-codex--codex-settle-untested) in `rust-codex`, found independently from another file. That entry carries the fuller treatment and the severity to triage at.

*Not covered today.* The 5 tests in this file all target `price_usage`/`reserve_cost`. `CodexAdmission::settle` — the clamp to `hold + OVERDRAFT_NANO` (:129-130), the `priced.real_nano > 0` gate on the usage_event (:131), the mapping of Codex cache-write tokens into the `cache_write_1h` column (:139), and `reservation.guard.disarm()` ordering after `settle_detached` (:155-164) — has no test, and neither does `begin_admission` (:175-223: authority-ready gate, concurrency permit, `available_nano > 0` LowBalance branch, key limiter). A turn that errors or times out returns `Err` before `settle` (runner.rs:545 and :560), so the `HoldGuard` refunds the entire hold even when authoritative usage had already accumulated.

*Production risk.* A disarm/clamp regression either double-refunds (guard fires after a successful settle) or charges the hold twice; the token-bucket mapping decides what the customer sees in usage_events. The error path gives away every timed-out or interrupted Codex turn for free even though the ChatGPT subscription quota was consumed — a direct margin leak on the newest revenue surface.

*Suggested test.* Unit tests over a fake billing sink asserting `settle` emits exactly one `settle_detached` with the clamped charge, writes a usage_event only when real_nano > 0, disarms the guard, and is a no-op for an admin (no reservation). Add a runner-level test that an interrupted turn carrying authoritative usage still reaches `settle` rather than the blanket refund.

<a id="rust-billing-meter--delivery-marker-failure-charges-full-hold"></a>
#### `delivery-marker-failure-charges-full-hold` — A fenced delivery marker charges the customer the entire hold for a response they never receive, with no test

**MEDIUM** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1407`

> Same defect as [`delivery-marker-charges-full-hold`](#rust-forward-proxy--delivery-marker-charges-full-hold) in `rust-forward-proxy`, found independently from another file. That entry carries the fuller treatment and the severity to triage at.

*Not covered today.* When `mark_delivering` does not return `Ok(true)`, the code settles `actual = hold` with reference `delivery-marker-failed`, disarms the HoldGuard and returns a 503-shaped Overloaded to the client (proxy.rs:1407-1422). This is the only place in the money path that deliberately charges the full reservation, and no test — Rust, shell or e2e — exercises it. The Codex analogue (codex/billing.rs:108-120) instead returns `Unavailable` and lets the guard refund, so the two providers behave oppositely and nothing pins either.

*Production risk.* During a fencing event or authority blip, every in-flight metered request bills the full hold — sized by `cap_to_balance` to nearly the account balance — while the client receives an error and zero tokens. A customer can be drained by an infrastructure fault, and the divergence from the Codex path means one of the two policies is unintentional.
