*Suggested test.* Test the settlement decision at the seam: extract the marker-failure branch into a small pure function (inputs: marker result, hold) or drive `AsyncBilling` with a writer stub that fails `MarkDelivering`, and assert the resulting settle amount matches the documented policy for both providers; add a note-level assertion that Claude and Codex agree.

*Verified absent by.* Verified the code at crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1407-1423: `!matches!(mark_delivering(...), Ok(true))` → `settle_detached(request_id, account_id, key, *hold, *hold, Some("delivery-marker-failed"), None)` → `hold_guard.disarm()` → `local_err(LocalErr::Overloaded, Some(2))`, i.e. actual == hold with no usage event. Searched the entire repo for any test touching it: `grep -rn 'delivery-marker-failed'` (crates/, deploy/, apps/, packages/, excluding node_modules/target/dist) returns exactly one hit, crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1416. `grep -rn mark_delivering crates/` shows the only tested call sites are the registry primitives (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2313, :2359, :2440; crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2364, :2400, :2424) — those assert the marker returns true, never the forward-side false branch. proxy.rs's own test module (crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1619-1939) contains 12 tests, none of which enter `forward_once`. The divergence with Codex is real: crates/forward/src/codex/billing.rs:117-119 maps both `Ok(false)` and `Err(_)` to AdmissionError::Unavailable and lets the HoldGuard refund. The hold-size premise holds: crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1806-1854 pins hold ≤ balance+$1, so a large max_tokens does reserve nearly the whole balance.

<a id="rust-billing-meter--provider-attribution-pg-side"></a>
#### `provider-attribution-pg-side` — New provider attribution is tested only on SQLite; the PostgreSQL outbox index shift that carries account and hold is not

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:530`

*Not covered today.* Three factual corrections. (1) The provider work is not uncommitted — the tree is clean on master and it landed in 529d2f3. (2) The columns are not uniformly interchangeable: index 24 (`hold`) is i64 while 20-23 and 25 are String, and rust-postgres `row.get` panics on a type mismatch, so a shift across that boundary fails loudly rather than settling the wrong amount. (3) The settlement arithmetic that reads account_id (22) and hold (24) IS asserted with exact balances by a test that DOES run in the deploy gate. The residual, genuine gap is narrow: the `provider` value itself (index 20) is never asserted on the PostgreSQL side, `pg.spend_by_provider` has no test, and the forward reader arms have none.

*Production risk.* `process_outbox_request` is the settlement transaction: a wrong index there settles against the wrong account id or the wrong hold, i.e. charges the wrong customer or the wrong amount, and local `cargo test` stays green because the only covering test skips itself.

*Suggested test.* Extend the DSN-gated pg test to settle one `anthropic` and one `openai` usage event through `process_outbox_request` and assert (a) the ledger charge lands on the expected account with the expected hold release, and (b) `spend_by_provider` returns both rows with exact `requests`/`charge_nano`/`real_nano` plus the empty-string→anthropic coalesce, mirroring registry/src/lib.rs:2588.

### 8.6 `rust-codex` — Rust forward/codex subsystem (Codex provider)

The Codex subsystem exposes an OpenAI-shaped `/v1/responses`, `/v1/chat/completions` and `/v1/models` surface backed by a pool of pinned `codex app-server` child processes, each bound to one ChatGPT-authenticated `CODEX_HOME`, and it debits the same integer-nanoUSD engine balance as the Claude path. The in-flight (uncommitted) change is a substantial hardening refactor: single child -> multi-home pool with per-home cooling/auth quarantine/window-headroom admission, a background health sweep, and pricing moved from a config-embedded copy to the effective-dated `metering` catalog. Test coverage is sharply bimodal and the split is almost exactly wrong for a money system. The transport and rotation layer (`runner.rs`, 13 tests) is genuinely excellent — it drives a real fake app-server subprocess and pins pool rotation, headroom admission, transport-vs-account blame, and the no-retry-after-first-byte invariant; the new pool logic shipped with tests. Everything money-adjacent above it is not: `CodexAdmission::settle` — the single function that turns provider usage into a customer charge — has zero tests, and the two ~600-line streaming translators (`stream_responses`, `stream_chat`) that own settle-on-disconnect ordering, SSE sequencing and the OpenAI wire contract have no test that ever executes them. The binary-attestation gate (SHA-256 + version) and the prompt-isolation `--config` hardening flags, both explicit CLAUDE.md invariants, have no negative test at all.

**Working well here:**

- **Real-subprocess transport harness instead of mocked JSON-RPC** — `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs:783` — The fake app-server is an actual `/bin/sh` child that the production `CodexProcess::spawn` path launches, digest-verifies and version-verifies for real (the test computes the script's own SHA-256 at runner.rs:869). Per-home behaviour is selected by a `mode` file inside each CODEX_HOME rather than by a second binary, so the pool tests exercise one attested binary across many homes exactly as production does. This catches regressions no mocked-channel test could: framing, ordering, spawn, per-home CODEX_HOME binding, and process death.
- **The multi-home pool refactor shipped with its own behavioural tests** — `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs:1193-1295` — Six tests were added alongside the in-flight rotation change and each pins a distinct policy with a distinguishable failure: a limited home rotates and stays cooling while the healthy one serves (1193), all-limited returns one retryable wait rather than an account error (1211), a 97% home is refused under the 95% headroom (1230), a dead child is retried once elsewhere (1248), a transport fault cools ~10s and does NOT clear auth_ok (1260), and a failure after the first delta is never retried on a second home (1277). They assert on per-home request logs, so 'which home actually served it' is proven rather than inferred.
- **Exhaustive, adversarial error-leak table for the public surface** — `crates/forward/src/codex/api.rs:2154` — `public_errors_never_leak_internal_architecture` builds every reachable public error (including ones carrying attacker-visible upstream text like `Rpc{message:"sensitive upstream diagnostic"}` and `Spawn("...\/srv\/claude-api\/data\/codex\/home")`) and asserts 18 forbidden substrings never appear. Its sibling at api.rs:2191 pins the status<->type pairing so an internal fault can never surface as a non-retryable client error. This is the correct twin of the Claude-path `local_err_never_leaks_internal_architecture` (proxy.rs:1870) and would catch a real leak regression.
- **Cancellation and disconnect semantics proven at the transport layer** — `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs:1157` — `dropping_an_inflight_turn_sends_interrupt` aborts the task mid-stream and then waits for `turn/interrupt` to actually arrive in the child's request log, proving the Drop-based `TurnInterruptGuard` works across a real await point. `closed_downstream_update_channel_still_drains_authoritative_usage` (runner.rs:1047) proves the turn keeps draining to authoritative usage after the public channel is gone — the invariant that stops a client escaping settlement by hanging up.
- **DoS bound on the JSON-RPC reader tested at the boundary, not after allocation** — `crates/forward/src/codex/process.rs:885` — `jsonrpc_line_reader_enforces_the_cap_before_growing` feeds 32 MiB + 1 of newline-free bytes and asserts both the error and that the accumulator never exceeded the cap. That second assertion is the one that matters: a naive `read_until` would OOM the engine on a hostile or wedged child, and the test would fail if someone reverted to it.
- **Tenant isolation and at-rest binding of customer transcripts** — `crates/forward/src/codex/history.rs:339` — `local_history_is_tenant_bound` proves account B cannot read account A's `previous_response_id` items, and `encrypted_envelope_is_bound_to_response_id` (history.rs:353) proves the AEAD uses the response id as AAD so a ciphertext cannot be replayed under a different id, plus asserts the plaintext does not appear in the ciphertext. Two tests, both non-trivial security properties.
- **Pricing authority is pinned against config drift** — `crates/forward/src/codex/billing.rs:389` — `pricing_comes_from_the_audited_catalog_not_the_config_copy` deliberately corrupts the config-embedded rate and asserts the audited `metering` catalog still wins, and its sibling at billing.rs:406 asserts a model the catalog stopped advertising keeps the rate it was admitted with instead of silently repricing. These pin the exact invariant the in-flight pricing refactor introduced.

<a id="rust-codex--codex-settle-untested"></a>
#### `codex-settle-untested` — `CodexAdmission::settle` — the only function that charges a Codex customer — has zero tests

**CRITICAL** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/codex/billing.rs:122`

*Not covered today.* All 5 tests in codex/billing.rs stop at `price_usage`/`reserve_cost`, i.e. the pure rate arithmetic. Nothing exercises `settle`, which is where priced usage becomes an actual debit: the tenant multiplier application (`apply_multiplier(priced.real_nano, reservation.mult_bp)`, billing.rs:128), the ceiling clamp `hold + OVERDRAFT_NANO` that is the only thing stopping an overcharge (billing.rs:129-130), the i64 saturation of every usage-event field, the `PROVIDER_OPENAI` attribution stamp (billing.rs:134), the `real_nano > 0` gate that decides whether a usage_event is written at all, and the `reservation.guard.disarm()` at billing.rs:164 that must happen exactly once after `settle_detached` or the hold is refunded on top of the charge. `grep -rn 'CodexAdmission' crates/` returns only definition sites, no test.

*Production risk.* Direct money error on every OpenAI-surface request, in either direction and silently. If the ceiling were ever computed from `computed_charge` instead of `hold`, a long-context turn charges past what the customer reserved (overcharge + negative balance). If `disarm()` moved above `settle_detached` or the guard were dropped on an early return, the RAII refund fires after the charge and the request becomes free. If the multiplier were applied twice, every B2C customer is billed at the square of their discount tier. The usage_event nano split also feeds the admin spend breakdown and the commerce tier-consumption ledger, so a wrong split corrupts downstream B2C pricing without ever failing a test.

*Suggested test.* Unit tests in `crates/forward/src/codex/billing.rs` `mod tests`. Refactor the charge computation out of `settle` into a pure `fn settled_charge(model, usage, hold, mult_bp, now) -> (i64, Option<UsageEventInput>)` and table-test it: (a) usage far exceeding the hold clamps to exactly `hold + metering::OVERDRAFT_NANO`; (b) `mult_bp` is applied exactly once (assert against a hand-computed nanoUSD constant, not against `apply_multiplier` re-invoked); (c) zero usage yields charge 0 and `usage_event == None`; (d) the usage_event's `real_nano` equals `input_nano+cached_nano+cache_write_nano+output_nano` and `provider == registry::PROVIDER_OPENAI`; (e) u64::MAX token counts saturate rather than wrap. Separately add a test that a `CodexAdmission` dropped without `settle` produces exactly one refund settle_detached(hold, 0) against a fake AsyncBilling recorder.

*Verified absent by.* Verified line-by-line. `CodexAdmission::settle` is at crates/forward/src/codex/billing.rs:122; multiplier at :128, ceiling `hold+OVERDRAFT_NANO` at :129-130, `real_nano > 0` usage-event gate at :131, PROVIDER_OPENAI stamp at :134, `guard.disarm()` at :164. The only test mod in the file is crates/forward/src/codex/billing.rs:312-421 and its 5 tests (:337 prices_non_cached_cached_and_cache_write_without_double_counting, :363 long_context_multiplier_applies..., :382 reserve_covers_full_output_and_cache_write_rate, :388 pricing_comes_from_the_audited_catalog_not_the_config_copy, :405 a_model_outside_the_catalog_keeps_the_rate_it_was_admitted_with) all stop at price_usage/reserve_cost. Searched: `rg -n 'CodexAdmission' crates/ apps/ packages/` -> only billing.rs:31,37,44,55,99,107,179,217, api.rs:1253, chat.rs:792 (no test); `rg -ln 'settle_detached' crates/` -> billing.rs, meter.rs, proxy.rs, billing.rs only, no test file; crates/forward has no tests/ dir; no *.test.ts or deploy/*.test.sh touches it. Two sub-risks in the claim are overstated, though the coverage gap itself is real: (a) the 'refund fires on top of the charge' scenario is structurally prevented — the settlement outbox is first-write-wins per request_id (`ON CONFLICT(request_id) DO NOTHING`, crates/registry/src/pg.rs:444), so a stray HoldGuard refund after a charge is a no-op; (b) the downstream provider split/attribution consumer IS tested at crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2587 `spend_is_attributed_to_the_serving_provider`. What remains genuinely unpinned is the money arithmetic itself (multiplier application, the hold+$1 ceiling clamp, the i64 saturations, the real_nano>0 gate) on the only charge path of the whole OpenAI surface — critical stands.

<a id="rust-codex--stream-settle-on-disconnect"></a>
#### `stream-settle-on-disconnect` — Settle-after-client-disconnect ordering in both streaming adapters is unpinned

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/codex/api.rs:1658`

*Not covered today.* No test pins the settle-before-early-return ordering in either streaming adapter (api.rs:1658-1659, chat.rs:901-902); a future reorder would silently stop billing every client-disconnect turn while still burning pool quota, and nothing in the suite would fail.

*Production risk.* Free inference at scale. A one-line reordering — moving the `if downstream_closed { return; }` above the `settle` call, which reads like an obvious short-circuit optimisation — makes every request where the client hangs up mid-stream cost the pool real subscription quota and bill the customer nothing; the reserved hold is then refunded by the RAII HoldGuard. It is trivially exploitable by any client that aborts after the first token, and nothing in the test suite, the type system or the metrics would flag it.

*Suggested test.* Integration-level test in `crates/forward/src/codex/api.rs` and `chat.rs` `mod tests`, reusing the runner's `fake_gateway("text")` harness. Build a real `AppState` with a recording fake `AsyncBilling`, call `stream_responses`/`stream_chat`, drop the response body receiver after the first frame, then assert the recorder observed exactly one `settle_detached` with a non-zero charge (not a hold refund). Add the mirror case: a fully consumed stream also settles exactly once.

<a id="rust-codex--attestation-negative-path"></a>
#### `attestation-negative-path` — Binary SHA-256 and version attestation is never tested for refusal

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/codex/process.rs:529`

*Not covered today.* `verify_binary_digest` (process.rs:529) and `verify_version` (process.rs:558) run before every child spawn and are the CLAUDE.md invariant 'Проверять exact binary SHA-256/version до запуска'. `grep -rn 'DigestMismatch\|VersionMismatch' crates/` finds only the definition, the Display impl, the diagnostic_class arm, the raise sites, the cooling arm in mod.rs:473-474, and their appearance as fixture values in the api.rs error-leak table — never as an assertion that a mismatched binary is refused. Every runner test computes the correct digest and correct version, so only the happy path is executed. Equally, `child_command` (process.rs:586) `env_clear()`s and passes ten `--config include_*=false` / `mcp_servers={}` isolation flags that implement CLAUDE.md Codex invariants 2 and 3, and the fake app-server logs only stdin JSON-RPC frames (runner.rs:797) — it never records argv or environment, so none of those flags or the env isolation is observable by any test.

*Production risk.* Supply-chain and prompt-isolation. A regression that inverts the digest comparison, or short-circuits it when the file is unreadable, launches an unattested binary with live ChatGPT subscription credentials in its CODEX_HOME. A silently dropped `--config include_environment_context=false` or `project_doc_max_bytes=0` puts host paths, environment context or project docs into the model-visible prompt of a paying customer's request; a dropped `env_clear` leaks the engine's own environment (which contains CONTROL_KEY-adjacent secrets) into the child. All of these ship green today.

*Suggested test.* Unit tests in `crates/forward/src/codex/process.rs` `mod tests`: write a temp file, assert `verify_binary_digest` returns `DigestMismatch{expected, actual}` when the configured hash differs by one character, and `verify_version` returns `VersionMismatch` for a script printing a different version string. Separately, extend the runner fake binary (runner.rs:783) to append `argv=$*` and a sorted `env` dump to its log on startup, then add `child_is_launched_with_isolation_flags_and_no_inherited_environment` asserting all ten `--config` values are present and that the child's environment is exactly {CODEX_HOME, PATH, NO_COLOR, TERM} plus configured proxy vars.

*Verified absent by.* `verify_binary_digest` at crates/forward/src/codex/process.rs:529 (raise at :550), `verify_version` at :558 (raise at :578), `child_command` at :586 with `env_clear()` at :589 and the ten `--config` isolation flags at :602-611. Searched `rg -n 'DigestMismatch|VersionMismatch' crates/ --glob '!target'` -> definition process.rs:24-25, Display :44/:50, diagnostic_class :84-85, raise sites :550/:578, cooling arm mod.rs:473-474, and fixture values in the error-envelope table crates/forward/src/codex/api.rs:2120-2127 — never an assertion that a mismatch is refused. Searched `rg -n 'include_environment_context|project_doc_max_bytes|env_clear' . --glob '!target'` -> only process.rs:563,589,605,610, tools/codex-app-server/probe-live.py:93,98 (a manual live probe, not a test), and crates/forward/CLAUDE.md:141. The fake app-server script (crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs:781-869) appends only stdin JSON-RPC lines to its log and never records "$@" or the environment, so argv/env are unobservable to any test. The happy path is genuinely exercised — runner.rs:870-873 writes the script, computes its real Sha256 into `binary_sha256` and pins `expected_version: "codex-cli test"` matching the script's `--version` output — which makes the missing negative-path assertion cheap to add and its absence the whole gap. No deploy/*.test.sh or e2e script covers it either (deploy/watchdog.sh:535-586 checks metrics and the error envelope only).

<a id="rust-codex--chat-response-direction-untested"></a>
#### `chat-response-direction-untested` — The entire response direction of the Chat Completions adapter is untested

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/codex/chat.rs:789`

*Not covered today.* All 5 tests in chat.rs (1069-1150) test `parse_chat_request`/`translate_messages`/`translate_response_format` — the request direction only. `stream_chat` (chat.rs:789), `completed_chat` (chat.rs:703), `chat_output` (chat.rs:739) and `chat_usage` (chat.rs:771) have no test references anywhere in the repo. That leaves unverified: the `finish_reason` selection `stop` vs `tool_calls` (chat.rs:710, 896), `content: null` when empty vs a string (chat.rs:724), the tool-call chunk `index` sequencing and `emitted_tools` dedup (chat.rs:836-856), the `include_usage` trailing usage chunk with an empty `choices` array (chat.rs:919-936), and the terminal `data: [DONE]\n\n` sentinel (chat.rs:937).

*Production risk.* Silent contract breakage for every OpenAI SDK client. A missing or misplaced `[DONE]` sentinel makes the official `openai` Python/Node clients hang until their own timeout on every streamed completion. A `finish_reason` of `stop` when tool calls were emitted makes agent frameworks drop the tool call and produce a wrong answer; duplicated or non-monotonic tool-call `index` values corrupt argument reassembly in every streaming tool client. None of this is caught by build, typecheck or the current gate, and the customer-visible failure looks like 'the API is broken' rather than like a specific regression.

*Suggested test.* Tests in `crates/forward/src/codex/chat.rs` `mod tests`. Pure ones first: `completed_chat`/`chat_output` over a `CodexTurnResult` containing (a) message only -> finish_reason `stop`, content String; (b) function_call only -> finish_reason `tool_calls`, content Null; (c) both. Then a stream test using the runner's `fake_gateway("tool")`/`("text")` harness plus a fake billing recorder: collect all `Bytes` frames, parse each `data: ` line, and assert the frame sequence is [role chunk, content/tool chunks, finish_reason chunk, optional usage chunk, `[DONE]`], that every tool_call `index` is unique and ascending, and that the usage chunk appears only when `stream_options.include_usage` was set.

*Verified absent by.* Verified: the only test mod in crates/forward/src/codex/chat.rs holds 5 tests at :1069, :1088, :1103, :1121, :1141, all request-direction (parse_chat_request / translate_messages / translate_response_format). Response-direction functions `completed_chat` (chat.rs:703), `chat_output` (chat.rs:739), `chat_usage` (chat.rs:771) and `stream_chat` (chat.rs:789) have zero test references — `rg -n 'stream_chat' crates/` returns only chat.rs:97 (call site) and :789 (definition). The specific behaviours cited exist and are unasserted: finish_reason selection at chat.rs:710 and :896, emitted_tools dedup at :841, include_usage trailing chunk at :919, `data: [DONE]\n\n` at :937 and :1005. No cross-layer coverage: `rg -ln 'chat/completions' . --glob '!target'` returns only deploy/watchdog.sh, crates/server/src/http.rs, docs, ARCHITECTURE.md — and the watchdog's only OpenAI-surface probe (deploy/watchdog.sh:576-583) posts to /v1/responses and asserts an error envelope, never touching chat completions or any streamed frame.

<a id="rust-codex--turn-error-classification"></a>
#### `turn-error-classification` — `classify_turn_error` blame mapping is only covered for one of its six branches

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs:582`

*Not covered today.* Five of classify_turn_error's six branches (object-key form, 429/400/401/403 httpStatusCode fallbacks, contextWindowExceeded, cyberPolicy, sessionBudgetExceeded, None fall-through) are unexercised, and no test drives a BadRequest through note_turn_error; the real fragility is that the kind is read as the alphabetically-first key of a BTreeMap, so a nested or multi-key error payload silently falls through to the httpStatusCode fallback or to Protocol.

*Production risk.* Pool drain and misrouted rotation. `info.as_object().keys().next()` takes the first key of a JSON object in serde_json's preserved insertion order; if app-server reorders or nests its error payload, a client 400 can classify as `unauthorized` and quarantine a fully healthy ChatGPT subscription for 15 minutes per malformed request — a handful of bad requests takes the whole home pool out of rotation and returns 503 to every paying customer. The inverse (a real usage limit classified as BadRequest) returns a non-retryable 400 to clients instead of a 429 with a reset, and leaves an exhausted home in rotation to fail every subsequent turn.

*Suggested test.* Pure unit tests in `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs` `mod tests` over a table of `codexErrorInfo` shapes: `"contextWindowExceeded"`, `{"usageLimitExceeded":{"httpStatusCode":429,"resetsAt":...}}`, `{"someUnknownKind":{"httpStatusCode":401}}`, `{"x":{"httpStatusCode":400}}`, `"cyberPolicy"`, and `null` — asserting the exact ProcessError variant. Then add a `bad_request` mode to the fake app-server and a pool test asserting a client-fault turn leaves `homes[0].cooling_until == 0` and `auth_ok == true` (the anti-drain invariant), and is not retried on a second home.

<a id="rust-codex--begin-admission-untested"></a>
#### `begin-admission-untested` — `begin_admission` — the authz and balance gate for the whole OpenAI surface — has no test

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/codex/billing.rs:175`

*Not covered today.* The codex-specific admission composition (authority_ready -> concurrency permit -> authorize -> tenant-scope derivation -> available_nano>0 -> key limiter) in front of /v1/responses and /v1/chat/completions has no test, and nothing pins the auth-before-body-buffering ordering at api.rs:214/218.

*Production risk.* Auth/billing bypass or unbounded unauthenticated work. If the tenant-scope derivation regressed so `Authz::Admin`'s affinity scope collided with a metered `account_id`, one customer's `previous_response_id` history becomes readable by another (history.rs scoping is keyed on exactly this string). If the `available_nano > 0` branch were relaxed, zero- or negative-balance accounts get free inference on the Codex pool. If `begin_admission` moved after body buffering, an unauthenticated attacker can make the engine buffer and JSON-parse 8 MiB per connection.

*Suggested test.* Tests in `crates/forward/src/codex/billing.rs` `mod tests` against a constructed `AppState`: assert Unauthorized when no credential is present and `auth_failures` is incremented; assert `Authz::Metered` with `available_nano == 0` yields `AdmissionError::LowBalance` before any process is touched; assert the tenant_scope returned for a metered key equals the `account_id` and never equals an admin affinity scope; assert exhausting `app.concurrency` yields `Busy` and bumps `breaker_rejects`. Add one handler-level test posting an 8 MiB body with no credential and asserting a 401 is returned without the body being read.

<a id="rust-codex--stream-responses-sse-contract"></a>
#### `stream-responses-sse-contract` — The 570-line Responses SSE state machine has no end-to-end test

**MEDIUM** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/codex/api.rs:1250`

*Not covered today.* Beyond the settlement ordering already noted, `stream_responses` owns the whole public event contract: monotonic `sequence_number` across ~15 event types, `output_index` allocation shared between text and reasoning items (api.rs:1312, 1334, 1417), `emitted_non_messages` dedup keyed on `output_item_key` (api.rs:1533, 1577, 1798), the authoritative-vs-accumulated item reconciliation at api.rs:1666-1685 and 1762-1780, and the zero-delta and backfill lifecycle passes (api.rs:1739, 1791). Only two leaf helpers are tested (`reasoning_completion_events_use_authoritative_summary_text`, api.rs:2449; `sse_send_stops_immediately_after_downstream_disconnect`, api.rs:2486). Nothing asserts the assembled stream.

*Production risk.* Protocol breakage for every streaming Responses client. Duplicated output items (a dedup-key regression) make an SDK render the same tool call twice — in an agent loop that means the customer's function executes twice. A non-monotonic or reused `sequence_number`, or an `output_item.done` for an index that never got `output_item.added`, makes strict SDK stream parsers raise. Because the non-streaming path is separately assembled by `build_completed_response`, the two can drift apart with no test comparing them.

*Suggested test.* Test in `crates/forward/src/codex/api.rs` `mod tests` using the runner's `fake_gateway("reasoning")` and `("parallel_tool")` modes: drive `stream_responses`, collect every SSE frame, and assert (a) `sequence_number` is strictly increasing with no gaps, (b) each `output_index` receives exactly one `.added` and one `.done`, (c) no `call_id` appears in two `function_call` output items, (d) the `response.completed` payload's `output` array is deep-equal to `build_completed_response` for the same `CodexTurnResult` — the property that keeps streaming and non-streaming honest.

*Verified absent by.* `stream_responses` at crates/forward/src/codex/api.rs:1250; emitted_non_messages dedup at :1313/:1533/:1577/:1798, output_item_key at :1532/:1576/:1797, ~30 `sequence_number` emission sites between :1275 and :2004. Only two tests touch any of it, and both are leaf-level: `reasoning_completion_events_use_authoritative_summary_text` at api.rs:2537 (asserts 5 frames from three emit_* helpers, and does not assert the sequence_number values) and `sse_send_stops_immediately_after_downstream_disconnect` at api.rs:2574 (one send_sse call). Note the auditor's line numbers for these two tests are wrong (2449/2486); the correct ones are 2537 and 2574 — the claim's substance is unaffected. Nothing assembles or asserts a full stream: searched `rg -n 'stream_responses' crates/` (definition + api.rs:260 call site only), `rg -ln 'v1/responses' . --glob '!target'`, and deploy/watchdog.sh:576-583, whose /v1/responses probe deliberately sends an invalid `temperature` so it can never start a turn and only checks the error envelope shape.

<a id="rust-codex--history-availability-vs-notfound"></a>
#### `history-availability-vs-notfound` — History TTL, eviction and the Unavailable-vs-NotFound distinction are untested

**MEDIUM** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/codex/history.rs:160`

*Not covered today.* The 2 history tests cover tenant binding and AEAD only. Untested: TTL expiry in `local_get` (history.rs:231-237), `local_cap` eviction by minimum `expires_at` (history.rs:206-214) — which, because every entry gets an identical second-granularity expiry, evicts an essentially arbitrary entry under load rather than the oldest — the entire Redis path (`connection()` backoff at history.rs:286-307, `reset_connection` at 310), and the mapping in `prepare_turn` (api.rs:445-454) where `HistoryError::NotFound|WrongTenant` becomes a 400 'previous_response_id was not found' but `Unavailable|TooLarge|Corrupt` becomes a 503.

*Production risk.* Conversation data loss presented as user error. If a Redis outage or timeout ever collapsed into `NotFound`, every client with a live multi-turn conversation is told its `previous_response_id` does not exist — a permanent-looking 400 that well-behaved clients respond to by discarding the conversation, so the customer loses context they already paid to build. The blue/green deploy is exactly when Redis connections reset, so this would fire during a routine deploy.

*Suggested test.* Tests in `crates/forward/src/codex/history.rs` `mod tests`: construct a store with `ttl_secs` at its floor and assert an entry past `expires_at` returns `NotFound` rather than stale data; fill past `local_cap` and assert the store never exceeds the cap and never returns another tenant's entry. Then a test in `api.rs` asserting `prepare_turn` maps a `HistoryError::Unavailable` to `ApiError::unavailable()` (503, retry-after set) and only `NotFound`/`WrongTenant` to a 400 — ideally as an exhaustive `match` over `HistoryError` so a new variant fails to compile rather than silently defaulting.

*Verified absent by.* crates/forward/src/codex/history.rs has exactly 2 tests: `local_history_is_tenant_bound` at :339 and `encrypted_envelope_is_bound_to_response_id` at :353. Untested as claimed: `get` at :160, TTL expiry in `local_get` at :225-238, min-expires_at eviction in `local_put` at :204-214, `connection()` backoff at :282-308 and `reset_connection` at :310-314. The prepare_turn mapping is real and unasserted — crates/forward/src/codex/api.rs:445-448 maps NotFound|WrongTenant to a 400 'previous_response_id was not found.' while :451-455 maps Unavailable|TooLarge|Corrupt to 503. The Redis path is live in production, not hypothetical: deploy/install-watchdog.sh:123-127 provisions CLAUDE_API_REDIS_URL into server.env and crates/server/src/config.rs:410-412/300 feeds it straight into history_redis_url, so the outage-during-deploy scenario is reachable. No cross-layer coverage found (`rg -n 'HistoryError' crates/` -> history.rs and api.rs only).

<a id="rust-codex--health-probe-clears-limit-cooling"></a>
#### `health-probe-clears-limit-cooling` — The background health sweep clears usage-limit cooling, and that interaction is untested

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/codex/mod.rs:403`

*Not covered today.* probe_health (mod.rs:403) and codex_health_loop (poller.rs:147) have no tests; the specific unpinned interaction is that mark_healthy clears usage-limit cooling while the rate-limit refresh that would re-exclude the home is best-effort and fail-open, so only the combined snapshot-unavailable case re-admits an exhausted home.

*Production risk.* An exhausted subscription is re-admitted and every request routed to it 429s, converting a clean 'retry after N seconds' into a customer-visible error storm. The failure needs two conditions that co-occur precisely during an outage — a home at its window limit and a rate-limit endpoint that is briefly unavailable — which is exactly when nobody is reading the code. Because the pool is small, one wrongly re-admitted home can absorb a large share of traffic.

*Suggested test.* Pool test in `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs` `mod tests` using `fake_pool_gateway(&["usage_limit", "text"])`: run a turn so home 0 cools, call `gateway.probe_health().await`, then assert home 0 is still excluded from `select_home` and `operational_status().available == 1`. Add the harder variant with a fake mode whose `account/read` succeeds but whose `account/rateLimits/read` errors, asserting the home does not silently become available. If the intended fix is that `mark_healthy` should not clear limit-derived cooling, split cooling into transport-cooling and window-cooling and pin both.

<a id="rust-codex--preflight-partial-pool-policy"></a>
#### `preflight-partial-pool-policy` — The deployment-blocking `preflight` policy is only tested with an all-healthy pool

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/forward/src/codex/mod.rs:367`

*Not covered today.* No test asserts preflight's partial-pool tolerance (one dead home out of two still Ok, quarantined) or the all-dead Err; the inverse failure is already caught post-deploy by deploy/watchdog.sh:556-569.

*Production risk.* Irreversible in the deployment sense: the watchdog self-deploys from master and a Codex preflight that wrongly requires all homes turns one expired ChatGPT device login — a routine, expected event — into a hard startup failure for the entire engine, blocking every subsequent deploy including the fix. The inverse regression (all homes failing but preflight returning Ok) exposes a provider that 503s every request while `/metrics` reports it enabled.

*Suggested test.* Pool test in `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs` `mod tests`: `fake_pool_gateway` with one home whose CODEX_HOME lacks the auth store (add an `unauthenticated` mode returning `account.type=apiKey`) plus one healthy home — assert `preflight()` is `Ok`, that `operational_status().homes[0].auth_ok == false` and its `cooling_until` is ~900s out. Then assert an all-unauthenticated pool returns `Err` whose message contains only the diagnostic class and none of the home paths.

<a id="rust-codex--codex-env-config-parsing"></a>
#### `codex-env-config-parsing` — Codex env parsing, including the duplicate-home guard, is untested

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/server/src/config.rs:184`

*Not covered today.* Neither codex_homes()'s duplicate-home guard (config.rs:191) nor codex_config()'s catalog allowlist (config.rs:218) has a test; both are startup panics whose regression would only surface when combined with an operator misconfiguration.

*Production risk.* Two failure modes, both operational. If the duplicate guard regressed, two supervised children share one CODEX_HOME and race on OAuth token refresh — that can invalidate the refresh token and take a purchased ChatGPT subscription permanently offline, while the pool believes it has twice the capacity it does and over-admits into a 429 wall. If the catalog allowlist regressed, an operator could advertise a model with no audited rate; `effective_prices` then falls back to the config copy and bills at whatever number happened to be there.

*Suggested test.* Tests in `crates/server/src/config.rs` `mod tests`. Refactor the env read out of `codex_homes` into `fn parse_codex_homes(raw: &str) -> Result<Vec<String>, String>` and table-test it: duplicates rejected, whitespace trimmed, empty segments dropped, empty result rejected, single-home spelling preserved. Similarly extract `fn select_codex_models(requested: &[String], catalog: &[CodexModel]) -> Result<Vec<CodexModel>, String>` and assert an unknown id is rejected, duplicates are deduped, and an empty selection is rejected — returning Result rather than panicking also makes the failure diagnosable in the deploy log.

<a id="rust-codex--turn-timeout-path"></a>
#### `turn-timeout-path` — The turn-timeout branch (interrupt, classify, retry) is never executed

**LOW** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs:549`

*Not covered today.* On `turn_timeout_ms` (production default 600_000ms, config.rs:273-278) the event loop is dropped, a best-effort `turn/interrupt` is sent and `ProcessError::Timeout("turn completion")` is returned (runner.rs:555-564). `note_turn_error` routes Timeout through the `_` arm to a 10-second transport cooling (mod.rs:476), and `run_turn` spends the single transport retry to re-run the entire turn on another home (runner.rs:156-161). The `hang` fake mode exists but the only test using it (runner.rs:1157) aborts the task rather than letting the 2s turn timeout elapse, so no test ever reaches this branch.

*Production risk.* Double quota burn on a wedged model turn: a timed-out turn that already consumed provider tokens is retried in full on a second subscription, and the customer is settled only for whichever attempt returns usage. With a 600s timeout this silently doubles the pool cost of the slowest requests. A regression that failed to send the interrupt would additionally leave the app-server turn running against the subscription after the gateway has moved on.

*Suggested test.* In `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs` `mod tests`, add a `stall_after_start` fake mode that answers `turn/start` and then emits nothing. With `turn_timeout_ms` set low, assert `run_turn` returns `ProcessError::Timeout("turn completion")`, that `turn/interrupt` reached the child's log, that the home's cooling is the ~10s transport value and `auth_ok` is still true, and that exactly one retry was attempted on the second home.

*Verified absent by.* Timeout branch verified at crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs:549-564 (best-effort turn/interrupt at :555-560, `ProcessError::Timeout("turn completion")` at :563); routed through note_turn_error's `_` arm to TRANSPORT_COOL_SECS at crates/forward/src/codex/mod.rs:476 and retried once at runner.rs:150-160. Production turn_timeout_ms is set at crates/server/src/config.rs:273. The 'hang' mode is the fake script's default `else` branch (runner.rs:846-848, emits a 'ready' delta and never completes) and its only consumer is `dropping_an_inflight_turn_sends_interrupt` (runner.rs:1156-1174), which calls `task.abort()` rather than letting the 2s turn_timeout_ms elapse — so the timeout arm is never entered by any test. One refinement to the risk, in the claim's favour on scope: the double-burn cannot happen on a streaming turn that already delivered a delta, because `emitted` short-circuits the retry at runner.rs:146-149; it applies to non-streaming turns (updates=None, so send_update at runner.rs:693-704 never sets `emitted`) and to streams whose downstream closed before the first successful send. Low severity is correct.

### 8.7 `rust-server` — Rust server crate (composition, HTTP surface, CLI, poller)

crates/server is the composition layer of the money engine: it reads every environment variable (config.rs), wires the axum router (http.rs), exposes the `/admin/*` Control API that the TypeScript commerce layer uses to create accounts, credit real money and issue `sk-pool-` keys (admin.rs), runs the background poller/persist/retention loops (poller.rs), and provides the operator CLI plus graceful-shutdown/billing-flush sequencing (main.rs). Coverage is 15 `#[test]`s across 4,894 lines, and they are unevenly placed: config.rs and http.rs contain genuinely excellent pure-function tests that pin real invariants (upstream pinning, unroutable supply exclusion, readiness reasons, OpenAI/Anthropic route disambiguation), while admin.rs (908 lines, the entire money control plane) and main.rs (1,261 lines, CLI + secret redaction + shutdown) have zero tests each. `crates/server/Cargo.toml` declares no `[dev-dependencies]` at all — no `tower`, no `http-body-util` — so not a single HTTP handler in this crate has ever been driven end-to-end in a unit test; every tested function is one that could be called directly. The engine-client contract is mocked on the TypeScript side only: `packages/engine-client/src/index.test.ts` asserts against response fixtures it authors itself, and nothing anywhere runs the real Rust handler and validates the result against `packages/contracts` zod schemas. The one real multi-process integration test that exists, `deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh`, is root-only, manual, never touches `/admin/*`, and is only `bash -n` syntax-checked by the watchdog gate (deploy/watchdog.sh:341) — it is never executed.

**Working well here:**

- **Upstream origin pinning — the control that stops subscription OAuth tokens being sent anywhere except Anthropic** — `crates/server/src/config.rs:574` — `upstream_is_pinned_or_explicit_literal_loopback` is a genuine adversarial table test, not a happy-path check. It pins nine cases including the ones a careless refactor would break: `https://api.anthropic.com:443` rejected (explicit port would defeat a naive string compare), `https://api.anthropic.com/v1` rejected (path), `https://user@api.anthropic.com` rejected (userinfo), `http://localhost:18080` rejected even with the insecure opt-in (only literal loopback IPs, not resolvable names), and `http://[::1]` accepted only with the opt-in. This is the single highest-value test in the crate and it would catch a real token-exfiltration regression.
- **Operator-typo clamping of capacity reserve fractions** — `crates/server/src/config.rs:553` — `frac_clamps_percent_typo_and_rejects_garbage` encodes the exact production failure the code comment describes (`RESERVE_7D=3` meaning 3% -> 300% reserve -> whole pool considered exhausted -> every customer refused). It also pins NaN/inf/`0,1` decimal-comma to the default rather than to 0.0, which is the correct fail-open-for-supply direction. The test is written against the extracted pure `clamp_frac`, which is exactly the right testability refactor.
- **Sellable-supply accounting excludes unroutable subscriptions** — `crates/server/src/http.rs:1114` — `capacity_aggregate_excludes_unroutable_supply` asserts that a cooling subscription with $50 of nominal capacity contributes zero to the advertised pool availability, and that a single uncalibrated-but-routable sub flips the aggregate `calibrated` flag to false. This directly guards against over-selling: the /capacity aggregate is what the admin panel and capacity planning read. The second assertion (a lone dead sub yields `([0.0;4], false)`) correctly pins the empty-routable-set edge rather than letting `all_calibrated` vacuously stay true.
- **Readiness distinguishes draining from fenced authority** — `crates/server/src/http.rs:1128` — `readiness_flag_flips_before_drain` covers all three states of the two-atomic readiness gate and asserts the exact `reason` string for each. This matters because Caddy health-routes blue-green slots on /ready (deploy/Caddyfile:105-108) and the owner-epoch fence writes `authority_ready=false` from a background loop (poller.rs:614) — conflating 'draining' with 'authority_unavailable' would make a fenced engine indistinguishable from a healthy one being retired.
- **Protocol transparency: OpenAI route disambiguation never swallows Anthropic traffic** — `crates/server/src/http.rs:1195` — `known_unsupported_openai_families_never_match_anthropic_messages` explicitly asserts that `/v1/messages` and `/v1/messages/count_tokens` are NOT in the OpenAI family set, alongside 8 positive cases. Paired with `shared_models_route_preserves_x_api_key_anthropic_clients` (http.rs:1156), which walks a 5-step header table across `x-api-key`/`Authorization`/`anthropic-version`, this pins the crate's stated top invariant — for an Anthropic client, the protocol must remain byte-identical — at the one place where a second provider could break it.
- **Probe scheduling under hostile upstream data and under ban-risk constraints** — `crates/server/src/poller.rs:713` — `probe_429_rejects_stale_and_bounds_hostile_resets` asserts that a past `reset7d` is ignored in favour of a valid `reset5h`, and that `reset7d = i64::MAX` is clamped to `MAX_PROBE_COOL_SECS` rather than overflowing or cooling a subscription for eternity. Its sibling `suspect_and_dead_probe_ignore_cooling` (poller.rs:661) pins a subtle three-way interaction — that a suspect subscription cooled for 900s by its own 401 probe must still mature on `SUSPECT_INTERVAL`, otherwise the dead-detection verdict would freeze forever — including the negative case that a freshly-probed dead sub is not due. These are exactly the tests that catch a regression in the ban detector.
- **Real multi-process authority e2e** — `deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:165-176` — The `2:2:2:0:0:0` assertion is a genuine exactly-once money check across two concurrently-running PostgreSQL-backed engines: two settled reservations, two charge ledger rows with non-null request_id, two done outbox rows, zero leftover active capacity leases, zero pool inflight, zero residual reserved_nano. It also verifies engine role least-privilege by attempting to read the commerce `users` table (:181) and asserts the SQLite singleton flock actually refuses a duplicate (:146). Nothing else in the repo covers these paths. The only reason this is not listed as a strength of the gate is that the gate does not run it.

<a id="rust-server--admin-authz-per-handler-convention"></a>
#### `admin-authz-per-handler-convention` — Every /admin/* money endpoint's authorization is an un-enforced per-handler convention with zero tests

**CRITICAL** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/server/src/admin.rs:18`

*Not covered today.* `deny()` is called by hand as the first statement of each of the 13 handlers; there is no axum layer, no middleware, and no route-level guard. No test — Rust, TypeScript or shell — ever sends an unauthenticated request, a wrong-key request, or a `CLAUDE_API_PANEL_KEY` request to any `/admin/*` route and asserts 401. The privilege lattice documented as an invariant in crates/server/CLAUDE.md (`authed` ⊂ `control_authed` ⊂ `readonly_authed`; panel key must never reach `/admin/*`) is asserted nowhere. In crates/forward, only the `ct_eq` primitive is tested (proxy.rs:1798); `authed` (proxy.rs:286), `key_in` (:302), `control_authed` (:314) and `readonly_authed` (:330) have no tests either, including the branch at proxy.rs:322-325 that grants loopback callers full control access when both key lists are empty.

*Production risk.* A new `/admin/*` route added to the router (http.rs:86-104) whose handler forgets the `deny()` prologue is an unauthenticated endpoint that creates accounts, credits arbitrary money, issues live `sk-pool-` keys or revokes them — and `cargo test` stays green. Equally, downgrading a route from `control_authed` to `readonly_authed`, or a regression in `key_in`'s constant-time fold, silently hands the low-privilege panel key the ability to mint balance. Detection today depends entirely on code review.

*Suggested test.* Add `[dev-dependencies] tower = { version = "0.5", features = ["util"] }` and `http-body-util` to crates/server/Cargo.toml, then a Rust integration test that builds `http::router(app, accepting)` against an AppState with `api_keys=[A]`, `control_keys=[C]`, `panel_keys=[P]`, `trust_loopback=false` and `billing=None`, and drives it with `tower::ServiceExt::oneshot`. Table-drive over a const list of all 13 admin route/method pairs asserting: no key -> 401, panel key P -> 401, control key C -> not-401, admin key A -> not-401. Add a second test asserting the list of `/admin/` routes in the table equals the routes registered in `router()` so a new route cannot be added without extending the table.

*Verified absent by.* Verified the mechanism and the absence of coverage. crates/server/src/http.rs:86-104 registers exactly 13 /admin/* routes; `grep -c 'deny(&app, &headers, &peer)' crates/server/src/admin.rs` returns 13 — one hand-written prologue per handler, and `crates/server/src/http.rs:71-122` shows no `.layer()`, `route_layer` or middleware on the router. Searches run: `grep -rn 'ct_eq|control_authed|readonly_authed|key_in|authed(' crates/ --include='*.rs'` — the only test hit is crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1798 `ct_eq_is_correct` (asserts the byte-fold primitive only); `authed` (proxy.rs:286), `key_in` (proxy.rs:302), `control_authed` (proxy.rs:314) and `readonly_authed` (proxy.rs:330) have no test. `grep -rn '/admin/' deploy/ tests/ tools/ systemd/ observability/` returns only deploy/Caddyfile:159 and deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:275, both about the *commerce* Caddy vhost, not the engine control plane. The one 401 assertion in the pipeline, deploy/watchdog.sh:471-475 `require_admin_auth_vhost`, checks Caddy's `managed_admin_auth` on an HTTPS vhost — it never reaches an engine /admin/* handler. The six inline tests in crates/server/src/http.rs:1084-1268 cover capacity aggregation, readiness, header sniffing, OpenAI error shapes and the embedded panel HTML — none send a request. Line citations check out: admin.rs:18 is `fn deny`, crates/server/CLAUDE.md:54-57 states the `authed ⊂ control_authed ⊂ readonly_authed` lattice and `/admin/* — control_authed`. Nothing asserts it. The loopback branch at proxy.rs:322-325 is real and untested.

<a id="rust-server--control-api-contract-mock-only"></a>
#### `control-api-contract-mock-only` — The engine-client contract is verified only against hand-authored mocks on the TypeScript side; neither side is checked against the other

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `packages/engine-client/src/index.test.ts:75`

*Not covered today.* No test on either side checks that the Rust `json!` literals in crates/server/src/admin.rs satisfy the zod schemas in packages/contracts/src/index.ts — every EngineClient test hand-authors its own fetch fixture (packages/engine-client/src/index.test.ts:12-15, :62-66, :74-83, :95-116, :165-172, :198-201, :217-220, :236-239) and `grep -rln 'crates/' --include='*.test.ts' apps packages` returns nothing, so no cross-language contract check exists. The break is narrower than stated: zod objects here are non-strict (they strip unknown keys), and many fields are `.nullish()` with fallbacks (packages/contracts/src/index.ts:41-46, :57-63, :79), so *additive* engine changes are safe; only renaming or retyping a required field (`account`, `balance_nano`, `mult_bp`, `key_id`, `entries[].id/kind/amount_nano`, `buckets.*`) breaks parsing. The cited CONTROL_API.md drift is real but is doc-only and carries no production risk: the actual lines are CONTROL_API.md:103 and :155 (not :112/:157), and `{"usd":25,"ref":"stripe_pi_123"}` would be rejected twice over by admin.rs:323-328 `deny_unknown_fields` and by admin.rs:330 `qualified_topup_ref` (no colon) — but every production caller goes through packages/engine-client/src/index.ts:110/:126, which always sends `amount_nano` as a string, so no live code path sends `usd`.

*Production risk.* Renaming or retyping any field in an admin.rs `json!` literal — `mult_bp`, `key_id`, `balance_nano`, `updated`, `entries[].id`, `buckets.cache_write` — passes `cargo test`, passes `pnpm test`, passes the full watchdog gate, and deploys. The commerce API then throws a zod parse error on every customer dashboard load, every payment credit, every key issuance. Because engine and backend are deployed as separate blue-green units from the same SHA (deploy/watchdog.sh:774-798), the break appears in production with no prior signal.

*Suggested test.* Add a contract test in packages/engine-client that boots the real `claude-api serve` binary against the disposable engine PostgreSQL the gate already provisions (deploy/watchdog-test-db.sh:38, `CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL`), points EngineClient at it with a known CONTROL_KEY, and exercises the full lifecycle — createAccount, creditAccount, issueKey, listKeys, replaceKeyPolicy, getLedger, getLedgerAfter, acknowledgeLedger, getUsage, setAccountMultiplier, setAccountStatus, disableKey — asserting each response parses through its contracts schema. Gate it on the env var so local `pnpm test` still runs. Alternatively, a cheaper first step: add Rust tests that serialize each handler's success/error `json!` shape and assert field names and JSON types against a checked-in fixture that packages/contracts also parses.

<a id="rust-server--metrics-revenue-disclosure-and-email-masking"></a>
#### `metrics-revenue-disclosure-and-email-masking` — Two privilege-scoped redactions — revenue metrics behind the control key, pool emails masked for the panel key — are untested

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/server/src/http.rs:343`

*Not covered today.* `/metrics` is gated by `readonly_authed` (http.rs:273), so a `CLAUDE_API_PANEL_KEY` holder reaches it; the client balance / billed / reserved / metered-accounts gauges are then emitted only inside `Some(b) if control_authed(&app, &headers, &peer)` (http.rs:343), falling through to operational-only metrics otherwise (http.rs:360). Separately, `/capacity` — also `readonly_authed` — masks subscription emails to four characters via a closure at http.rs:544, with the comment explaining these are resellable Claude accounts whose full addresses aid correlation and banning. Neither the metrics privilege branch nor `mask_email` is covered by any test; the six http.rs tests never touch either.

*Production risk.* Deleting or inverting the `control_authed` guard at http.rs:343 exposes aggregate revenue, total client balances and account counts — explicitly labelled commercial secrets in the crate's own docs — to the lowest-privilege key class. Removing `mask_email` leaks the full email addresses of every pooled Claude subscription to the same key class; those addresses are the identities Anthropic would ban. Both are one-line regressions that no test detects, and both are irreversible once the data has been read.

*Suggested test.* With the router test harness from the authz gap: assert `GET /metrics` with the panel key returns a body containing `claude_api_inflight` but NOT `claude_api_client_balance_usd`, `claude_api_billed_usd_total` or `claude_api_reserved_usd`, and that the same request with the control key does contain them. Separately extract `mask_email` to a free function and unit-test it: a normal address yields exactly four leading chars plus an ellipsis, the full address never appears in `capacity_value`'s serialized output, and short/unicode addresses do not panic.

*Verified absent by.* Both branches verified present and uncovered. crates/server/src/http.rs:273 gates `/metrics` on `readonly_authed`; crates/server/src/http.rs:343 `Some(b) if control_authed(&app, &headers, &peer)` guards the `claude_api_client_balance_usd` / `claude_api_billed_usd_total` / `claude_api_reserved_usd` / `claude_api_metered_accounts` gauges, falling through at :360 (`_ => body`). `mask_email` is a closure at crates/server/src/http.rs:544-547 inside `capacity_value`, applied at :553; `grep -rn 'mask_email' crates/ deploy/ apps/ packages/` returns only those two lines — zero test references. The six tests in crates/server/src/http.rs:1084-1268 touch `routable_capacity_summary`, `readiness_snapshot`, `prefers_anthropic`, `unsupported_openai_endpoint_error`, `is_known_openai_route_family` and `ADMIN_PANEL_HTML` — none constructs an AppState or calls `capacity_value`/`metrics`. deploy/monitoring-config.test.sh:140-160 greps crates/server/src/http.rs for Codex *metric name strings* only; it asserts nothing about the privilege branch or email masking. The privilege lattice at crates/server/CLAUDE.md:54-57 makes the panel key a real, provisioned key class (deploy/install-monitoring.sh:67-82 writes CLAUDE_API_PANEL_KEY into the engine env), so the exposure is reachable, not hypothetical.

<a id="rust-server--credit-money-validation-and-error-classification"></a>
#### `credit-money-validation-and-error-classification` — The credit endpoint's idempotency-ref rule and its 409-vs-503 error classifier are untested pure functions on the payment path

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/server/src/admin.rs:330`

*Not covered today.* `qualified_topup_ref` (crates/server/src/admin.rs:330) and `is_control_conflict` (admin.rs:55) genuinely have no direct unit test — `grep -rn 'is_control_conflict|qualified_topup_ref' crates/` returns only the definitions and three call sites (admin.rs:115, :396, :598). But failure mode (1) as described is not reachable: every current commerce ref producer is provider-qualified, whitespace-free, and asserted by an existing test, so no live caller can trip the 400. Failure mode (2) — the substring coupling between admin.rs:55-62 and the registry's error wording — is the real, uncovered risk, and it is a 409-vs-503 misclassification on an already-idempotent write, not a lost payment.

*Production risk.* Two concrete failures. (1) Any new commerce credit ref without a colon gets HTTP 400; EngineClientError marks 4xx non-retryable, and apps/worker/src/credit-worker.service.ts:77-81 then logs and *retains the job for retry forever* — a paid customer top-up is never delivered and no alert distinguishes it from a transient failure. (2) If registry error wording changes, a duplicate-ref conflict returns 503 'billing authority unavailable' instead of 409, or a genuinely transient authority error is misclassified as a terminal 409 on a real payment. Both directions are money-visible and neither is detectable by any current test.

*Suggested test.* Unit tests in a new `#[cfg(test)]` mod in crates/server/src/admin.rs: for `qualified_topup_ref`, a table covering `stripe:pi_123` ok, `signup-bonus:uuid` ok, `promo:id` ok, and rejecting `nocolon`, `:tx`, `provider:`, `pro vider:tx`, `provider:t x`, empty. For `is_control_conflict`, assert `true` for `anyhow!("idempotency reference belongs to another monetary operation")` — constructed by calling the real registry error path or at minimum by referencing the same string constant — and `false` for `anyhow!("connection closed")` and `anyhow!("timeout")`. Then add a cross-crate test asserting the exact bail! text in crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1163 is matched by `is_control_conflict`, so renaming the message breaks the build's tests rather than production.

<a id="rust-server--ledger-ack-prune-coupling"></a>
#### `ledger-ack-prune-coupling` — The durable ledger watermark that authorizes irreversible charge-row deletion is untested on both ends

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/server/src/admin.rs:231`

*Not covered today.* The claim's central factual assertion — '`ledger_ack` and `ledger_prune` appear in zero tests' — is wrong: crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2852-2866 `ledger_prune_is_disabled_without_consumer_watermarks` exercises `ledger_prune` and asserts the fail-closed direction (no checkpoint ⇒ zero rows deleted, charge stays cursor-visible), which is the safety-critical half. What is genuinely uncovered is the *permissive* half: no test ever writes a checkpoint and then asserts prune deletes only rows at or below it, `ledger_ack` has no test on either the SQLite (crates/registry/src/lib.rs:1172) or PostgreSQL (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1360) backend, the `GREATEST`/`MAX` monotonicity is unasserted, and the duplicated `"pricing"` consumer literal (crates/server/src/admin.rs:259 vs crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1380 / lib.rs:1196) is unpinned — a mismatch there fails safe (retention silently stops, ledger grows) rather than deleting revenue.

*Production risk.* Charge-ledger rows are the sole input to B2C tier pricing (CLAUDE.md: 'B2C pricing derives only from idempotently consumed engine charge-ledger rows'). If `ack_ledger` accepts a watermark the pricing worker has not actually applied — a parse or ordering regression, or the worker's replay-then-fetch sequence at apps/worker/src/pricing-worker.service.ts:116-122 inverting — retention deletes those charges 30 days later and the revenue attribution is unrecoverable, with no backup path since prune is a normal DELETE. Conversely, a mismatch in the `"pricing"` literal silently disables retention and the ledger grows without bound on the money database.

*Suggested test.* An integration test in crates/registry against `CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL` (the gate already supplies it, deploy/watchdog.sh:333): insert charge rows with ids 1..10 and ts older than the cutoff, call `ledger_ack("pricing", acct, 5)`, run `ledger_prune(cutoff)`, assert exactly ids 1..5 are gone and 6..10 remain; then assert a second `ledger_ack("pricing", acct, 3)` does not lower the watermark and that topup/adjust rows are never pruned. Add a Rust unit test on the `ack_ledger` handler's parsing (non-numeric, negative, i64-overflow `last_id` -> 400) and a constant shared between admin.rs and pg.rs for the consumer name.

<a id="rust-server--usage-bucket-aggregation"></a>
#### `usage-bucket-aggregation` — The customer-facing usage/spend breakdown — 45 lines of six-way accumulator arithmetic — has no test

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/server/src/admin.rs:844`

*Not covered today.* The *SQL* aggregate feeding this handler is well tested — crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2562-2582 asserts per-model grouping, all five token counters, `real_nano`, `charge_nano`, the DESC ordering and the window cutoff, and crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2646-2695 asserts settle writes the usage event in the same transaction. What is genuinely untested is only the HTTP-layer fold in crates/server/src/admin.rs:843-905: the six-way monetary component split (`input_nano`…`web_search_nano` are never asserted by any registry test either), the `cache_write` = 5m+1h merge, the i128 accumulation, and the `stored_components == 0 && real_nano > 0` legacy branch that diverts money to `unattributed_legacy` while still folding that row's tokens into the normal buckets. This is a read-only reporting surface: no money moves, `total_charged_nano` is the independently-tested `charge_nano` sum, and the buckets-vs-total 'silent disagreement' is by design (total includes unattributed, which is reported as its own bucket).

*Production risk.* A regression here shows every customer a wrong spend breakdown on their dashboard — tokens attributed to the wrong bucket, cache-write costs double-counted or dropped, or legacy rows repriced instead of being reported as unattributed. Because `total_official_nano` and the per-bucket sums are computed independently, they can silently disagree, producing a dashboard where the buckets do not add up to the total. That is a direct billing-dispute and refund vector, and it is entirely invisible to `cargo test` and `pnpm test`.

*Suggested test.* Extract the accumulation loop into a pure `fn usage_summary(aggs: &[registry::UsageModelAgg]) -> serde_json::Value` and unit-test it in crates/server/src/admin.rs: (a) a modern row asserting each bucket receives exactly its component and that `sum(buckets.official_nano) + unattributed == total_official_nano`; (b) a legacy row with all components zero and `real_nano > 0` asserting it lands entirely in `unattributed_legacy` and not in `input`; (c) a mixed set asserting the two are additive; (d) values near i64::MAX asserting i128 accumulation does not overflow and serializes as an exact decimal string, not a JSON number.

<a id="rust-server--cli-secret-redaction-and-ingestion"></a>
#### `cli-secret-redaction-and-ingestion` — Secret redaction and 0600-permission enforcement in the CLI have no tests, and admin.rs's key mask can panic on a non-ASCII key

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/server/src/main.rs:494`

*Not covered today.* The coverage claim is exactly right — `grep -n 'cfg(test)' crates/server/src/main.rs` returns nothing, so main.rs has no test module at all and `mask_proxy`, `read_secret_file`, `mask_key`, `usd_to_nano` and `usd_str` are entirely untested. The `admin::mask` byte-slice panic is also real and reachable. Two corrections to the stated impact: the workspace builds with unwind panics (Cargo.toml has no `panic = "abort"`), so a panicking axum handler aborts that one connection task, not the engine — it 500s/drops `GET /admin/account/{id}/keys` and `/ledger` for that account rather than 'taking down' anything process-wide; and reaching it requires an operator to deliberately feed a multi-byte key through `--key-file`, since `gen_key` (main.rs:566-573) only ever emits `sk-pool-<48 hex>`.

*Production risk.* A regression in `mask_proxy` prints a proxy URL with its password into operator stdout and journald, exposing pool egress credentials — and the fallback branches are exactly the ones a refactor breaks. A regression in `read_secret_file`'s mode check lets subscription OAuth tokens and customer API keys be read from world-readable files. The `admin::mask` byte-slice panics inside an axum handler on `GET /admin/account/{id}/keys` and `GET /admin/account/{id}/ledger`, taking down the customer's key list and payment history for that account until the row is removed.

*Suggested test.* Pure unit tests in main.rs: `mask_proxy("http://user:pass@1.2.3.4:8080/path?q=1")` must equal `http://***:8080` and must not contain `pass`, `user`, `path` or `1.2.3.4`; `mask_proxy("http://[2001:db8::1]:8080")` yields the port; malformed input yields `<proxy-redacted>`; empty yields the dash. For `read_secret_file`, write temp files with modes 0644/0640/0600 and assert only 0600 is accepted, plus rejection of empty, multi-line and oversized content. For `admin::mask`, add the same non-ASCII guard `mask_key` already has and a test asserting `mask("sk-pool-ééééééééééé")` returns a redaction marker rather than panicking, plus tests at the 16-byte boundary.

<a id="rust-server--e2e-not-in-gate"></a>
#### `e2e-not-in-gate` — The only real end-to-end test of this crate is never executed by the automated gate

**MEDIUM** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `deploy/watchdog.sh:340`

*Not covered today.* deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh is the sole test that starts `claude-api serve`, exercises the instance flock (:140-146), SIGUSR1 pre-drain (:187-193), graceful shutdown with billing flush (:197-200), the SQLite->PostgreSQL importer (:67-68) and concurrent settlement across two owners (:165-176). The watchdog gate only runs `bash -n` over `deploy/*.sh` (deploy/watchdog.sh:340-342) and executes exactly two shell suites — watchdog-lib.test.sh and monitoring-config.test.sh (:343-344). test-stage2-e2e.sh is documented as a manual root-only command (DEPLOYMENT.md:161). It also never issues a single `/admin/*` request.

*Production risk.* Regressions in main.rs's startup ordering, flock singleton, drain deadline handling or the mandatory post-deadline billing flush (main.rs:1202-1210, explicitly designed so a slow money-writer never becomes lost revenue) reach production unchecked. Because the watchdog self-deploys from master after its own gate passes, a break in exactly these paths is what would prevent the engine from starting or draining cleanly on the very deploy that introduced it.

*Suggested test.* Add a non-root, container-free subset of test-stage2-e2e.sh to the gate: with the disposable engine DSN already available at deploy/watchdog.sh:331, start two `claude-api serve` processes on loopback ports against a mock upstream, run the /ready, SIGUSR1 pre-drain and SIGTERM graceful-shutdown assertions, and add `/admin/*` coverage — create account, credit with a qualified ref, credit again with the same ref asserting the balance is unchanged, credit with an unqualified ref asserting 400, issue a key, list keys asserting the full secret is absent, and hit each admin route unauthenticated asserting 401. Invoke it from `prepare_and_test_candidate` alongside the existing two suites.

*Verified absent by.* Gate contents verified verbatim: deploy/watchdog.sh:337-342 iterates `find "$candidate/deploy" -type f -name '*.sh'` running only `bash -n "$shell_file"`, then deploy/watchdog.sh:343-344 runs exactly two suites (`watchdog-lib.test.sh`, `monitoring-config.test.sh`). The Rust and TypeScript legs are at deploy/watchdog.sh:326-334 (`pnpm -r --if-present test`, `cargo test --locked --workspace`) — neither invokes the e2e. `grep -rn 'test-stage2-e2e' . --include='*.sh' --include='*.md' --include='*.yml' --include='*.yaml'` returns only DEPLOYMENT.md:161 and docs/STAGE2_POSTGRES_AUTHORITY.md:126, both documenting it as a manual `sudo` command; the script never references itself from any other script. `ls .github/workflows` contains only indexnow.yml, so there is no CI leg either. The unique coverage is real: deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:130-147 exercises the SQLite flock singleton (asserts a duplicate `serve` exits non-zero), :158-176 concurrent settlement across two PG owners asserting `2:2:2:0:0:0` authority totals, :187-195 SIGUSR1 pre-drain (readiness 503 while /health stays 200), :197-200 graceful SIGTERM shutdown. It sets `CLAUDE_API_TRUST_LOOPBACK=0` and `CLAUDE_API_DRAIN_DEADLINE_SECS=10` (:104) — the only place those config paths run at all. Confirmed it issues no /admin/* request (`grep -n 'curl|admin' deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh` → only /ready, /health, /v1/messages). The mandatory post-deadline billing flush it would guard is at crates/server/src/main.rs:1202-1210.

<a id="rust-server--key-policy-guardrail-validation"></a>
#### `key-policy-guardrail-validation` — The spend-limit/expiry replacement endpoint's three-way JSON validation is untested

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/server/src/admin.rs:683`

*Not covered today.* The Rust-side `Value` discrimination in crates/server/src/admin.rs:692-744 and the four-variant status mapping at :753-777 have no direct Rust test, but the claim overstates the risk on both counts. (1) The 2^53-truncation scenario is unreachable through the only production caller: packages/engine-client/src/index.ts:204-216 range-checks `spendLimitNano` against signed-64 bounds and always serialises it as a decimal *string*, and both that check and the schema-level rule are tested. (2) The `LimitBelowUsage` -> 409 `limit_below_committed` contract is covered on both flanks — the registry variant by a live-PostgreSQL test, and the 409-code-to-typed-exception mapping by an API test — so a break would not go undetected. The residual gap is only a direct/hand-rolled control-API caller bypassing EngineClient.

*Production risk.* If the string-vs-number discrimination regresses, a numeric `spend_limit_nano` above 2^53 is silently truncated by the JSON layer and a customer's key gets a wrong lifetime spend cap — either far too high (uncapped spend against a prepaid balance) or too low (a paying customer's key stops working). If the `LimitBelowUsage` -> 409 `limit_below_committed` mapping breaks, the commerce layer cannot distinguish 'your edit would invalidate an in-flight reservation' from a generic failure, and EngineClient's `replaceKeyPolicy` (packages/engine-client/src/index.ts:222) throws an opaque error instead.

*Suggested test.* Rust unit tests over the deserialized `KeyPolicyReq` + validation prologue (extract it to a pure `fn parse_key_policy(req) -> Result<(Option<i64>, Option<i64>), Response>`): assert null/null accepted; `"5000"`/null accepted; `5000` (number) rejected 400; `"0"` and `"-1"` rejected; `"9223372036854775808"` rejected; `expires_ts` as a string rejected; `expires_ts` in the past rejected; a missing field rejected by `deny_unknown_fields`/required-field semantics. Add a router-level test asserting each `KeyPolicyUpdate` variant maps to the documented status and that the 409 body carries `code: "limit_below_committed"` as CONTROL_API.md promises.

<a id="rust-server--env-clamps-swallow-malformed-values"></a>
#### `env-clamps-swallow-malformed-values` — Bounded env parsing silently substitutes defaults for malformed values, and the loopback-trust anti-footgun is untestable as written

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/server/src/config.rs:55`

*Not covered today.* Factually correct that `bounded_u64`/`bounded_usize`/`bounded_i64`/`finite_nonnegative` (crates/server/src/config.rs:55-77) swallow parse failures without a log line and have no tests, while only `clamp_frac` (:331) warns and is tested. But the consequence is a silent fall back to a *safe, in-range default*, never to an unsafe value — these are fail-closed clamps, not validators, so the failure mode is a config no-op an operator must notice, not a production hazard. The `trust_loopback` half is over-stated: crates/server/src/config.rs:371-374 requires *two* independent conditions (explicit `CLAUDE_API_TRUST_LOOPBACK=1|true` opt-in AND a literal loopback bind host), defaults to false, and `control_authed` additionally requires both key lists to be empty (crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:322-325) — so a reverse-proxy auth bypass needs a regression in the conjunction plus an operator who configured no keys at all.

*Production risk.* An operator writing `CLAUDE_API_DRAIN_DEADLINE_SECS=10m` or `CLAUDE_API_MAX_INFLIGHT=6 ` with a stray character gets the default silently, and the deployment behaves differently from the config file with no log line to explain it — the failure mode that `clamp_frac`'s warning was specifically added to prevent. The `trust_loopback` conjunction is the difference between 'anonymous loopback callers get the full control plane' and 'they get 401'; a regression there is a complete auth bypass on a host running behind Caddy, and it is currently guarded only by reading the code.

*Suggested test.* Unit-test the bounded helpers by extracting the parse/clamp core to `fn clamp_parsed_u64(raw: Option<&str>, default, min, max) -> u64` and asserting: in-range passes, above-max clamps, below-min clamps, unparseable returns default, empty returns default — and add the missing eprintln warning for the unparseable case, asserted the same way `clamp_frac` is. Extract `fn trust_loopback(opt_in: Option<&str>, host: &str) -> bool` and table-test it: `(Some("1"), "127.0.0.1") == true`, `(Some("true"), "::1") == true`, `(Some("1"), "0.0.0.0") == false`, `(None, "127.0.0.1") == false`, `(Some("yes"), "127.0.0.1") == false`, `(Some("1"), "localhost") == true`.

<a id="rust-server--poller-leader-and-cas-persistence"></a>
#### `poller-leader-and-cas-persistence` — Poller leader-lease renewal and pool-state CAS rebase — the blue-green correctness paths — are untested

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `crates/server/src/poller.rs:407`

*Not covered today.* The specific production failure the claim describes — 'a cooling verdict written by the outgoing blue-green slot is lost during CAS rebase and a rate-limited subscription immediately re-enters rotation' — is directly tested and does not survive: `pool::merge_persisted_state`, the rebase primitive `persist_state_cas` calls on conflict, has a dedicated test asserting the persisted cooling window and newer provider window win while calibrated capacity is not inflated. The database-level CAS version conflict and leader mutual exclusion are also tested against live PostgreSQL. What remains genuinely uncovered is only the *orchestration* around those tested primitives: the bounded retry/backoff loop and 5-vs-8 attempt budgets in crates/server/src/poller.rs:407-480, and the `abort_all()`-on-lost-renewal path at poller.rs:558-571 plus the 10s sleep ceiling at :584.

*Production risk.* If the CAS rebase regresses, a cooling verdict written by the outgoing blue-green slot is lost during overlap and a rate-limited or banned subscription immediately re-enters rotation, amplifying the ban signal — the exact failure the write-through persist design exists to prevent. If the leader-renewal abort regresses, two overlapping engines probe the same subscriptions concurrently, doubling the synthetic request rate against Anthropic from the pool's own IPs. Both are silent: they produce no error, only a higher ban rate.

*Suggested test.* Refactor `persist_state_cas` to take a small trait (`save_pool_state`/`load_pool_state`) instead of `AuthorityConfig` directly, then unit-test with a fake that fails the first N saves: assert the rebase-load is invoked between attempts, `accept_persist_versions` is called exactly once on success, `Ok(())` is returned early for an empty export, and the final error message names the last failure after exhausting attempts. For `poll_loop`, extract the leader-renewal decision into a testable function and assert that a `Ok(false)` or `Err(_)` renewal result yields the abort branch. Additionally, extend deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh to cool a subscription on slot A, kill A, and assert slot B still sees it cooling.

<a id="rust-server--cli-f64-money-conversion"></a>
#### `cli-f64-money-conversion` — The CLI top-up path converts money through f64 with no test

**LOW** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `crates/server/src/main.rs:472`

*Not covered today.* `usd_to_nano` (main.rs:472) is `(usd * 1e9).round() as i64`, fed directly from clap's `--usd: f64` (main.rs:177) into `db.account_topup` (main.rs:417). The project's own rule is integers-only for money, and the HTTP path correctly enforces it (`amount_nano` as an i64 decimal string, admin.rs:357-375). No test covers the CLI conversion, and no test covers `usd_str` (main.rs:477) either. In Rust, a float-to-int `as` cast saturates, so `--usd 1e30` becomes i64::MAX and `--usd nan` becomes 0, both silently.

*Production risk.* An operator correcting a balance with a large or malformed `--usd` value writes a saturated or zero adjustment into the authoritative ledger with no error. Values like `--usd 0.1` are also not exactly representable, so the nano amount can differ by one from what the operator typed — small, but it breaks the stated to-the-nanodollar invariant and is the kind of drift that only surfaces during a reconciliation dispute.

*Suggested test.* Unit-test `usd_to_nano` in main.rs: assert `1.0 -> 1_000_000_000`, `-100.0 -> -100_000_000_000`, `0.000000001 -> 1`, and assert that `f64::NAN`, `f64::INFINITY` and `1e30` are rejected rather than silently saturating to 0 / i64::MAX — which requires changing the signature to `Result<i64>`. Ideally switch `--usd` to a string parsed as an exact decimal, matching the HTTP contract.

*Verified absent by.* crates/server/src/main.rs:472-474 is exactly `(usd * 1e9).round() as i64`; the clap arg is `usd: f64` at main.rs:177-178 (with `allow_negative_numbers = true` at :172); it feeds `db.account_topup(&id, usd_to_nano(usd), r#ref.as_deref())` at main.rs:422. `usd_str` is at main.rs:477-479. Neither is tested — main.rs contains no `#[cfg(test)]` module at all (`grep -n 'cfg(test)' crates/server/src/main.rs` → no matches). The saturating-cast and NaN->0 semantics are correct for Rust `as` casts.
