The contrast with the HTTP path is accurate: crates/server/src/admin.rs:326 types `amount_nano: Option<String>` and admin.rs:355-372 parses it as an i64 decimal string with a 400 on failure. Severity stays low: this is an operator-only escape hatch — all commerce credits go through the HTTP path (packages/engine-client/src/index.ts:108-120), and the CLI ref rules are enforced downstream by the tested `account_topup` idempotency (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2013-2020).

### 8.8 `commerce-api` — Commerce apps/api (users, auth, billing surface)

apps/api is the NestJS commerce backend that owns humans, sessions, OAuth identities, TOTP, api-key issuance/masking, admin operations and the checkout/webhook money path; it touches the Rust engine only through the HTTP Control API. Its 17 test files (~79 `it()` blocks) are strongest exactly where money and cryptography are: real Postgres integration tests for register/verify/login/reset/OAuth-claim, real RS256+JWKS verification for Google, real Cryptomus MD5 and Platega RUB→USD checkout paths, and a real bcrypt→argon2 admin credential upgrade. The coverage collapses at two boundaries. First, nothing in the entire repository exercises the HTTP pipeline: every "controller" test constructs the class with `new` and calls methods directly, so `SessionAuthGuard` and the global `OriginGuard` — the only enforcement of the CLAUDE.md invariant that user identity comes from the server-side session and not the client — have zero tests, and deleting a `@UseGuards` decorator would leave `pnpm test` green. Second, several money-mutating units are wholly untested: the welcome-bonus antifraud gate (`maybeGrantSignupBonus` and its `claimSignupBonus`/`releaseSignupBonus` compensation), `AdminService.creditUser` (which mints a fresh random idempotency ref on every call), `AdminOperationsService.revokeSignupBonus` (a real engine debit), `PromoService.redeem`, and the whole `SalesFeedController` internal cross-context surface. Every Postgres-backed suite is wrapped in `describe.runIf(Boolean(process.env.TEST_DATABASE_URL))`, so locally the auth and billing surface silently reports green with nothing executed — only the prod watchdog sets the variable.

**Working well here:**

- **OAuth claim of an existing password account (full state machine in real Postgres)** — `apps/api/src/auth.service.test.ts:264-361` — Parameterised over both providers, it asserts the entire transaction outcome in one place: password_hash nulled, exactly one active session and one revoked session, every pending auth_token burned, the auth.oauth_claimed audit row written, the welcome bonus credited exactly once with the exact ref, the old session token no longer authenticating, and the old password no longer logging in. Almost any regression in packages/db/src/oauth.ts:120-160 fails this test loudly.
- **Google ID-token verification is cryptographically real, not mocked** — `apps/api/src/auth-providers.test.ts:22-54` — The test generates an RS256 keypair, signs a genuine ID token with issuer/audience/nonce, and serves the JWK through a stubbed fetch so jose actually verifies the signature. It tests the verifier rather than a stand-in for it — the failure mode most OAuth test suites have.
- **Payment-provider money authority** — `apps/api/src/checkout.service.test.ts:135-178` — The Platega test drives a RUB-denominated provider (3700 RUB at a fixture rate) and then asserts the engine_credits row is exactly 37000000000 nano — i.e. the locally recorded USD, never the provider's collected currency. That is the single most valuable assertion in the billing surface, and it is made against real Postgres with a real provider adapter.
- **Raw API key is returned once and never persisted** — `apps/api/src/account.service.test.ts:52-94` — It asserts `JSON.stringify(persisted.rows)` does not contain the raw sk-pool value, that the list view has no `key` property, and that cross-user policy update and revoke both fail while the engine call counters stay unchanged — proving the rejection happened before any engine mutation, not merely that an error was thrown.
- **Legacy admin credential migration** — `apps/api/src/admin-accounts.service.test.ts:121-143` — Real bcryptjs hashes (including the $2y$ variant rewrite) are imported, then a successful login is used to prove the stored hash was transparently upgraded to argon2id and password_changed_at was set. It tests the migration's observable effect on the database rather than the function's return value.
- **Sub-second API key expiry boundary** — `apps/api/src/api-key-policy.test.ts:29-33` — Fake timers pinned to 2030-01-01T00:00:00.100Z prove that an expiry 800ms in the future is rejected because it floors to the current engine second. That is a genuinely subtle off-by-one against the engine's second-resolution expiry, and it is pinned deliberately.
- **Antifraud primitives used by the welcome bonus** — `packages/db/src/antifraud.unit.test.ts:3-79` — Table-driven with realistic adversarial input: gmail dot/plus collapsing, googlemail aliasing, IPv6 /64 with :: expansion, IPv4-mapped IPv6 unwrapping, and malformed addresses. These pure helpers are the pieces most likely to be quietly broken by a refactor, and they are thoroughly pinned (the orchestration that calls them is not — see gaps).

<a id="commerce-api--session-guard-untested"></a>
#### `session-guard-untested` — SessionAuthGuard and OriginGuard have zero tests; nothing exercises the HTTP pipeline

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/api/src/auth.guard.ts:22`

*Not covered today.* Neither SessionAuthGuard (apps/api/src/auth.guard.ts:22) nor the global OriginGuard (apps/api/src/origin.guard.ts:11) is exercised by any test, and no test boots a Nest app, so @UseGuards wiring, param binding, the /v1 prefix and Cache-Control headers are unverified. BUT the auditor's headline failure scenario is wrong: removing @UseGuards(SessionAuthGuard) from AccountController would NOT make the endpoints anonymous — every handler takes @CurrentAuth(), whose decorator throws UnauthorizedException when request.auth is unset (auth.guard.ts:35-38), so that specific regression fails closed with 401. The real uncovered risk is OriginGuard, which is a global APP_GUARD (auth.module.ts:37) and DOES fail open: its three bypass branches (origin.guard.ts:16 webhooks, :20 /v1/internal/*, :25 admin-key) and the final origin comparison have no test. Partial mitigation: the admin-key bypass delegates to matchesConfiguredAdminKey/safeEqual (admin.guard.ts:20-35), which IS covered.

*Production risk.* AUTHENTICATION.md's core authorization invariant — 'Controllers never accept userId ... SessionAuthGuard resolves the current user from the server-side session' — is enforced only by decorators that no test observes. Removing @UseGuards(SessionAuthGuard) from apps/api/src/account.controller.ts:31 would make balance, ledger, usage, api-key issuance and revocation anonymous for every user, and the full watchdog gate would stay green. The same applies to the global OriginGuard, the only CSRF defence, whose three bypass branches (origin.guard.ts:16, :20, :25) are equally unverified.

*Suggested test.* Add apps/api/src/http.e2e.test.ts building the real app with Test.createTestingModule + FastifyAdapter and app.inject(): (a) GET /v1/account with no cookie → 401, with a forged cookie → 401, with a real session cookie → 200 and the body scoped to that user; (b) GET /v1/account?userId=<other> and a x-user-id header → still the session user's data; (c) POST /v1/checkouts with Origin absent and with a foreign Origin → 403, with the configured origin → 200; (d) POST /v1/payments/cryptomus/webhook with a foreign Origin → not 403; (e) GET /v1/admin/dashboard without x-admin-key → 401.

<a id="commerce-api--welcome-bonus-antifraud-ungated"></a>
#### `welcome-bonus-antifraud-ungated` — The $4 welcome-bonus antifraud gate and its compensation path are entirely untested

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/api/src/auth.service.ts:402`

*Not covered today.* The orchestration branches of maybeGrantSignupBonus (apps/api/src/auth.service.ts:408 short-circuit, :411-413 email-domain flag, :416-418 subnet-velocity flag, :428-431 missing-engine-account release, :438-441 release-on-credit-failure, and the swallow-to-'granted' catch at :391-393) are genuinely untested, and no test exercises upsertSignupProfile/claimSignupBonus/releaseSignupBonus/flagSignupProfile/countRecentSubnetSignups directly. However the auditor's two named regression scenarios are partly covered: the isBonusEligibleEmailDomain allow/deny predicate and the canonicalizeEmail / ipSubnetOf clustering keys ARE unit-tested, and the happy path (claim + engine credit with the exact idempotent ref) runs against real Postgres in the watchdog gate.

*Production risk.* This gate is the only thing standing between a scripted GitHub-OAuth signup farm and unlimited $4 engine credits (real Claude quota resold at 40-20% of list). A regression that makes upsertSignupProfile return bonusGranted:false on a repeat device, or that inverts the isBonusEligibleEmailDomain check, is free money at scale. Conversely, a broken releaseSignupBonus leaves the claim row set after a failed credit, permanently denying a legitimate customer their bonus with no operator signal.

*Suggested test.* Integration tests in apps/api/src/auth.service.test.ts against the real database: (1) two OAuth signups from the same /24 beyond SUBNET_SIGNUP_MAXIMUM → second gets flagged_reason='subnet-velocity' and creditAccount not called; (2) a verified GitHub identity on a disposable domain → flagged_reason='email-domain', no credit; (3) two accounts sharing a device cookie hash → only the first credited; (4) two accounts whose canonical emails collide (user.name+x@gmail.com vs username@gmail.com) → one credit; (5) an EngineClient whose creditAccount rejects → assert the signup-bonus claim row is released and a subsequent sign-in credits exactly once.

<a id="commerce-api--revoke-signup-bonus-untested"></a>
#### `revoke-signup-bonus-untested` — Admin welcome-bonus revocation performs a real engine debit with no test

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `apps/api/src/admin-operations.service.ts:191`

*Not covered today.* revokeSignupBonus debits B2C_SIGNUP_BONUS_BALANCE_NANO from the engine under ref `bonus-revoke:<user_id>` and marks the antifraud profile revoked. admin-operations.service.test.ts covers dashboard, creditUser, setUserStatus and resetTotp but never calls it; the controller test's fakeOperations() (admin-operations.controller.test.ts:72-84) does not even declare the method, so the POST /admin/users/:id/bonus/revoke route (admin-operations.controller.ts:83) has no test path either. The related 409 branch in creditUser, where an idempotency key is replayed with a different user or amount (admin-operations.service.ts:148-150), is likewise untested.

*Production risk.* This is an irreversible debit of a customer's live balance, and its own comment notes it can drive the balance negative (blocking all their API keys). A regression in the replay branch (admin-operations.service.ts:206-219) would debit a second time on an operator retry; a regression in the ref namespace would collide with, or fail to deduplicate against, Control-API manual revocations. The untested creditUser 409 branch is the guard that stops one idempotency key from being reused to authorise a different amount.

*Suggested test.* Extend apps/api/src/admin-operations.service.test.ts: revoke once against the FakeAdminEngine and assert exactly one debit with ref bonus-revoke:<id> plus the signup-profile revocation flag; revoke again and assert idempotent_replay:true with no second engine call; revoke for a user with no engine account → 404; and add a creditUser case replaying one idempotency_key with a different amount → AdminOperationError(409) with no engine call.

*Verified absent by.* Searched: rg -n 'it\(|describe\(|revokeSignupBonus' apps/api/src/admin-operations.service.test.ts apps/api/src/admin-operations.controller.test.ts — the service suite has exactly four tests (:82 dashboard, :102 creditUser idempotency, :126 status disable/enable, :158 TOTP reset) and never names revokeSignupBonus; the controller's fakeOperations() (apps/api/src/admin-operations.controller.test.ts:72-84) declares dashboard/topups/audit/businessInvites/creditUser/setUserStatus/revokeSessions/resetTotp and omits revokeSignupBonus entirely, so POST users/:id/bonus/revoke (apps/api/src/admin-operations.controller.ts:83-95) has no test path. rg -n 'revokeSignupBonus|debitAccount' across all *.test.ts: zero hits. The debit + replay logic is apps/api/src/admin-operations.service.ts:191-241 (ref `bonus-revoke:<user_id>` at :196, findAdminCreditByRef replay at :206-219, engine.debitAccount at :221). The creditUser 409 replay-mismatch branch (admin-operations.service.ts:147-150) is likewise unhit: the one idempotency test (:102-124) replays the SAME user and amount.

<a id="commerce-api--oauth-browser-binding-untested"></a>
#### `oauth-browser-binding-untested` — OAuth state/cookie binding and transaction single-use have no negative test

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `apps/api/src/auth.service.ts:265`

*Not covered today.* completeOAuth first requires safeTokenEqual(input.state, input.stateCookie) and then consumes the transaction exactly once. Both OAuth tests pass `stateCookie: state` (auth.service.test.ts:247 and :313); no test passes a null cookie, a mismatched cookie, a replayed state, or an expired transaction, and consumeOAuthTransaction has no test in packages/db either.

*Production risk.* This is the login-CSRF / session-fixation defence for the two OAuth flows that also carry the welcome bonus and the referral attribution. A regression that skips the cookie comparison, or that fails to mark the transaction consumed, lets an attacker complete an OAuth callback in a victim's browser (logging the victim into the attacker's account) or replay a captured code+state to mint a second session.

*Suggested test.* In apps/api/src/auth.service.test.ts add: completeOAuth with stateCookie:null → InvalidOAuthTransactionError; with a different valid state in the cookie → InvalidOAuthTransactionError; the same state consumed twice → second call rejects and creates no second session row; a transaction whose expires_at is set in the past → rejects. Assert auth_sessions count stays at the expected value in each case.

*Verified absent by.* Searched: rg -n 'consumeOAuthTransaction|safeTokenEqual|stateCookie|createOAuthTransaction' over the whole tree (non-.md). Production code: apps/api/src/auth.service.ts:265-268 (cookie binding then single-use consume), packages/db/src/oauth.ts:46 (consumeOAuthTransaction). Test hits: only apps/api/src/auth.service.test.ts:247 and :313, both passing `stateCookie: state` (the matching value). No test passes null/mismatched cookie, replays a state, or expires a transaction; consumeOAuthTransaction has no packages/db test (rg over packages/db/src/*.test.ts: zero hits). The browser-side test apps/web/src/app/auth/callback/oauth-callback.test.ts:5 only asserts failure copy, not binding. Note the cookie is read at apps/api/src/auth.controller.ts:269 from `apitoken_oauth_state`, which is itself only set by the untested oauthCookie() helper (auth.controller.ts:340).

<a id="commerce-api--admin-credit-nonidempotent-untested"></a>
#### `admin-credit-nonidempotent-untested` — POST /v1/admin/users/:id/credit mints a new idempotency ref per call and has no test

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/api/src/admin.service.ts:65`

*Not covered today.* Correct that AdminService has no test file at all (creditUser, listUsers, refundEligibility, createBusinessInvite, setBusinessPricing) and that admin.service.ts:65 mints a fresh random ref per call. But the production risk is smaller than stated: no UI or service calls POST /v1/admin/users/:id/credit — the admin panel posts to /admin/users/<id>/balance-adjustments, i.e. the tested AdminOperationsService.creditUser path with a caller-supplied idempotency_key — so a duplicate credit requires an operator issuing the legacy route by hand with the admin key. Reduce to medium: real coverage gap on a legacy/orphaned money route, not a live double-charge path.

*Production risk.* The untested route is still mounted (apps/api/src/admin.controller.ts:36) behind the same shared admin key. A double-click, a proxy retry or a timeout-then-retry credits the customer twice with real money, and nothing in commerce or the engine can collapse the duplicate afterwards because the refs differ. Its body schema is also a bare z.string() (admin.controller.ts:24), so the only amount validation is the untested regex at admin.service.ts:56.

Also assert amount_usd '0', '01', '1.5' and '100000' are rejected before any engine call.

<a id="commerce-api--google-verified-email-negative"></a>
#### `google-verified-email-negative` — Google's email_verified, nonce, audience and issuer rejections are never exercised

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/api/src/auth-providers.ts:91`

*Not covered today.* Accurate that there is no negative test for the two conjuncts the code itself enforces — email_verified:false and a mismatched nonce (apps/api/src/auth-providers.ts:91-93) — while GitHub's negative counterpart exists. Overstated on audience/issuer: those are not checked by application code but passed as options to jose's jwtVerify (auth-providers.ts:86-89), so a 'wrong audience / foreign issuer' test would be testing the library, not this codebase. Narrow the claim to: 'no test signs a Google ID token with email_verified:false or a wrong nonce'.

*Production risk.* CLAUDE.md and AUTHENTICATION.md make the provider-verified email the sole basis of Google identity, and packages/db/src/oauth.ts:120 uses that email to silently claim an existing password account. If the email_verified conjunct were dropped in a refactor, an attacker who adds an unverified victim@gmail.com to a Google Workspace-style account could claim the victim's paying commerce account — the takeover the doc explicitly forbids — and the suite would stay green.

*Suggested test.* Add table-driven cases to apps/api/src/auth-providers.test.ts reusing the existing keypair fixture: sign ID tokens with (a) email_verified:false, (b) nonce:'other-nonce', (c) audience:'wrong-client', (d) issuer:'https://evil.example', and assert each rejects with OAuthProviderError and never returns an identity.

<a id="commerce-api--sales-feed-surface-untested"></a>
#### `sales-feed-surface-untested` — The internal sales feed — its auth guard, its pricing writes and its cross-partner data exposure — has no tests

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/api/src/sales-feed.controller.ts:37`

*Not covered today.* Correct that apps/api/src/sales-feed.controller.ts has no test file and SalesFeedGuard is never constructed, leaving the 404-when-unconfigured branch, the header-type check, referralDiscount's override write and referralProfiles' live-balance fan-out unverified — and setReferralFloor itself has no test in packages/db. Two parts of the stated risk are overstated: (a) the constant-time comparison the guard uses is the shared safeEqual/matchesConfiguredAdminKey helper, which IS covered; (b) a parseCursor reset to 0 would NOT double-pay partner commissions — sales ingestion is dedupe-keyed on commerce_event_id/commerce_payment_id with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING, and the consumer additionally rejects a cursor that lags its items.

*Production risk.* This is the sole commerce↔sales boundary and it both moves pricing and exposes money. A broken guard exposes every user's live balance and lets an unauthenticated caller rewrite discount floors; a parseCursor regression that resets to 0 makes the sales-side feed reprocess the whole usage-event history, double-paying partner commissions; and because ownership is enforced only in sales-api, a bug there turns into arbitrary cross-partner balance disclosure here.

*Suggested test.* Add apps/api/src/sales-feed.controller.test.ts: guard tests for missing key, wrong key and unset SALES_CONTROL_KEY (→NotFound); table-driven parseCursor/parseLimit cases including '', '-1', '1e9', a 20-digit number and a value above the max; a real-Postgres test that the attributions/usage-events/topups payloads contain no email field; and referralDiscount with override:true vs. omitted, asserting the monotonic-floor invariant holds in the automatic path.

A regression in validateMaskedApiKey would let a full raw key be written into commerce Postgres and rendered in the dashboard key list. A regression in renameApiKey's re-verification would let one user rename another user's key by key_id.

Ownership enforcement is proven only for updateApiKeyPolicy/disableApiKey (account.service.test.ts:79-91).

<a id="commerce-api--cookie-and-cors-contract"></a>
#### `cookie-and-cors-contract` — No test asserts the session cookie attributes, Clear-Site-Data, no-store, or the CORS method list

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/api/src/auth.controller.ts:294`

*Not covered today.* Correct that no test asserts the cookie attribute strings or the CORS method list. But two supporting details are wrong: (1) Clear-Site-Data is not merely untested, it is not implemented — the only occurrence in the repo is the doc sentence at AUTHENTICATION.md:53, while logout (auth.controller.ts:208-213) sets only the expiring cookie; that is doc drift, not a coverage gap. (2) The __Host-/Secure 'drift' cannot arise from configuration: sessionCookieName() reads process.env.NODE_ENV (auth.guard.ts:42-44) and setSession reads ConfigService NODE_ENV (auth.controller.ts:295), but ConfigService NODE_ENV is the validated projection of the same process.env value (config.ts:30), so divergence requires a code edit, not a bad deploy. The real, unmitigated gap is the CORS method list (main.ts:38-44), which has a documented past-outage failure mode and no assertion anywhere.

*Production risk.* The __Host- prefix is only valid together with Secure and Path=/ and no Domain; any drift between the name selection (which reads process.env.NODE_ENV directly at auth.guard.ts:43) and the Secure flag (which reads ConfigService at auth.controller.ts:295) makes browsers silently reject the cookie — a total login outage in production that is invisible in dev. The CORS methods list is a documented past outage: @fastify/cors defaults to GET,HEAD,POST, so dropping PATCH/DELETE breaks key revocation and profile edits with 'Failed to fetch' and no server-side error.

*Suggested test.* With the e2e harness from session-guard-untested: assert the Set-Cookie from POST /v1/auth/login contains HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax, Path=/ and Max-Age=SESSION_TTL_SECONDS, and that with NODE_ENV=production the name is __Host-apitoken_session and Secure is present; assert POST /v1/auth/logout emits both a zero-Max-Age cookie and clear-site-data; assert GET /v1/account and /v1/auth/me carry Cache-Control: no-store; and assert an OPTIONS preflight for DELETE and PATCH from the configured origin is allowed.

<a id="commerce-api--engine-base-url-refine-untested"></a>
#### `engine-base-url-refine-untested` — ENGINE_BASE_URL credential and plain-HTTP restrictions have no test

**MEDIUM** · verdict `UNVERIFIED` · `apps/api/src/config.ts:13`

*Not covered today.* engineBaseUrlSchema rejects embedded credentials, non-origin paths/queries and plain HTTP to a non-loopback host (config.ts:18-26), and isLoopbackHostname (config.ts:3) hand-parses IPv4 and bracketed IPv6. config.test.ts exercises every other superRefine group (GitHub all-or-none, production callback, Content Studio key, admin previous key) but never this one.

*Production risk.* A regression in isLoopbackHostname — e.g. accepting 127.0.0.1.evil.com or 0x7f.0.0.1 — lets a misconfigured deploy send the ENGINE_CONTROL_KEY over plaintext HTTP to an external host on every Control API call, leaking the credential that mints accounts and credits balances. Config validation is the only place this is caught, since it runs at boot.

*Suggested test.* Table-driven cases in apps/api/src/config.test.ts: accept http://127.0.0.1:8790, http://localhost:8790, http://[::1]:8790 and https://engine.internal; reject http://engine.internal, http://127.0.0.1.evil.com, http://256.0.0.1, https://user:pass@engine.internal, https://engine.internal/admin and https://engine.internal/?x=1 — each with the specific message asserted.

<a id="commerce-api--engine-provisioning-failure-branches"></a>
#### `engine-provisioning-failure-branches` — Both provisioning state machines' error and lost-CAS branches are untested

**MEDIUM** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `apps/api/src/auth.service.ts:487`

*Not covered today.* AuthService.provisionEngineAccount has five distinct post-creation outcomes (rowCount===1, latestStatus active, latestStatus disabled with an AUDIT-TODO at auth.service.ts:505, mapping-changed error, and the whole error path at :511-532); no test drives any but the success path. AccountService.ensureEngineAccount has parallel branches, of which only the 'error → recover' path is covered (account.service.test.ts:96-133); the lost-CAS branch at account.service.ts:108-112, also carrying an AUDIT-TODO about a missing engine-side disable compensation, is untested. EngineAccountDisabledError on login (auth.service.ts:466) has no test.

*Production risk.* These branches decide whether a customer ends up with an orphaned engine account (created remotely, never recorded in commerce — leaking pool capacity and, for OAuth signups, a bonus credit on an account nobody owns) or is wrongly told their account is disabled. Both AUDIT-TODOs describe exactly this, so the compensation gap is known and unmonitored.

*Suggested test.* Table-driven tests with a FakeEngine plus a database wrapper that mutates engine_accounts between the create call and the UPDATE: assert (a) concurrent admin disable during provisioning → EngineAccountDisabledError and no active mapping; (b) concurrent successful provisioning by another instance → the caller returns the winner's account id and does not overwrite it; (c) engine.createAccount throwing → status 'error' with last_error truncated to 1000 chars, and a subsequent call recovering via the idempotent handle.

*Verified absent by.* AuthService.provisionEngineAccount (apps/api/src/auth.service.ts:460-533): only the rowCount===1 success branch at :493-495 is reached by tests; nothing drives the lost-claim branches at :498-508 (including the AUDIT-TODO at :505) or the whole catch at :509-532, because no test injects an engine failure — the engine doubles in apps/api/src/auth.service.test.ts:27-31, :81-83, :233-236 always resolve. EngineAccountDisabledError (thrown at :466, :475, :506, :525) appears in no test: rg -n 'EngineAccountDisabledError|EngineAccountUnavailableError' --glob '*.test.ts' returns zero hits. AccountService.ensureEngineAccount: the FakeEngine (apps/api/src/account.service.test.ts:159-257) has no createAccount-failure switch, so the catch at account.service.ts:77-94 and the lost-CAS branch at :108-112 (with its AUDIT-TODO about missing engine-side disable compensation) are unreached; the three provisioning tests (account.service.test.ts:96, :111, :125) all pre-seed an 'error'/missing mapping and then succeed.

<a id="commerce-api--promo-redeem-untested"></a>
#### `promo-redeem-untested` — Promo redemption credits real money with no test coverage

**MEDIUM** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `apps/api/src/promo.service.ts:43`

*Not covered today.* PromoService.redeem has no test file. Unverified: the 503 when SALES_API_URL/SALES_CONTROL_KEY are absent (:46), the sales-api status mapping 404/409/400/502 (:119-122), the 3-attempt engine credit retry (:59-71), the best-effort attribution swallow (:74-78) and the 3-attempt discount-floor retry whose final failure is only logged while the credit stands (:84-97).

*Production risk.* The code itself documents the dangerous window: the code is already consumed in sales, so a mishandled failure means the customer's promo is burned without the balance arriving, or the credit lands without the promised discount floor. Both are money-visible support incidents with no automated guard.

*Suggested test.* Add apps/api/src/promo.service.test.ts with a stubbed fetch for sales-api and a FakeEngine: assert each sales status maps to the right PromoError; assert the engine credit uses the exact redemptionRef and that a transient failure retries then succeeds with exactly one net credit; assert a permanent failure raises PromoError(502) and logs no code; assert discountBps>0 applies the floor and that a persistently failing setReferralFloor still returns the successful credit.

*Verified absent by.* Searched: rg -n 'PromoService|promo' --glob '*.test.ts' across the repo — hits are only packages/db/src/pricing.unit.test.ts:12-14 (isFreeCreditRef('promo:SUMMER') classification), packages/sales-db partner-lifecycle/analytics fixtures inserting promo_codes rows, and apps/web/src/migration.test.ts:137 (a dashboard section name). None touch apps/api/src/promo.service.ts, and there is no promo.service.test.ts or promo.controller.test.ts in `ls apps/api/src`. Uncovered branches confirmed by reading the file: 503 when SALES_API_URL/SALES_CONTROL_KEY missing (:46), engine-credit 3-attempt retry then 502 (:59-71), best-effort attribution swallow (:74-78), discount-floor retry whose final failure is only logged after the credit landed (:84-97), and the sales status mapping 404/409/400/502 (:119-122). The sales-side counterpart is equally bare: rg for 'redeem' in packages/sales-db/src and apps/sales-api/src matches only packages/sales-db/src/promo.ts, apps/sales-api/src/internal.controller.ts and partner.controller.ts — no test file.

<a id="commerce-api--oauth-email-linking-contradicts-doc"></a>
#### `oauth-email-linking-contradicts-doc` — The test suite pins automatic email-based account linking, which AUTHENTICATION.md forbids

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `packages/db/src/oauth.ts:120`

*Not covered today.* The doc-vs-code contradiction is real and correctly cited, but this is a specification/documentation defect, not a test-coverage gap: the linking behaviour is deliberately and thoroughly pinned by tests (both providers, password_hash nulled, sessions revoked, tokens invalidated, audit event, and the bonus credit), so it cannot regress silently — what is wrong is AUTHENTICATION.md, which still describes a prohibition the product does not implement. The one substantive sub-claim (the welcome bonus is granted on a claim of a pre-existing account, isNewAccount=false) is also asserted as intended behaviour by those tests rather than being untested.

*Production risk.* There is no test enforcing the documented invariant, so an auditor reading AUTHENTICATION.md has a false picture of the identity model, and any future safety property derived from it (for example 'a password account can only be taken over via the reset-email flow') is unfounded. The practical exposure is bounded — the claim requires a provider-verified email — but the bonus is granted on the claim path even though the account is not new (isNewAccount is false at oauth.ts:159), which is a distinct, untested money decision.

*Suggested test.* Decide the intended behaviour and pin it: if claiming stays, update AUTHENTICATION.md and add an explicit test naming it (claim requires identity.emailVerified === true, and assert what happens to a b2b/invited account and to a disabled account — oauth.ts:121 throws a bare Error('account is disabled') that surfaces as a generic authentication_failed redirect). If it should not stay, add a test asserting a same-email OAuth sign-in creates no link and returns a distinguishable error.

### 8.9 `commerce-db` — packages/db (commerce PostgreSQL, pricing, migrations)

packages/db is the commerce authority for humans, payments, B2C tier pricing and the durable job queues that push multipliers into the Rust engine. Its money core is pricing.ts: it consumes engine charge-ledger pages idempotently (pricing_usage_events unique on (engine_account_id, ledger_entry_id)), maintains a "free credit spent first" bucket so bonus spend never earns partner commission or blocks refunds, advances/reverses tiers from confirmed top-ups via pricing_credit_accruals markers, and closes rolling 30-day retention windows with a one-tier downgrade. Test coverage is sharply bimodal: the parts that were written in response to a specific incident (free-first accounting, refund eligibility, webhook dedup, durable email, content-studio workflow, sales feed) have genuinely good integration tests running against real PostgreSQL in the watchdog gate; the rest of the money loop has none at all. Concretely, of pricing.ts's 798 lines only isFreeCreditRef and part of applyPricingLedgerPage are exercised — closeElapsedTierWindows (irreversible downgrade), reconcileTopupTier/applyTopupTier (the sole tier-advancement and refund-reversal path), setReferralFloor (partner discount floor), the pricing-job lease trio, getPricingView and the entire B2B invite flow have zero tests anywhere in the repo. migrate.test.ts does NOT enforce the never-edit-a-migration rule (that lives in deploy/watchdog-lib.sh and is well tested by deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh); what it does contain is a brittle hardcoded three-file string check. All six *.integration.test.ts suites are describe.runIf(TEST_DATABASE_URL) and silently no-op to a green exit locally — the gate does set the variable (deploy/watchdog.sh:328-329), but nothing asserts they actually ran.

**Working well here:**

- **Refund-eligibility boundary discipline** — `packages/db/src/refund-eligibility.integration.test.ts:74-92` — Asserts exactly 5 days is eligible and 5 days + 1 ms is not, then separately proves that spend covered entirely by free balance (real_funded_nano = 0) does NOT void the refund while $10 of real spend does. That pair pins both halves of the user-agreement rule against a real PostgreSQL timestamp comparison, not a mocked clock.
- **Free-first accounting under adversarial page ordering** — `packages/db/src/pricing-free-first.integration.test.ts:69-77` — Feeds applyPricingLedgerPage a page whose charge (id=3) precedes its funding bonus (id=2) in the array and asserts real_funded ends at 0. Without the BigInt sort at pricing.ts:321-325 the result would be 80, so this test would actually fail on the regression it targets — and the regression it targets is over-paying partner commission on gifted credit.
- **Webhook idempotency proven by counting rows, not by return values** — `packages/db/src/payments.integration.test.ts:94-110` — Replays the identical event, then sends a second distinct event id for the same payment, and asserts payments=1, credits=1, events=2 straight from SQL. It also asserts the amount-mismatch rejection leaves zero webhook_events rows (:112-120), proving the whole transaction rolled back rather than just that a promise rejected.
- **Concurrent worker-lease exclusivity tested for real** — `packages/db/src/payments.integration.test.ts:137-143 and packages/db/src/email.integration.test.ts:38-43` — Both fire two claims via Promise.all against live PostgreSQL and assert exactly one winner. This exercises FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED semantics that no mock could reproduce, on queues that move money and auth tokens.
- **Invariant enforced at two layers and tested at both** — `packages/db/src/content-studio.integration.test.ts:88-98` — The blog-first rule is asserted through the service (BlogFirstPublicationError) and then bypassed with a raw INSERT to prove the database CHECK constraint rejects it with SQLSTATE 23514. This is the pattern the pricing code needs and does not have.
- **Cursor advance past filtered rows in the partner feed** — `packages/db/src/sales-feed.integration.test.ts:82-87 and :105-110` — Requests a page of size 1 that contains only an excluded (non-referred) row, asserts items is empty but nextCursor still advanced, then proves the following page returns the referred row. This catches the classic stalled-cursor bug that would freeze partner payouts silently.
- **Never-edit-a-committed-migration is genuinely enforced and tested** — `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:83-98` — Copies the real packages/db/migrations tree, appends a legitimate migration (accepted), then separately appends a byte to 0000_curved_skrulls.sql and mutates a historical _journal.json entry — both rejected by wd_manifest_is_append_only. The check runs before any production DB command at deploy/watchdog.sh:638. The rule is real; it is simply not in migrate.test.ts.
- **Cross-user authorization on API keys** — `apps/api/src/account.service.test.ts:79-92` — Proves bob cannot update the policy on or disable alice's key, then verifies the persisted status by SQL. Covers packages/db/src/engine.ts ownership filtering with a real negative case rather than only the happy path.

<a id="commerce-db--tier-advance-reverse-untested"></a>
#### `tier-advance-reverse-untested` — The only path that advances or reverses a customer's tier has zero tests

**CRITICAL** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `packages/db/src/pricing.ts:694`

*Not covered today.* reconcileTopupTier (packages/db/src/pricing.ts:694) has zero tests and IS live — it runs on every pricing poll via getPricingUsageCursor (packages/db/src/pricing.ts:300), which apps/worker/src/pricing-worker.service.ts:109 calls per target. The named wrapper applyTopupTier (pricing.ts:422) is, however, dead code: a repo-wide grep excluding node_modules/dist/.git finds no in-repo caller, so it should not be cited as the risk surface. Everything else in the claim (accrual CTE pricing.ts:713-730, reversal DELETE pricing.ts:731-741, saturating arithmetic pricing.ts:746-749, tierForTopups pricing.ts:43, tier-change side effects pricing.ts:755-767) is accurate and untested.

*Production risk.* This function sets the multiplier every B2C customer is billed at. A regression in the accrual CTE double-counts a confirmed credit and hands a customer a permanent 80%-off Scale multiplier they never paid for; a regression in the reversal leaves a refunded/disputed payment still counted, so a chargeback buys a permanent discount; a missed accrual means a paying customer is silently overcharged forever because later top-ups only add their own amount.

*Suggested test.* Add packages/db/src/pricing-tier.integration.test.ts (integration, real PG, same runIf pattern as pricing-free-first). Seed a b2c profile with an engine_accounts row and a paid payment + confirmed engine_credits row; call applyTopupTier and assert cumulative_topup_nano, current_tier, customer_profiles.multiplier_bp, engine_accounts.mult_bp, tier_window_start non-null and exactly one engine_pricing_jobs row. Then call it a second time and assert nothing changed (marker idempotency). Then flip payments.status to 'refunded', call again, and assert the accrual row is deleted, cumulative decreases, the tier drops and the job reason is 'b2c_refund_reversal'. Add a $99.999999999 vs $100.000000000 threshold pair against B2C_PRICING_TIERS[1].spendThresholdNano.

<a id="commerce-db--signup-bonus-cluster-untested"></a>
#### `signup-bonus-cluster-untested` — The atomic one-welcome-bonus-per-device/subnet/email guarantee is never tested

**CRITICAL** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `packages/db/src/antifraud.ts:121`

*Not covered today.* claimSignupBonus, upsertSignupProfile, releaseSignupBonus, countRecentSubnetSignups and recordDeviceSighting appear in zero test files. The partial unique indexes that make the guarantee race-proof (packages/db/migrations/0021_signup_antifraud.sql:17-19) and the constraint-name→reason mapping in duplicateReason (antifraud.ts:188-197) are unverified. apps/api/src/auth.service.test.ts:214-262 and :264-361 only assert the bonus IS granted; no test asserts a second account in the same cluster is denied.

*Production risk.* Each grant is a real $4.000000000 engine balance credit ($10 at official prices). If an index is renamed or dropped, or the constraint mapping drifts, duplicateReason returns null, the 23505 is rethrown out of claimSignupBonus, and depending on the caller either the signup 500s or the farm-detection collapses — a bonus-farming wave (the code comments date one to 2026-07-22) drains balance unbounded. releaseSignupBonus on engine failure (auth.service.ts:440) makes retry-driven repetition easy.

*Suggested test.* Integration test in packages/db (real PG, needs the indexes). Insert two users, upsertSignupProfile both with the same device_hash, claim for the first → {claimed:true}; claim for the second → assert {claimed:false, reason:'duplicate-device'} AND that signup_profiles.flagged_reason was persisted. Repeat for identical ip_subnet ('duplicate-subnet') and identical email_canonical ('duplicate-email'). Add a concurrency case: Promise.all of two claims in the same cluster, assert exactly one true. Add a case proving a flagged profile can never claim later (the AND flagged_reason IS NULL clause at antifraud.ts:126), and one proving releaseSignupBonus re-opens the claim.

*Verified absent by.* Searched: `grep -rn --include='*.test.ts' --exclude-dir=node_modules` for claimSignupBonus, upsertSignupProfile, releaseSignupBonus, countRecentSubnetSignups, recordDeviceSighting, duplicateReason, signup_profiles, device_sightings, 'duplicate-device', 'duplicate-subnet', 'duplicate-email', bonus_granted — zero hits in any test file. The only antifraud tests are packages/db/src/antifraud.unit.test.ts:1-70 (isBonusEligibleEmailDomain / canonicalizeEmail / ipSubnetOf pure functions). apps/api/src/auth.service.test.ts:214-262 and :264-361 do exercise the grant happy path indirectly (assert creditAccount called once with signup-bonus ref at :254 and :327), so claimSignupBonus's success branch is covered; the denial branches are not. Migration indexes verified present at packages/db/migrations/0021_signup_antifraud.sql:17-19 and the constraint-name mapping at packages/db/src/antifraud.ts:191-196 matches them exactly. The rethrow risk is real and unguarded: apps/api/src/auth.service.ts:289 awaits maybeGrantSignupBonus with no try/catch inside completeOAuth, so an unmapped 23505 propagates out of OAuth sign-in.

<a id="commerce-db--refund-execution-untested"></a>
#### `refund-execution-untested` — Refund execution and the delayed-paid-after-refund guard are untested while eligibility is well tested

**CRITICAL** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `packages/db/src/payments.ts:220`

*Not covered today.* payments.integration.test.ts never sends state:'refunded'. Untested: killing a pending/retry engine credit to 'dead' (payments.ts:233-239), the deliberate throw when the credit is already confirmed (payments.ts:242), the payments→'refunded' update (payments.ts:246-249), and the guard at payments.ts:153-155 that stops a late 'paid' event from recreating credit after an authoritative refund. The status CASE at payments.ts:254-257 that protects paid/refunded from being reverted to pending/canceled is also unasserted.

*Production risk.* A regression in the payments.ts:153-155 guard means a provider's delayed paid webhook after a refund re-issues an engine credit — the customer keeps the money and gets the balance. A regression in the 'dead' transition means a refunded top-up is still credited to the engine by the credit worker. Both are direct, unrecoverable cash loss on a live payment provider integration.

*Suggested test.* Extend packages/db/src/payments.integration.test.ts. Case 1: paid → refunded while the credit is still 'pending' → assert engine_credits.status='dead' with the cancellation last_error, payments.status='refunded', checkout_sessions.status='refunded'. Case 2: paid → claimNextCredit+confirmCredit → refunded → assert it rejects with /engine credit compensation/ and that nothing was mutated. Case 3: paid → refunded → a later 'paid' event with a new providerEventId → assert credits count stays 1 and checkoutStatus is 'refunded'. Case 4: paid → a 'pending' event → assert checkout status remains 'paid'.

*Verified absent by.* packages/db/src/payments.integration.test.ts has 6 `it(` blocks (lines 77, 83, 94, 112, 122, 134) covering paid / duplicate / amount-mismatch / canceled / claim+confirm only; its event() helper defaults state:'paid' (line 71) and the sole override is `state: 'canceled'` at line 125. `grep -rn --include='*.test.ts' refund .` across the repo returns only packages/payments/src/{cryptomus,platega}.test.ts (provider status→state mapping, e.g. platega.test.ts:169) and packages/db/src/refund-eligibility.integration.test.ts (which never calls applyVerifiedCheckoutPaymentEvent with state:'refunded' — its paidCheckout helper at :47-58 only sends 'paid'). All four cited source regions verified: payments.ts:153 guard, :220 refunded branch, :233-239 'dead' transition, :242 throw, :246-249 payments update, :254-257 status CASE.

<a id="commerce-db--ledger-page-replay-idempotency"></a>

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `packages/db/src/pricing.ts:341`

*Not covered today.* pricing.ts:338-340 documents that free top-ups have no ON CONFLICT protection and rely solely on the startCursor comparison at pricing.ts:353 to avoid being applied twice. No test re-submits an already-consumed page. Also untested: the pricing_months upsert (pricing.ts:382-388) and the reachedStablePageEnd flag (pricing.ts:396) that gates the completion marker window closure depends on.

Source lines verified: cursor read pricing.ts:341-343, guard `if (ledgerId <= startCursor) continue` at :350, pricing_months upsert :382-388, reachedStablePageEnd :396.

<a id="commerce-db--referral-floor-untested"></a>
#### `referral-floor-untested` — The partner discount floor — written by three independent callers — has no test

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `packages/db/src/pricing.ts:193`

*Not covered today.* setReferralFloor has zero tests. Untested: the 0..9500 RangeError (pricing.ts:202-204), the b2c-only / current_tier-not-null skip (pricing.ts:220-223), the monotonic GREATEST merge vs the explicit override branch (pricing.ts:229-231), the no-op early return (pricing.ts:233-236), and that customer_profiles.multiplier_bp, engine_accounts.mult_bp and the enqueued job all agree.

*Production risk.* Three callers (auth.service.ts:152 signup, promo.service.ts:88, sales-feed.controller.ts:124) write the same column; the GREATEST merge exists precisely because an earlier absolute write let them clobber each other and silently strip a customer's better discount. A regression either quietly raises a partner referral's price (support/refund exposure) or, on the override branch, lets an automatic replay lower a floor to 95% off for accounts that were never entitled.

*Suggested test.* Integration test: set floor 5000 then 3000 without override → assert floor stays 5000 and multiplier_bp = min(tierMult, 5000). Then floor 3000 with override:true → assert it drops to 3000. Then floor 0 → assert the floor clears and multiplier returns to the pure tier value. Assert engine_accounts.mult_bp matches customer_profiles.multiplier_bp in every case and that exactly one engine_pricing_jobs row exists with reason 'referral_floor'. Add a b2b profile → assert {applied:false} and no mutation. Add floorBps 9501 and 3.5 → RangeError.

*Verified absent by.* `grep -rn --include='*.test.ts' --exclude-dir=node_modules 'setReferralFloor\|floorBps\|referral_floor'` → zero hits (the 'referral' hits that do exist are all in packages/sales-db/* partner tables and apps/web analytics, unrelated to customer_profiles.referral_floor_bps). packages/db/src/sales-feed.integration.test.ts only covers listPaidTopupsAfter/listUsageEventsAfter (2 tests at :67 and :90). All cited source lines verified: RangeError pricing.ts:202-204, b2b/current_tier skip :220-223, GREATEST vs override :229-231, no-op early return :233-236, the three writes (customer_profiles :237-241, engine_accounts :242-244, enqueuePricingJob :246-252). The three callers exist: apps/api/src/auth.service.ts:152, apps/api/src/promo.service.ts:88, apps/api/src/sales-feed.controller.ts:124.

<a id="commerce-db--pricing-job-lease-untested"></a>
#### `pricing-job-lease-untested` — Durable multiplier delivery to the engine (claim/confirm/retry) is untested

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `packages/db/src/pricing.ts:517`

*Not covered today.* claimNextPricingJob, confirmPricingJob, retryPricingJob and recoverStalePricingJobs have no tests. The confirm and retry statements each repeat a five-clause CASE distinguishing 'confirmed' from 'superseded_after_processing' (pricing.ts:573-583, :598-609); enqueuePricingJob's refusal to disturb an in-flight lease (pricing.ts:667-671) and the expired-lease recovery folded into claiming (pricing.ts:527-533) are likewise unverified.

*Production risk.* If confirm marks a superseded job 'confirmed', the engine keeps the old multiplier permanently and every request for that customer is billed at the wrong price — silently, since commerce believes it synced. If the lease-recovery UPDATE regresses, a crashed worker strands jobs in 'processing' and no tier change ever reaches the engine.

*Suggested test.* Integration test: enqueue a job, claim it with worker-a, change customer_profiles.multiplier_bp underneath it, then confirmPricingJob → assert status='pending', reason='superseded_after_processing', attempts=0. Repeat without the underlying change → assert 'confirmed' with confirmed_at set. Backdate locked_at by 10 minutes and claim from worker-b → assert reclamation. Promise.all two claims on one job → assert exactly one winner.

*Verified absent by.* `grep -rn --include='*.test.ts' --exclude-dir=node_modules` for claimNextPricingJob, confirmPricingJob, retryPricingJob, recoverStalePricingJobs, enqueuePricingJob, engine_pricing_jobs, superseded_after_processing → zero hits. The live consumer apps/worker/src/pricing-worker.service.ts:359-378 (flushPricingJobs) has no test file (apps/worker/src has only config.test.ts and email-template.test.ts). Cited source verified: lease recovery folded into claiming at pricing.ts:527-533, the confirm CASE ladder at :573-583, the retry CASE ladder at :598-609, and enqueuePricingJob's in-flight-lease bail at :667-671.

<a id="commerce-db--b2b-invite-untested"></a>
#### `b2b-invite-untested` — One-time business invite (up to 95% discount) consumption is untested

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `packages/db/src/pricing.ts:69`

*Not covered today.* lockBusinessInvite/createBusinessInvite/setBusinessPricing genuinely have no tests and no test registers with an inviteToken — that part is confirmed. But the sub-claim that 'auth.ts:95-98 consumes it without checking rowCount' is a single-use hole misreads the code: the SELECT at pricing.ts:73-79 takes FOR UPDATE inside the same transaction that later performs the UPDATE, so a concurrent second redemption blocks on the row lock and then fails the `consumed_at IS NULL` predicate on re-read. The unchecked rowCount is redundant, not the guarantee. Real risk is therefore a silent regression in the WHERE predicate (expiry / email match), not unbounded concurrent redemption.

*Production risk.* A regression lets one leaked invite token provision unlimited near-free B2B accounts, or lets an expired/wrong-email invite be redeemed. At 95% off official pricing this is effectively free inference at our subscription cost.

*Suggested test.* Integration test in packages/db: create an invite, register a user with it, assert customer_type='b2b', current_tier IS NULL, multiplier_bp equals the invite value, consumed_at set and consumed_by_user_id correct. Then attempt a second registration with the same token → InvalidBusinessInvitationError. Add cases for an expired invite, a case-differing email (must succeed), and a different email (must fail). Add Promise.all of two concurrent redemptions → exactly one succeeds.

<a id="commerce-db--pricing-ladder-duplicated-unpinned"></a>
#### `pricing-ladder-duplicated-unpinned` — The billing pricing ladder is duplicated in web and pinned by a test only on the display copy

**MEDIUM** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `packages/contracts/src/index.ts:255`

*Not covered today.* B2C_PRICING_TIERS (thresholds, multiplierBp, holdNano, discountPercent) is what packages/db/src/pricing.ts bills from, and packages/contracts has no test script at all (packages/contracts/package.json:14-17), so --if-present skips it. apps/web/src/lib/pricing-tiers.ts:5-11 is a second, independent copy of the same ladder, and apps/web/src/lib/pricing-tiers.test.ts:6-12 pins only that copy. Nothing asserts the two agree, and nothing asserts the internal invariant multiplierBp === 10000 - discountPercent*100 inside the billing table.

*Production risk.* Editing discountPercent without multiplierBp (or updating web without contracts) makes getPricingView (pricing.ts:119-120) advertise one discount while the engine multiplier charges another — every affected invoice is wrong and the marketing page contradicts the bill. The web-side test passes throughout.

*Suggested test.* Add packages/db/src/pricing-tiers.unit.test.ts (unit, runs with no DB) asserting for every entry of B2C_PRICING_TIERS: multiplierBp === 10000 - discountPercent*100, holdNano === spendThresholdNano/2, strictly ascending spendThresholdNano, strictly descending multiplierBp, and tierForTopups at threshold-1 / threshold / threshold+1 for each tier. Separately assert B2C_PRICING_TIERS and apps/web's B2C_PRICING_MILESTONES agree on code/discountPercent/spendThresholdNano (or delete the duplicate and import from contracts).

*Verified absent by.* packages/contracts/package.json:14-17 has only build/typecheck scripts — no `test`, so `pnpm -r --if-present test` (deploy/watchdog.sh:328-329) skips the package entirely. The billing table is packages/contracts/src/index.ts:255-261; the independent display copy is apps/web/src/lib/pricing-tiers.ts:5-11; the only test pinning it is apps/web/src/lib/pricing-tiers.test.ts:6-12, which asserts against B2C_PRICING_MILESTONES only. `grep -rn --include='*.test.ts' B2C_PRICING_TIERS .` → zero hits repo-wide, so neither the cross-copy agreement nor multiplierBp === 10000 - discountPercent*100 is asserted anywhere. apps/web/src/public-product-truth.test.ts reads only page copy files, not contracts. getPricingView's advertised fields verified at packages/db/src/pricing.ts:118-120.
