*Verified absent by.* apps/worker/src/config.test.ts (50 lines, read in full) contains one validateEnvironment call at :22-27 asserting a VALID production environment parses, and three smtpSecurityOptions unit assertions at :29-49. There is no `assert.throws` / rejection case anywhere in the file, so none of the four superRefine branches at apps/worker/src/config.ts:29-53 is negatively exercised — including the PUBLIC_APP_BASE_URL origin lock at :38-53, which is what constrains the verify-email/reset-password links minted at apps/worker/src/email-worker.service.ts:107. Closest cross-coverage is a DIFFERENT schema and variable: apps/api/src/config.test.ts:57-60 negatively tests the GITHUB_REDIRECT_URI canonical-callback refinement, which proves the pattern is exercised in the API but says nothing about the worker's origin lock. Severity low stands (the other three refinements fail closed at boot).

### 8.11 `sales` — Sales/partner platform (apps/sales-api, packages/sales-db)

This domain runs a multi-level partner/affiliate program: partners onboard via signed Telegram Login, get a referral code, and earn `commission_bps` of their referred users' real-money spend, with overrides cascading up to 10 levels of parents. Money accrues into `commission_entries` in semi-monthly UTC periods (7-day lock, 3-day payout window) and is paid out as real USDT-BEP20 on BNB Chain from a hot wallet driven by `apps/sales-api/src/payout/payout.service.ts`. Test coverage is sharply bimodal: the *pure* and *read-model* layers are genuinely well tested (periods.ts, computeCommissionChain, the due-list/cabinet "committed ≠ paid" invariants, and most of the payout-row state machine at the SQL level), and several tests pin exactly the invariants whose violation means double payment. But everything that *composes* those primitives is untested: `payout.service.ts` (452 LOC, the on-chain sender that decides nonces, reserves before broadcast, and stops on uncertainty) has zero tests; `partner.controller.ts` (513) and `admin.controller.ts` (481) have no adjacent test file and are the *only* enforcement point for partner commission/discount ceilings; promo redemption and one-shot discount links — the two paths that hand out free money and permanent price floors — have no test at any level in the repo. All five sales-db integration suites are `describe.runIf(Boolean(TEST_SALES_DATABASE_URL))`, so they silently no-op on every developer machine and only actually run inside the watchdog gate (`deploy/watchdog.sh:328`).

**Working well here:**

- **Double-payment invariants at the payout-row level** — `packages/sales-db/src/payout-batch.integration.test.ts:126` — "a released (rejected) row is NOT sendable" and the companion at :143 ("a broadcast row must NOT be releasable") test the two directions of the single most dangerous transition pair in the whole domain. They are written against real Postgres, assert on the actual filter in listSendablePayouts, and would fail immediately if someone widened releasePayoutRow's chain_status predicate or dropped 'rejected' from the sendable filter — the precise edits that turn release→re-send into a duplicate on-chain transfer.
- **"Committed ≠ paid" accounting across both the admin due list and the partner cabinet** — `packages/sales-db/src/payout-periods.integration.test.ts:64` — The SEV1/SEV2/SEV3 cases pin the subtraction of requested+approved+paid (not just paid) in getDuePayoutList and getPartnerPeriodState, and the mirror case at :84 pins that 'rejected' does NOT reduce payable. This is the exact bug class where a partner with a payout in flight is re-listed and paid twice, and it is asserted from both surfaces with real rows, on a frozen date so the assertions are exact strings rather than ranges.
- **Period / lock / window arithmetic** — `packages/sales-db/src/periods.test.ts:72` — phaseOf is asserted at the exact instant of every transition (07-16T00:00:00 locked, 07-22T23:59:59 still locked, 07-23T00:00:00 payable, 07-26T00:00:00 closed) rather than at comfortable midpoints, plus a next/previous inverse property over three seeds at :55 and a December→January rollover at :48. Off-by-one-day errors in the 7-day lock — which would release money early — cannot survive this file.
- **Commission chain math** — `packages/sales-db/src/commissions.test.ts:29` — "floors integer division at every level" uses 101 nano at 3333/3333 bps and asserts [33n, 10n], i.e. it proves the floor is applied per level rather than once at the end — the difference between compounding rounding correctly and silently over-paying. The suite also pins the exact 10-level cap with a 15-partner chain (:67), zero-amount short-circuit (:38), and that both 'suspended' and 'pending' stop the walk without creating an entry.
- **Offline transaction determinism as the double-pay defence** — `apps/sales-api/src/payout/chain.unit.test.ts:18` — Asserting that two signTransfer calls with identical (recipient, amount, nonce) yield the identical raw and hash is precisely the property the re-broadcast strategy in payout.service.ts relies on, and the companion at :27 proves a different nonce yields a different hash. Small file, but it tests the load-bearing claim rather than the easy one.
- **Telegram login signature verification** — `apps/sales-api/src/telegram.test.ts:36` — The test builds the HMAC independently (its own sign() helper reimplementing the Telegram spec) instead of calling the code under test, then checks a tampered id, a wrong bot token, a stale auth_date beyond the max-age window, and a malformed hash. Since Telegram identity is the only way into the partner portal, this is exactly the right place to have real crypto assertions.
- **Pre-attribution event buffering and replay idempotency** — `packages/sales-db/src/reconcile.integration.test.ts:65` — "leaves the buffer untouched while the user is still unattributed, and is idempotent" covers the ordering hazard that a spend can arrive before the attribution row exists, verifies double-buffering the same commerce_event_id creates no second row, and verifies a second reconcile pass is a no-op. That is the difference between losing a partner's commission forever and paying it twice.
- **Feed cursor safety** — `apps/sales-api/src/sync-feed.test.ts:29` — "rejects a producer cursor that would skip a returned item" tests a defensive check most codebases would not have written at all: if commerce ever returns nextCursor behind its own items, sales refuses rather than silently skipping usage events (= permanently lost commissions). The legacy-array compatibility case at :16 also pins the rolling-deploy contract.

<a id="sales--payout-send-composition-untested"></a>
#### `payout-send-composition-untested` — The entire on-chain send path (PayoutService) has zero tests

**CRITICAL** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `apps/sales-api/src/payout/payout.service.ts:336`

*Not covered today.* sendRow (:336), send (:258), sendOne (:288), confirmBroadcasts (:395) and computeStartNonce (:236) are not driven by any test. apps/sales-api/src/payout/ contains exactly one test file, chain.unit.test.ts (59 lines), which only exercises offline signing and address helpers. The SQL primitives these functions compose are tested individually in payout-batch.integration.test.ts, but the composition — which is where every double-pay/lost-pay decision is made — is not: the reserve-before-broadcast check (`if (!reserved) return` at :359-360), the stop-the-queue-on-uncertainty rule (:373 and :390), the never-re-sign-with-a-new-nonce rule (:363-376 broadcasts the SAME raw), the 'already known'/'nonce too low' success classification (:367), and the nonce accounting (only +1 on a definitive on-chain outcome).

*Production risk.* Real USDT leaves a hot wallet here. A regression that bumps the nonce after a broadcast whose fate is unknown, or that re-signs a timed-out transfer with a fresh nonce, pays the same partner twice on-chain — irreversible, with no chargeback. Conversely, treating a genuine 'already known' as a failure and re-signing produces two competing transactions for the same payout. Neither failure is detectable by any current test; the first evidence would be an unexplained hot-wallet drain.

*Suggested test.* Integration tests in apps/sales-api/src/payout/payout.service.test.ts against TEST_SALES_DATABASE_URL, with a fake PayoutChain injected (this requires adding a seam — getChain() at :103 currently news up the real class from config, so expose an optional chain override in the constructor or a protected factory). Drive sendRow through each branch and assert both the returned {nextNonce, stop} and the persisted row: (a) simulate throws → status stays requested, chain_status failed, nonce NOT consumed, row still sendable; (b) markPayoutBroadcast returns false because the row was released concurrently → broadcastRaw is never called (assert the fake recorded zero broadcasts); (c) broadcastRaw throws 'already known' → treated as success, waits for confirmation; (d) broadcastRaw throws SEND_RETRIES+1 times → returns stop:true, tx_hash and raw_tx retained, nextNonce unchanged; (e) waitForConfirmation returns null (timeout) → stop:true, row left broadcast; (f) returns reverted → markPayoutFailed and nextNonce+1. Then a send() loop test asserting that after a stop the following rows are untouched, and a computeStartNonce test where getMaxOutstandingNonce(7) beats a stale pendingNonce(3) → 8.

*Verified absent by.* Verified line numbers all exact: apps/sales-api/src/payout/payout.service.ts:236 computeStartNonce, :258 send, :288 sendOne, :336 sendRow, :359-360 `if (!reserved) return`, :367 already-known classification, :373/:390 stop-the-queue, :382/:387 nonce+1 only on definitive outcome, :395 confirmBroadcasts. `rg -n 'PayoutService|payout.service' --glob '!node_modules'` returns ONLY payout.service.ts, payout.module.ts, payout.controller.ts — no test constructs it. apps/sales-api/src/payout/ contains exactly one test: chain.unit.test.ts (59 lines, verified) which only calls signTransfer/nanoToUsdtWei/normalizeBscAddress. packages/sales-db/src/payout-batch.integration.test.ts (153 lines, read in full) covers the SQL primitives individually (markPayoutBroadcast at :84, markPayoutConfirmed :91, releasePayoutRow :135/:151) but never the composition. `find apps/sales-api packages/sales-db -name '*test*'` outside dist/ confirms the full inventory: 4 sales-api tests + 7 sales-db tests, none of them a PayoutService driver.

<a id="sales--promo-redemption-untested"></a>
#### `promo-redemption-untested` — Promo redemption — the free-money path — is untested end to end

**CRITICAL** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `packages/sales-db/src/promo.ts:162`

*Not covered today.* redeemPromoCode has no test anywhere in the repo (repo-wide grep for `redeemPromoCode` returns only promo.ts and internal.controller.ts:68), and neither does its commerce-side caller apps/api/src/promo.service.ts:110 (no promo.service.test.ts exists). Untested invariants: one promo per commerce user (the explicit prior-redemption check at :212 plus the 23505 partial-unique fallback at :231); idempotent re-redemption returning the SAME redemption_ref (:196), which commerce uses as the engine credit idempotency key; the freeze kill-switch that blocks redemption when the partner is suspended or promo_enabled was revoked (:207); case-insensitive lookup under `FOR UPDATE OF pc` (:178-179). createPromoCode's limits (max value :86, max count :99, discount ceiling :91) are equally untested.

*Production risk.* redemption_ref is the idempotency key for a real balance credit. If a change makes the already-redeemed branch return a fresh ref (or if the prior-redemption check regresses under concurrency), the same promo credits a user's balance twice — direct issuance of free API quota. If the count/value limit check regresses, a partner mints unlimited free credit against the platform's margin. If the suspended-partner check regresses, a partner caught defrauding can still have their outstanding codes redeemed.

*Suggested test.* Add packages/sales-db/src/promo.integration.test.ts (same TEST_SALES_DATABASE_URL fixture style as reconcile.integration.test.ts): redeem once → recorded ref; redeem the same code by the same user → alreadyRedeemed true and byte-identical redemptionRef; redeem the same code by a different user → PromoAlreadyRedeemedError; redeem a second, different code by a user who already redeemed → UserAlreadyRedeemedError; suspend the partner, then redeem an active code → PromoAlreadyRedeemedError; two concurrent redeem calls for two different codes by one user (Promise.allSettled) → exactly one fulfilled. Plus createPromoCode: value above promo_max_value_nano → PromoLimitError; the (max_count)th code succeeds and the (max_count+1)th throws; discountBps > referral_discount_bps or without referral_discount_enabled → PromoNotAllowedError.

*Verified absent by.* `rg -n 'redeemPromoCode|createPromoCode|disablePromoCode' --glob '!node_modules' -l` → packages/sales-db/src/promo.ts, apps/sales-api/src/internal.controller.ts, apps/sales-api/src/partner.controller.ts. Zero test files. All cited lines verified in promo.ts: :86 max value, :91 discount ceiling, :99 max count, :162 redeemPromoCode, :178-179 `upper(pc.code)=upper($1) FOR UPDATE OF pc`, :196 redemptionRef reuse, :207 suspended/promo_enabled kill-switch, :212-219 one-promo-per-user, :231 23505 fallback. The only tests touching promo_codes insert rows via raw SQL and never call the functions: packages/sales-db/src/analytics.integration.test.ts:66 and :110, packages/sales-db/src/partner-lifecycle.integration.test.ts:41. Commerce caller apps/api/src/promo.service.ts exists (consumeInSales at :107); `rg 'promo.service|PromoService' apps/api/src --glob '*.test.ts'` returns nothing and no apps/api/src/promo.service.test.ts exists.

<a id="sales--partner-margin-caps-controller-only"></a>
#### `partner-margin-caps-controller-only` — Partner commission and discount ceilings are enforced only in an untested controller

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `apps/sales-api/src/partner.controller.ts:253`

*Not covered today.* `Math.min(parsed.data.commissionBps ?? cap, cap)` at :253 is the ONLY thing stopping a partner from issuing a sub-partner invite at a higher rate than their own — createPartnerInvite (packages/sales-db/src/invites.ts:73) writes whatever bps it is given and has no ceiling check, and createInviteSchema (apps/sales-api/src/schemas.ts) only bounds it to 0..10000. The same pattern holds for the discount cascade at :256-260 (a partner without referralDiscountEnabled must not be able to grant it) and for the per-referral rate override ceiling at :186 (`discountBps > current.partner.referralDiscountBps` → 422), where commerce is called with override:true and applies whatever floor it receives. partner.controller.ts has no adjacent test file.

*Production risk.* A partner posting {"commissionBps": 10000} to /v1/partner/invites would, if the Math.min regresses, create a sub-partner earning 100% of their referred users' spend — the platform pays out more than it collects on every request those users make, silently and permanently until someone reads the ledger. The discount-ceiling variant lets a partner set a referred customer's price floor below what the admin authorised, giving away platform margin from the partner's own downline.

*Suggested test.* Unit tests over the controller class (instantiate PartnerController directly with stub SalesDatabase/ConfigService/CommerceService — no HTTP needed) in apps/sales-api/src/partner.controller.test.ts: assert createInvite passes commissionBps = min(requested, current.partner.commissionBps) to createPartnerInvite for requested above, equal to, and below the cap; assert subDiscountEnabled is false and subDiscountBps is 0 when the inviting partner lacks referralDiscountEnabled even if the body requests them; assert setReferralDiscount throws UnprocessableEntityException before CommerceService is called when discountBps exceeds the partner's ceiling, and ForbiddenException when referralDiscountEnabled is false. Assert on the arguments captured by the stub, not on the return value.

*Verified absent by.* apps/sales-api/src/partner.controller.ts:253 `Math.min(parsed.data.commissionBps ?? cap, cap)`, :256-260 discount cascade, :186 `discountBps > current.partner.referralDiscountBps` → 422 — all verified verbatim. packages/sales-db/src/invites.ts:71-104 createPartnerInvite is a bare INSERT with no ceiling. I additionally checked the DOWNSTREAM consumption path for a second ceiling and found none: packages/sales-db/src/auth.ts:207-208 writes `invite.commissionBps ?? input.defaultCommissionBps` straight into partners, and packages/sales-db/src/applications.ts:135 likewise — so the controller Math.min really is the only guard. `rg 'createPartnerInvite|commissionBps' --glob '*.test.ts'` hits ONLY packages/sales-db/src/commissions.test.ts:5,10,18,31,40,69 (pure computeCommissionChain fixtures). No apps/sales-api/src/partner.controller.test.ts exists. Minor correction to the claim's wording: createInviteSchema bounds commissionBps 0..10000 (schemas.ts:7) but referralDiscountBps to 0..9500 (schemas.ts:9), not 10000 — does not change the finding.

<a id="sales--multi-level-commission-persistence"></a>
#### `multi-level-commission-persistence` — Multi-level commission is proven only as pure math, never as persisted rows

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `packages/sales-db/src/commissions.ts:149`

*Not covered today.* computeCommissionChain is well covered, but recordReferredSpend — which loads the real chain via loadCommissionChain (:68, walking parent_partner_id) and writes commission_entries (:186-191) — is exercised by exactly one integration suite (reconcile.integration.test.ts) and only with a single partner having no parent and commission_bps 1000. No test ever creates a parent/grandparent and asserts that level 1 and level 2 rows land on the right partners with the right applied_bps. The ordering contract (partnersChain[0] must be the DIRECT referrer) is asserted nowhere against the DB. Also untested: the 'duplicate' return path (:180-183) that rolls back without writing entries, and the ON CONFLICT (usage_event_id, partner_id) idempotency at :189.

*Production risk.* If loadCommissionChain ever returned the chain reversed or off by one, every commission in the system would be computed with the wrong partner's bps — the direct referrer would be paid the parent's sub_commission_bps and vice versa. The pure test suite passes unchanged because it is handed a pre-ordered array. Similarly, a break in the duplicate short-circuit would double-credit every replayed feed row, and since the sync loop retries whole pages on failure, replays are routine, not exceptional.

*Suggested test.* Extend packages/sales-db/src/reconcile.integration.test.ts (or a new commissions.integration.test.ts): build P0 (1500 bps) ← P1 (2000 sub) ← P2 (500 sub) via parent_partner_id, attribute a user to P0, call recordReferredSpend with $100, then assert the three commission_entries rows by (partner_id, level, applied_bps, amount_nano) exactly — P0 level 0 $15, P1 level 1 $3, P2 level 2 $0.15. Call it again with the same commerceEventId and assert it returns "duplicate" and the entry count is unchanged. Suspend P1 and record a second event, asserting only a level-0 row appears.

*Verified absent by.* Verified packages/sales-db/src/commissions.ts:68 loadCommissionChain (walks parent_partner_id at :89), :149 recordReferredSpend, :180-183 duplicate rollback, :186-191 INSERT with ON CONFLICT (usage_event_id, partner_id) at :189. `rg 'recordReferredSpend' --glob '!node_modules'` → only commissions.ts, sync.service.ts:191, and packages/sales-db/src/reconcile.integration.test.ts (lines 37, 67, 73). Read reconcile.integration.test.ts in full (84 lines): its only partner factory is line 22, `INSERT INTO partners (referral_code,status,telegram_username,commission_bps) VALUES ($1,'active',$1,1000)` — no parent_partner_id column, so every case is a single flat partner. `rg 'parent_partner_id|parentPartnerId'` across all tests hits exactly one place: analytics.integration.test.ts:24 — and that suite only asserts `teamSize: 1` (line 80) via listPartnerAnalytics; it inserts commission_entries by raw SQL (lines 47-50) and never calls recordReferredSpend. So level-1/level-2 persistence and chain ORDERING are asserted nowhere against the DB. The duplicate path is also genuinely uncovered: reconcile.integration.test.ts:73 calls recordReferredSpend twice with the same commerceEventId but the user is UNATTRIBUTED, so both calls take the `buffered` branch at commissions.ts:164-168 and never reach the `duplicate` return at :182.

<a id="sales--session-authz-and-cross-partner-isolation"></a>
#### `session-authz-and-cross-partner-isolation` — Nothing tests that a session is required, still valid, or scoped to its own partner

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `packages/sales-db/src/auth.ts:272`

*Not covered today.* resolvePartnerSession's WHERE clause — `revoked_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now() AND p.status = 'active'` — is the entire kill switch for logout, session expiry, and partner suspension, and it has no test. SessionAuthGuard (apps/sales-api/src/auth.guard.ts:21) with its hand-rolled readCookie (:45) and the 43-char token regex gate (apps/sales-api/src/auth.service.ts:183) are equally untested. On the data side, the partner-scoping of deleteDiscountLink (discount-links.ts:109), disablePromoCode (promo.ts:138) and resolveReferredUserByPrefix (referrals.ts:44) is a `AND partner_id = $1` predicate with no test asserting that partner B's id fails to match partner A's row.

*Production risk.* A suspended partner — suspension being the fraud response — keeps full cabinet access, including changing their payout wallet before the next batch, if the `p.status = 'active'` predicate is dropped. A dropped partner_id predicate in deleteDiscountLink/disablePromoCode lets any authenticated partner destroy a competitor's live discount links by id, and in resolveReferredUserByPrefix lets a partner set the price floor on another partner's customer (an 8-hex prefix is guessable in bulk).

*Suggested test.* Two layers. (1) packages/sales-db/src/auth.integration.test.ts: create a session, resolve it (ok); revoke it → null; expire it (expires_at in the past) → null; set the partner to suspended → null; assert the happy path also bumps last_seen_at. (2) A cross-partner isolation suite: create partners A and B each with a discount link, a promo code and a referred user; assert deleteDiscountLink(db, B, linkOfA) is false and the row survives, disablePromoCode(db, B, promoOfA) is false and status stays active, and resolveReferredUserByPrefix(db, B, prefixOfAsUser) returns null. Add a guard unit test for readCookie covering a cookie header with multiple pairs, whitespace, and a value containing '='.

*Verified absent by.* packages/sales-db/src/auth.ts:272 is exactly `WHERE s.token_hash = $1 AND s.revoked_at IS NULL AND s.expires_at > now() AND p.status = 'active'`. `rg 'resolvePartnerSession|SessionAuthGuard|createPartnerSession|revokePartnerSession'` returns only source files (auth.ts, apps/sales-api/src/auth.guard.ts:21, auth.service.ts:14/184/189, auth.controller.ts, partner.controller.ts:89) plus docs — no test. auth.guard.ts readCookie is at :45-52 and the 43-char gate `/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}$/` at auth.service.ts:183, both untested. Partner-scoping predicates verified at the cited lines: discount-links.ts:109-115 `DELETE ... WHERE id=$1 AND partner_id=$2`, promo.ts:138-144, referrals.ts:44. No test file imports any of them (`rg 'deleteDiscountLink|resolveReferredUserByPrefix' --glob '!node_modules'` → only partner.controller.ts:442/:189 and admin.controller.ts:220). Note the only session row a test ever creates is a raw INSERT for the activity feed (analytics.integration.test.ts:111), which asserts a 'login' event type, not authz. Nearest analogue in another layer is apps/api (commerce) session tests, which cover a different guard class in a different app and do not exercise sales-db's predicate.

<a id="sales--one-shot-discount-link-race"></a>
#### `one-shot-discount-link-race` — The one-shot discount link claim is documented as race-proof but never tested

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `packages/sales-db/src/discount-links.ts:165`

*Not covered today.* claimReferralDiscount's doc comment states the link "НИКОГДА не даёт скидку более чем одному пользователю", and it is the atomic replacement for a previously racy resolve+consume pair. There is no test. The predicate that carries the guarantee — `(dl.consumed_by_commerce_user_id IS NULL OR dl.consumed_by_commerce_user_id = $2)` at :171 — plus the active-partner join and the COALESCE that keeps the original consumed_at on idempotent re-claims are all unverified. resolveReferralCode (:130) — which decides whether a ?ref= code is a plain partner code or a link, and returns discountBps 0 for an already-consumed link (:150) — is likewise untested, as is the caller wiring in apps/sales-api/src/sync.service.ts:122-133 that only claims when the user is owned by that partner.

*Production risk.* A discount link grants a permanent price floor on a customer account. If the claim ever stops being single-winner, one link handed out publicly discounts every user who registers through it — an unbounded, retroactive margin giveaway that no alert would catch because each individual account looks legitimately discounted. The inverse regression (the owner's idempotent re-claim returning 0) silently drops the floor a customer was promised.

*Suggested test.* packages/sales-db/src/discount-links.integration.test.ts: create an active partner with referral_discount_enabled and a link at 3000 bps; call claimReferralDiscount concurrently for two different user ids via Promise.all and assert exactly one result has discountBps 3000 and the other 0, and that consumed_by_commerce_user_id equals the winner; re-claim by the winner → still 3000 and consumed_at unchanged; suspend the partner then claim → 0; resolveReferralCode on the consumed link → discountBps 0, discountLinkId set, discountLinkConsumed true; resolveReferralCode on the partner's plain referral_code → discountBps 0, discountLinkId null.

*Verified absent by.* Verified packages/sales-db/src/discount-links.ts:165 claimReferralDiscount with the single-winner predicate at :171 `AND (dl.consumed_by_commerce_user_id IS NULL OR dl.consumed_by_commerce_user_id = $2)`, the `COALESCE(dl.consumed_at, now())` idempotent-reclaim, and the `JOIN partners p ... p.status='active'` guard; resolveReferralCode at :130 with the consumed→0 branch at :150. `rg 'claimReferralDiscount|resolveReferralCode' --glob '!node_modules'` returns only discount-links.ts, index.ts, apps/sales-api/src/sync.service.ts:108/:129 and apps/sales-api/src/internal.controller.ts:120 — no test file. Caller wiring at sync.service.ts:122-133 (`ownerPartnerId === resolved.partnerId && resolved.discountLinkId`) verified and also untested. The only discount-link rows any test touches are raw INSERTs for analytics counters (analytics.integration.test.ts:65, :109) and the delete guard (partner-lifecycle.integration.test.ts:47).

<a id="sales--payout-batch-concurrency-and-nonce-reservation"></a>
#### `payout-batch-concurrency-and-nonce-reservation` — Batch-prepare serialization is asserted sequentially, and the nonce/stuck-batch helpers are untested

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `packages/sales-db/src/payout-batch.ts:134`

*Not covered today.* The pg_advisory_xact_lock at :134 exists specifically because "two parallel prepare() calls could create two batches and pay everyone twice" (its own comment). The test at payout-batch.integration.test.ts:55 creates the two batches sequentially, so it is satisfied by the plain `SELECT 1 FROM payout_batches` existence check alone — delete the advisory lock and the test still passes. Separately, getMaxOutstandingNonce (:266), acquirePayoutSendLock/releasePayoutSendLock (:274/:286) and finalizeStuckSendingBatches (:212) are not imported by any test.

*Production risk.* Two admin browser tabs (or two HA instances) clicking Prepare at the same moment create two batches over the same unpaid balance; every partner is then paid twice on-chain. getMaxOutstandingNonce is what prevents reusing a nonce a public read-RPC cannot yet see — losing it produces either a replaced (dropped) transfer or a stuck queue mid-window. finalizeStuckSendingBatches is the anti-DoS release for a batch left in 'sending' by a crash; without it every future prepare() is blocked and no partner gets paid at all.

*Suggested test.* In payout-batch.integration.test.ts, replace the sequential assertion with a genuinely concurrent one: Promise.allSettled of two createPayoutBatch calls for the same recipient set, then assert exactly one fulfilled, one rejected with PayoutBatchInProgressError, and `SELECT count(*) FROM payout_batches` = 1 and `count(*) FROM payouts` = recipients.length. Add: getMaxOutstandingNonce returns null with no broadcast rows and the max nonce once two rows are broadcast at nonces 4 and 9; acquirePayoutSendLock twice from two clients → second returns null, and returns a client again after release; finalizeStuckSendingBatches flips a 'sending' batch with no broadcast rows to 'sent' (and to completed_at set only when every row is paid) while leaving a batch that still has a broadcast row alone.

*Verified absent by.* packages/sales-db/src/payout-batch.ts:134 `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock($1)` verified, with the existence check at :135-137. The claim's core point holds and is stronger than stated: payout-batch.integration.test.ts:66-70 awaits the first createPayoutBatch to completion before issuing the second, so the advisory lock is never contended and the `SELECT 1 FROM payout_batches WHERE status IN ('preparing','prepared','sending')` check alone satisfies the assertion — no test would fail if line 134 were deleted. Verified helper line numbers all exact: finalizeStuckSendingBatches :212, getMaxOutstandingNonce :266, acquirePayoutSendLock :274, releasePayoutSendLock :286. The test file's import block (lines 4-10) does NOT include any of the four, and `rg 'getMaxOutstandingNonce|acquirePayoutSendLock|releasePayoutSendLock|finalizeStuckSendingBatches' packages/sales-db/src/payout-batch.integration.test.ts` returns nothing — their only consumers are payout.service.ts:239/:246/:253/:426, itself untested (see claim 1).

<a id="sales--admin-and-internal-key-guards-untested"></a>
#### `admin-and-internal-key-guards-untested` — Both API keys guarding money operations are unverified

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/sales-api/src/admin.guard.ts:14`

*Not covered today.* apps/sales-api's AdminKeyGuard (admin.guard.ts:11) and InternalKeyGuard (internal.controller.ts:38) have no unit test for their accept/reject paths, unlike the equivalent commerce AdminGuard which is covered at apps/api/src/admin-operations.controller.test.ts:60. The header-name contract is not entirely unpinned — deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:451 asserts Caddy renders `x-sales-admin-key` — but nothing asserts the guard rejects `x-admin-key`, an array-valued header, or an empty string.

*Production risk.* An unauthenticated caller reaching the payout endpoints can prepare and send a real on-chain batch. The header-name choice is load-bearing infrastructure — the Caddy panel vhost injects it server-side — so a rename or a fallback added "for convenience" would either lock the operator out mid-window or open the surface, and no test would notice. safeEqual hashing both inputs before timingSafeEqual is correct today but has nothing pinning it.

*Suggested test.* apps/sales-api/src/admin.guard.test.ts: instantiate AdminKeyGuard with a stub ConfigService and a fake ExecutionContext; assert UnauthorizedException for a missing header, an empty-string header, an array-valued header, a wrong key, and a key that is a prefix of the real one; assert true only for an exact match; assert that `x-admin-key` alone is rejected. Same shape for InternalKeyGuard, including that an unset SALES_CONTROL_KEY disables the endpoint rather than accepting anything.

<a id="sales--decide-payout-batch-guard"></a>
#### `decide-payout-batch-guard` — The guard that stops a manual decision from freeing an on-chain row is untested

**MEDIUM** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `packages/sales-db/src/payouts.ts:157`

*Not covered today.* decidePayout's UPDATE carries `AND batch_id IS NULL` (:157) with the comment that without it a manual reject/paid could free a row the batch engine already broadcast, producing a double payout. Nothing tests it: packages/sales-db/src/payouts.ts has no test file, and admin.controller.ts:452 (the only caller) has none either. The DECISION_TRANSITIONS table (:132) — which statuses may move to which — is also unasserted.

*Production risk.* An operator clicking "reject" in the legacy payouts view on a row that the on-chain engine has already broadcast would set it to 'rejected', which removes it from sumCommitted; the next batch then re-pays the same amount while the original transfer confirms. The guard exists precisely because this is reachable from the admin UI.

*Suggested test.* Add packages/sales-db/src/payouts.integration.test.ts: create a batch-owned payout row (batch_id set, status requested) and assert decidePayout with 'reject' and with 'paid' both throw InvalidPayoutTransitionError and leave status/batch_id untouched; create a batch-free row and assert requested→approved→paid works, requested→approved→rejected works, and paid→anything throws; assert each successful decision writes a sales_audit_log row with the matching action.

*Verified absent by.* packages/sales-db/src/payouts.ts:157 is exactly `AND batch_id IS NULL`, with DECISION_TRANSITIONS at :132-136 and decidePayout at :138. `rg 'decidePayout' --glob '!node_modules'` returns exactly two non-definition hits: apps/sales-api/src/admin.controller.ts:23 (import) and :459 (the only call, inside `@Post('payouts/:id/decision')` at :452). packages/sales-db/src/payouts.ts has no test file (`find packages/sales-db -name '*test*'` lists 7 suites, none of them payouts.*), and admin.controller.ts has no adjacent test. The reachability argument checks out: the endpoint is live behind AdminKeyGuard and the batch engine sets batch_id on every row it creates (payout-batch.ts:148-151), so operator-initiated decisions and batch-managed rows do collide in the same table.

<a id="sales--payout-window-gate"></a>
#### `payout-window-gate` — The 3-day payout window gate has no test

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/sales-api/src/payout/payout.service.ts:131`

*Not covered today.* assertSendAllowed (:131), windowInfo (:119) and the PAYOUT_ENFORCE_WINDOW=false bypass are untested wiring over a well-tested primitive (periods.test.ts:84,:90). prepare() is not window-gated and can build a batch from accrual still inside its 7-day lock, but that batch cannot be sent while the gate holds, so the concrete exposure is a stuck prepare-lock and committed-but-unpayable balance rather than an early payout.

*Production risk.* If the gate regresses (or PAYOUT_ENFORCE_WINDOW is left false after an operational override), payouts can be sent at any time, including from commissions still inside the 7-day lock that exists to absorb chargebacks and clawbacks — money paid out that may later need to be reversed on-chain, which is impossible.

*Suggested test.* Unit-test PayoutService with a stub ConfigService (no DB, no chain needed) using injected `now`: with PAYOUT_ENFORCE_WINDOW=true, windowInfo inside the window returns open:true with opensAt/closesAt matching windowStart/windowEnd of the expected period, and outside returns open:false with nulls; assert send()/sendOne() reject with PayoutWindowClosedError before touching the DB when the window is closed (spy that getPayoutBatch was never called); with the flag false, assert enforced:false and no rejection. Add an assertion that prepare() outside a window produces a report whose batch total equals the sum the due list marks ineligible, so the divergence is at least pinned rather than accidental.

<a id="sales--min-payout-knob-split"></a>
#### `min-payout-knob-split` — Two different env knobs express the same payout minimum and nothing ties them together

**MEDIUM** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `apps/sales-api/src/config.ts:50`

*Not covered today.* PayoutService.minNano() reads PAYOUT_MIN_USD (payout.service.ts:93, config.ts:50, z.coerce.number() — fractional allowed), while the admin due list (admin.controller.ts:270) and the partner cabinet (partner.controller.ts:334) read SALES_MIN_PAYOUT_USD (config.ts:38, int only). Both default to 0 so they agree today, but nothing asserts they must. The comparison operators also differ: getPayoutCandidates filters `unpaidNano > minNano` (payout-batch.ts:50) while getDuePayoutList marks `payable < minPayoutNano` as below_minimum (payout-periods.ts:236), i.e. exclusive vs inclusive at the boundary. config.test.ts asserts no PAYOUT_* value.

*Production risk.* The moment an operator sets the documented minimum (SALES_PORTAL.md states "минимум SALES_MIN_PAYOUT_USD ($10)"), the admin preview will mark sub-$10 partners ineligible while prepare() — reading PAYOUT_MIN_USD, still 0 — includes them in the real batch. The operator sends a batch that does not match what they reviewed, paying dust transfers whose BNB gas can exceed the transfer value, and a partner at exactly the threshold is shown as payable but silently dropped from the batch.

*Suggested test.* Add to apps/sales-api/src/config.test.ts an assertion that both knobs exist with equal defaults, and a comment-anchored test that fails if they diverge. Better: collapse to one knob and add a PayoutService unit test asserting minNano() derives from the same value the due list uses. Add a boundary case to payout-batch.integration.test.ts: a partner whose unpaid is exactly minNano — assert getPayoutCandidates and getDuePayoutList agree on whether that partner is payable.

*Verified absent by.* All six line refs verified exactly: config.ts:38 `SALES_MIN_PAYOUT_USD: z.coerce.number().int()...default(0)` vs config.ts:50 `PAYOUT_MIN_USD: z.coerce.number()...default(0)` (no .int() — fractional really is accepted); payout.service.ts:93 reads PAYOUT_MIN_USD, admin.controller.ts:270 and partner.controller.ts:334 read SALES_MIN_PAYOUT_USD. Operator divergence confirmed: payout-batch.ts:50 `.filter((c) => c.walletAddress && c.unpaidNano > minNano)` (exclusive) vs payout-periods.ts:236 `else if (payable < minPayoutNano) reason = 'below_minimum'` (inclusive). apps/sales-api/src/config.test.ts (read in full, 54 lines) asserts PORT, HOST, session TTL, COMMERCE_BASE_URL, two public URLs, delivery mode, two intervals and two commission defaults — no PAYOUT_* or SALES_MIN_PAYOUT_USD assertion. payout-periods.integration.test.ts passes MIN_PAYOUT=10n throughout but never places a partner AT the boundary, so the >/< discrepancy is unasserted on both sides. The doc conflict is real and worse than stated: SALES_PORTAL.md:67 says the minimum is $10 while docs/SALES_PAYOUT_PERIODS.md:84 and :111 say it is 0 and a future knob; PAYOUT_MIN_USD is absent from apps/sales-api/.env.example entirely, so an operator following SALES_PORTAL.md would set only the SALES_ knob and get exactly the preview/batch divergence described.

<a id="sales--sync-cursor-partial-failure"></a>
#### `sync-cursor-partial-failure` — Cursor advance under partial page failure is untested — the loss mode is permanent

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/sales-api/src/sync.service.ts:138`

*Not covered today.* The sync loop that consumes parseFeedPage has no test at all (only the parser does, sync-feed.test.ts:1-35). The specific at-least-once contract — cursor advances only to the last row processed without throwing (sync.service.ts:135-139) — is a regression-protection gap, not a live defect: the current implementation is correct, so this is a 'nothing would catch a future regression here' finding rather than 'rows are being lost today'.

*Production risk.* advanceSyncCursor uses GREATEST, so a cursor can never move backwards — meaning any row skipped by an over-eager advance is lost forever. For attributions that means a referred customer is never linked to their partner and the partner earns nothing on that customer for life; for usage events it means commissions silently never accrue. The failure is invisible: the loop logs and continues, and the cabinet just shows a smaller number.

*Suggested test.* apps/sales-api/src/sync.service.test.ts with fetch stubbed (vi.stubGlobal) and a real TEST_SALES_DATABASE_URL: serve a 3-item attributions page where the middle item's referral-discount POST returns 500; assert the cursor lands on item 1's id (not item 3's), that a second tick re-serves from that cursor and completes, and that the replay creates no duplicate referred_users row. Add a case where the feed returns 404 → cursor unchanged, logged once; and one where nextCursor jumps far past the last item → cursor advances to nextCursor (the documented "filtered rows" case already covered in sync-feed.test.ts, here asserted through to the DB).

### 8.12 `web` — Frontend apps/web + content-studio

apps/web is the public marketing site, the localized (en/ru/ko/zh) SEO/learn corpus, the auth flows, and the entire authenticated customer dashboard where balances, API keys, tier pricing and top-up checkouts are shown and initiated. apps/content-studio is a small internal editorial tool behind Caddy admin auth. The 23 test files split cleanly into two populations: a genuinely good set of small behavioural/property tests over pure logic (locale routing, dashboard URL round-trip, SEO/sitemap/robots, analytics URL scrubbing, learn-corpus cross-locale parity, and one real jsdom render test of AuthEntryGuard), and a large source-text grep suite (migration.test.ts, public-product-truth.test.ts) that reads .tsx/.css files with readFileSync and asserts exact substrings. The second population is where most of the assertion volume lives and it protects almost nothing behavioural — migration.test.ts has 37 commits of churn including two explicit "update migration snapshot" commits, and it pins literal CSS declarations like ".nav-links{display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:space-evenly". Meanwhile the actual risk concentrates in apps/web/src/app/dashboard/dashboard.tsx — 1,626 lines containing every money formatter shown to a paying customer, the API-key spend-limit parser, and the sole allowlist gating the redirect to the payment provider — and it has zero behavioural tests; the only two tests naming it are a 23-line route-string test and migration.test.ts's substring greps. apps/sales-web (~5,000 LOC, including the irreversible on-chain USDT payout console) is confirmed to have no `test` script at all, yet apps/sales-web/* triggers a production sales deploy in deploy/watchdog-lib.sh:390.

**Working well here:**

- **Fail-closed auth gating on the login/register entry, tested as behaviour** — `apps/web/src/components/auth-entry-guard.test.ts:74-82` — The third case asserts that when the session check fails with a non-401 (503), the component shows an error and does NOT render the login form and does NOT redirect. That is the fail-closed direction, which is exactly the case a naive implementation gets wrong (treat any rejection as 'anonymous'). It renders the real component in jsdom via createRoot/act, so it would catch a refactor of the branch at auth-entry-guard.tsx:31, not just its text.
- **Money is pinned as exact strings using a value that only fails under float math** — `apps/web/src/lib/api.test.ts:18-24` — Sending amountUsd "9007199254740993" (2^53+1) and asserting it survives JSON.stringify verbatim is a deliberately chosen adversarial input: any refactor that routes the amount through a JavaScript number silently rounds it to ...992 and the test fails. This directly enforces the CLAUDE.md rule 'суммы — только integer, без JavaScript number'. api.test.ts:26-54 applies the same technique to spend-limit policy bodies.
- **Analytics/telemetry secret scrubbing tested with real adversarial URLs** — `apps/web/src/site-analytics.test.ts:7-16 and :28-32` — Both scrubbers are asserted against a URL carrying code, state and token alongside utm_* and yclid/gclid, with an exact expected output string. This pins both halves of the invariant (secrets removed, attribution kept) so a regression in either direction fails. Given Yandex Metrika runs with webvisor:true site-wide, leaking a reset token into a page URL would be a genuine incident, and this is the test standing in front of it.
- **Whole-corpus cross-locale structural parity for the SEO article set** — `apps/web/src/lib/learn.test.ts:44-62` — Instead of spot-checking one translated article, it iterates every article across every locale and asserts section count, FAQ count and keyword presence match the English source, then asserts the locale article sets are identical. On a ~500KB hand-maintained corpus this is the only economical way to catch a dropped or truncated translation, and it would fail loudly. :64-72 additionally pins that translations keep the base URL and model IDs verbatim.
- **Private routes proven absent from the public index surfaces** — `apps/web/src/seo.test.ts:17-30 and :32-43` — It asserts /dashboard, /login, /register, /reset-password, /verify-email and /auth/callback are not in the generated sitemap, that sitemap URLs are unique, and that robots.txt disallows /dashboard. This is a real leak class (indexing an authenticated surface or a token-bearing URL) tested against the actual generated output rather than a config file.
- **URL state round-trip as a property over the full section x language matrix** — `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/dashboard-route.test.ts:5-14` — It builds the href for every section in every language, parses it back through a real URL object, and asserts the section and pathname round-trip. It also pins the retired /security -> profile alias (:26-28), which preserves working bookmarks. Small, complete, and would catch any future section added to the list without a URL mapping.
- **Negative routing assertion that prevents a fabricated localized route** — `apps/web/src/components/persistent-route-shell.test.ts:28` — expect(usesAuthShell("/ru/auth/callback")).toBe(false) encodes a subtlety the source comments about (locale-routes.ts:11-13): only paths with a real Russian rendering may be treated as localized. A well-meaning refactor to 'handle /ru uniformly' would break the OAuth callback shell and this catches it.

<a id="web--dashboard-money-rendering"></a>
#### `dashboard-money-rendering` — Every money figure a paying customer sees is produced by untested module-private math in a 1,626-line file

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/dashboard.tsx:1569`

*Not covered today.* formatNanoUsd (dashboard.tsx:1569-1581), officialNanoFromCharged (:1559), paymentBasisPoints (:1541), discountOf (:1550), roundDivide (:1562), formatFixedRatio (:1589), formatNanoUsdSmart (:1267), niceNanoScale (:1278), boundedRatio (:1615), nanoToUsdInput (:850), usdInputToNano (:857) and keyPolicy (:868) are all module-private to dashboard.tsx and none is executed by any test. dashboard-route.test.ts covers only dashboard-route.ts; migration.test.ts:181-205 only greps this file for substrings. isApiKeyUsable is exported at :885 — apparently to be testable — yet nothing imports it. Meanwhile money.test.ts spends its first three assertions on nanoToUsd, which has zero production callers in apps/web.

*Production risk.* paymentBasisPoints silently defaults to 4_000 bp when pricing is absent or zero (dashboard.tsx:1548), so a backend field rename makes every 'official value of your balance' figure show a 60% discount to a customer on a different tier. formatNanoUsd's half-up rounding at :1576 and the quantum arithmetic decide the displayed balance; an off-by-one there mis-states real money on the account page. keyPolicy's limitReached/nearLimit comparison (:876-879, committed*10n >= limit*9n) drives whether a key is presented as usable — a sign or operator slip either hides working keys or presents exhausted ones as live. None of this fails any gate.

*Suggested test.* Extract the pure helpers into apps/web/src/lib/dashboard-money.ts and add dashboard-money.test.ts. Table tests: formatNanoUsd for 0, 1 nano, 999_999_999, exact half-cent rounding, negative values, and minimum/maximum fraction-digit combinations; officialNanoFromCharged x paymentBasisPoints for a b2c tier, a b2b manual rate, a referral floor override (referralFloorBps>0 with effectiveMultiplierBp), and the missing-pricing default asserting it does NOT silently claim 60%; usdInputToNano for '1.5', '0.000000001', and a >9-decimal input (currently BigInt('5000000009') is added whole, inflating the value); keyPolicy at exactly limit, at 90%, and expired-boundary. Assert isApiKeyUsable directly since it is already exported.

*Verified absent by.* Verified every function and line. apps/web/src/app/dashboard/dashboard.tsx:1569-1581 formatNanoUsd (half-up quantum rounding at :1576 `const scaled = (absolute + quantum / 2n) / quantum`), :1559-1561 officialNanoFromCharged, :1541-1552 paymentBasisPoints (the 4_000 bp default is real: `return BigInt(bp && bp > 0 ? bp : 4_000);` at :1550... confirmed at the `paymentBasisPoints` body), :1553-1558 discountOf, :1562-1568 roundDivide, :1589-1596 formatFixedRatio, :850-855 nanoToUsdInput, :857-860 usdInputToNano, :868-883 keyPolicy (limitReached `committed >= limit`, nearLimit `committed * 10n >= limit * 9n` at :877-878), :1615-1620 boundedRatio - all module-private, none exported. `rg -n 'nanoToUsd\b|isApiKeyUsable|formatNanoUsd|paymentBasisPoints|officialNanoFromCharged|roundDivide|keyPolicy' apps/web/src` outside dashboard.tsx returns only apps/web/src/lib/money.ts:1 and apps/web/src/lib/money.test.ts:2,6,7,8 - no test imports any dashboard helper. apps/web/src/app/dashboard/dashboard-route.test.ts covers only dashboardHref/parseDashboardSection; apps/web/src/migration.test.ts:180-205 only readFileSync+toContain greps dashboard.tsx. The nanoToUsd observation is exactly right: apps/web/src/lib/money.test.ts:6-8 exercises it, but the only production import from lib/money in apps/web is `import { normalizeUsd } from "@/lib/money"` at dashboard.tsx:10 - nanoToUsd has zero production callers. One correction to the claim's framing: isApiKeyUsable (dashboard.tsx:885) is not dead - it is used internally at dashboard.tsx:266 (`keys.filter((key) => isApiKeyUsable(key, policyNow))`); it is only untested. Nearest server-side coverage, apps/api/src/api-key-policy.test.ts, tests the zod input schemas (createApiKeySchema/updateApiKeyPolicySchema), not the near-limit/limit display predicate. Severity high stands: this is the entire money-display surface of the only authenticated page.

<a id="web--auth-token-url-scrub"></a>
#### `auth-token-url-scrub` — Password-reset and email-verification token scrubbing from URL and history is untested

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `apps/web/src/app/reset-password/reset-password-form.tsx:24`

*Not covered today.* reset-password-form.tsx:24-43 and apps/web/src/app/verify-email/verify-email.tsx:41-55 each capture a single-use auth token from either the query string or the fragment, then call window.history.replaceState to remove it from the visible URL before anything else happens. No test anywhere exercises either function (grep for captureAndScrubVerificationToken returns only the source). The related-but-different analytics scrubber IS tested at apps/web/src/site-analytics.test.ts:12.

*Production risk.* Yandex Metrika runs site-wide with webvisor:true and clickmap:true (asserted at apps/web/src/migration.test.ts:120-121) and Vercel Analytics fires on every route. If the scrub regresses — or the ordering changes so a pageview fires before replaceState — a live password-reset token is recorded in third-party session-replay, browser history, and any outbound Referer, which is account takeover. The fragment-vs-query fallback at reset-password-form.tsx:28 is exactly the kind of branch a refactor collapses. Note also verify-email.tsx:51 sets url.hash from URLSearchParams.toString(), which mangles a non key-value fragment such as #section into #section= — untested behaviour on a user-visible URL.

*Suggested test.* jsdom tests next to each form (follow the pattern already working in apps/web/src/components/auth-entry-guard.test.ts). Render with window.location set to /reset-password?token=T, then to /reset-password#token=T, then to a URL with both plus an unrelated query param and an unrelated fragment. Assert: (1) api.resetPassword is called with T; (2) after mount, window.location.href contains no 'token'; (3) history.replaceState was called; (4) unrelated params/fragments survive; (5) with no token at all, the submit button is disabled and the 'missing token' message shows.

*Verified absent by.* No test covers either function. `rg -n 'captureAndScrubVerificationToken|replaceState|scrub' apps/web/src` returns only apps/web/src/app/verify-email/verify-email.tsx:41,52,75, apps/web/src/app/reset-password/reset-password-form.tsx:38, apps/web/src/components/site-analytics.tsx:88 and apps/web/src/app/dashboard/dashboard.tsx:243 - no test file. Source verified: reset-password-form.tsx:24-43 (useLayoutEffect, fragment-first fallback `const capturedToken = fragmentToken || initialQueryToken.current;` at :28, delete+replaceState at :33-39) and verify-email.tsx:41-56. The hash-mangling sub-claim is real: verify-email.tsx:51 is `url.hash = hashParams.toString()`, and `new URLSearchParams('section').toString()` is 'section=', so #section becomes #section=. reset-password-form.tsx:34 avoids the empty case (`url.hash = fragmentParams.size ? ... : ""`) but has the same key= mangling for non key-value fragments. Metrika config assertions at apps/web/src/migration.test.ts:120-121 (webvisor:true / clickmap:true) confirmed. The nearest existing coverage is only adjacent, as the claim itself says: apps/web/src/site-analytics.test.ts:12-13 asserts withoutSensitiveUrlData('/reset-password?token=secret') === '/reset-password', which covers the Vercel/Metrika telemetry payload but not the address-bar/history/Referer scrub, and apps/web/src/migration.test.ts:83-93 only greps reset-password-form.tsx for the 8-character password strings. Severity high stands, with the note that the largest untested risk is arguably functional (if capture regresses, password reset is dead for every user) rather than only the replay-leak path.

<a id="web--referral-attribution"></a>
#### `referral-attribution` — Partner commission attribution (last-click-wins + 30-day TTL) has zero tests despite a documented prior regression

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/web/src/lib/referral.ts:14`

*Not covered today.* The client-side last-click/TTL/pattern logic in apps/web/src/lib/referral.ts is genuinely untested, and it is the sole decider of which code is sent at first registration - but the server side is first-wins and IS tested, so a client regression cannot re-attribute an already-attributed user.

*Production risk.* A silent revert to first-wins, an inverted TTL comparison at referral.ts:37, or a CODE_PATTERN tightening that rejects live partner codes re-introduces exactly the documented bug: a user who clicks a partner's discount link is attributed to a stale code, gets the wrong (or no) referral floor rate, and the wrong partner is paid real USDT. This is real money in both directions and it is invisible until a partner disputes their statement.

*Suggested test.* apps/web/src/lib/referral.test.ts with a stubbed localStorage (the pattern at apps/web/src/lib/product-analytics.test.ts:12-18 already works): last-click overwrite (store A, capture B, expect B); idempotent re-capture of the same code does not move the timestamp; a stored entry older than 30 days returns undefined AND is removed from storage; malformed JSON, wrong-typed fields, and a stored code failing CODE_PATTERN all return undefined; codes of length 2 and 33 and with a '.' are rejected on capture; a throwing localStorage does not propagate.

<a id="web--checkout-redirect-allowlist"></a>
#### `checkout-redirect-allowlist` — The only origin allowlist in the entire top-up funnel is 9 untested client-side lines

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/dashboard.tsx:1607`

*Not covered today.* safeCheckoutUrl/CHECKOUT_ORIGINS have no unit test (true), but the proposed gap does not mitigate the availability failure it describes, and the security failure is defence-in-depth behind an already-trusted provider response.

*Production risk.* Two failure directions, both live. (1) Allowlist too narrow: Platega or Cryptomus rotates or adds a checkout host (routine) and every top-up dies at the last step with 'invalid checkout URL' AFTER the checkout row was already created server-side, leaving orphan pending checkouts and a fully blocked revenue funnel — with no test and no server-side coordination to catch it. (2) Allowlist accidentally widened or the protocol/credential checks at dashboard.tsx:1611 dropped in a refactor: a compromised or misconfigured provider response redirects a paying customer to an attacker-controlled payment page.

*Suggested test.* Unit test in apps/web/src/app/dashboard/checkout-url.test.ts (export safeCheckoutUrl and CHECKOUT_ORIGINS, or move both to apps/web/src/lib/checkout-url.ts). Table-driven: accept https://pay.platega.io/x and https://app.platega.io/x and https://pay.cryptomus.com/x; reject http:// downgrade, https://user:pass@pay.platega.io, https://pay.platega.io.evil.tld, https://evil.tld/pay.platega.io, a cryptomus URL passed with provider 'platega', and a non-URL string. Add a companion assertion in packages/payments or apps/api that the provider base host used in production is a member of the same allowlist constant.

<a id="web--sales-web-no-test-script"></a>

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/sales-web/src/lib/api.ts:66`

*Not covered today.* apps/sales-web has no `test` script so the workspace gate silently skips it and any test added there would not run today (real); the specific '$0.00 on an irreversible payout' scenario is guarded upstream by BigInt.toString() and is largely theoretical.

*Production risk.* If sales-api ever returns a nano amount in any non-bare-integer form (a decimal-suffixed string, a value with whitespace, null, a JS-number-serialized value), the operator's on-chain confirmation dialog reads '$0.00' and they approve a real USDT-BEP20 batch believing it is empty. Partner dashboards independently show every balance as $0.00, generating support load and disputes. Because there is no test script, adding one test file to this package would not even run in the gate today.

Verify the new script actually executes under `pnpm -r --if-present test` before relying on it.

<a id="web--calculator-price-drift-sonnet5"></a>
#### `calculator-price-drift-sonnet5` — Public cost calculator hardcodes an expiring intro price and a duplicate tier table that nothing cross-checks against the billing engine

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/web/src/components/cost-calculator.tsx:26`

*Not covered today.* The calculator's MODELS/TIERS tables are hand-maintained duplicates with no test cross-checking them against apps/web/src/lib/pricing-tiers.ts or crates/metering; the drift is a maintenance/staleness gap, not an undisclosed price misrepresentation.

*Production risk.* On 2026-09-01 the engine starts metering Sonnet 5 at the standard rate while /tools/claude-api-cost-calculator keeps quoting the intro rate forever. Every prospective customer sees a per-task cost roughly 33% below what they will actually be charged on the most-recommended model, on a page whose own /models/claude-sonnet-5 page simultaneously quotes the correct higher figure. That is a consumer price misrepresentation on a live commercial site, and the deploy gate stays green through it. The same class of drift applies to a future tier change: editing pricing-tiers.ts and the backend leaves the calculator quoting a discount the billing engine will not honour.

*Suggested test.* Unit test in apps/web/src/lib/pricing-consistency.test.ts (new): (a) import B2C_PRICING_MILESTONES and the calculator's TIERS and assert label/discount/topup agree element-by-element — this requires exporting TIERS from cost-calculator.tsx; (b) import CLAUDE_MODELS from lib/models.ts and the calculator's MODELS and assert every shared model id has identical inputPerM/outputPerM, with an explicit allowlist entry for Sonnet 5 that carries the intro-rate cutoff; (c) a dated guard: assert Date.now() < 1788220800000 OR that the Sonnet 5 calculator rate equals the standard rate, so the suite goes red at the cutoff instead of silently under-quoting.

<a id="web--dashboard-route-protection"></a>
#### `dashboard-route-protection` — Route protection for /dashboard is entirely client-side and untested in the direction that matters

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/dashboard.tsx:197`

*Not covered today.* The unauthenticated-visitor path on /dashboard is untested (true), but the consequence is UX-only: no data is exposed, because every figure comes from credentialed API calls that 401.

*Production risk.* If the 401 branch is broken by a refactor (e.g. an error wrapper stops producing ApiError, or the Promise.all at dashboard.tsx:183 rejects differently), an expired-session user lands on a permanently broken dashboard instead of the login page. The 'loading' guard at dashboard.tsx:263 is the only thing preventing a flash of the empty authenticated shell. Neither behaviour has a regression test, and this is the app's only private route.

*Suggested test.* jsdom test in apps/web/src/app/dashboard/dashboard.test.ts mocking @/lib/api and next/navigation exactly as apps/web/src/components/auth-entry-guard.test.ts:21-28 does: (1) api.me rejects with ApiError 401 -> router.replace('/login') called and no account markup rendered; (2) api.me rejects with a 503 -> no redirect, error surface shown; (3) while api.me is pending -> the loading view renders and no balance element exists. Also assert the Russian dashboard redirects to /ru/login once that is fixed.

<a id="web--api-client-error-paths"></a>
#### `api-client-error-paths` — The browser API client's error and no-content paths are untested, including the map that produces user-visible failure text

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/web/src/lib/api.ts:160`

*Not covered today.* The 204 branch, the array-message join, the non-JSON .catch(() => null) path and the defaultError status map are untested (true); the 'missing credentials on an individual method' risk is structurally impossible and should be dropped.

*Production risk.* A 204 mishandled as JSON throws inside logout/revoke, so the user sees a failure toast for an operation that actually succeeded — and for revokeApiKey that means they retry a destructive action believing it did not take. An array-message regression shows '[object Object]' or the generic fallback instead of the field error, on the API-key creation and top-up forms. A missing credentials:'include' on any single method silently breaks that endpoint's auth in production only (cookies are same-site in dev-like setups).

*Suggested test.* Extend apps/web/src/lib/api.test.ts: a Response with status 204 and empty body resolves to undefined for api.logout() and api.revokeApiKey(); a 400 with {message:['a','b']} produces 'a. b'; a 500 with an HTML body produces 'The service is temporarily unavailable'; a 403 and a 429 produce their mapped strings. Add one loop over Object.entries(api) invoking each method against a stub fetch and asserting every call carried credentials:'include' and cache:'no-store' — that closes the 24 unasserted methods in about ten lines.

<a id="web--content-studio-untested-surface"></a>
#### `content-studio-untested-surface` — Content Studio's request wrapper and its entire UI, including irreversible publish actions, are untested

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `apps/content-studio/src/lib/api.ts:3`

*Not covered today.* request() and studio.tsx are untested and two apiErrorMessage branches (string at :16, array at :17-20) are uncovered; the formErrors/fieldErrors record branch IS covered, and this is an internal admin tool bound to 127.0.0.1, not a customer money surface.

*Production risk.* A publish action fired against the wrong project id or a double-submit publishes editorial content to the public blog irreversibly; the blog-first gate (gate.ts, well tested) only governs external syndication, not the blog publish itself. Errors from the admin API surface as 'Request failed (400)' instead of the field detail whenever the untested branches are hit, which is most real validation failures.

*Suggested test.* Extend apps/content-studio/src/lib/api.test.ts to cover the remaining apiErrorMessage branches (plain string, string array, formErrors-only, whitespace-only message falling through to the status), and add a stub-fetch test that request() targets /v1/admin/content<path>, sets content-type only when a body is present, and throws with the flattened message on !ok. Add an explicit apps/content-studio/vitest.config.ts so include/environment are pinned rather than inherited.

<a id="web--i18n-key-resolution"></a>
#### `i18n-key-resolution` — No test asserts that referenced i18n keys resolve, and one live key is missing from both locales

**LOW** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `apps/web/src/app/page.tsx:109`

*Not covered today.* messages.json is only ever read as a raw string by migration.test.ts:72,160,218 and public-product-truth.test.ts:13. Nothing asserts key parity or resolution. en and ru currently have 500 keys each with no asymmetry, but the key home_calc_more referenced at apps/web/src/app/page.tsx:109 is absent from both dictionaries. It does not break the page only because the T component falls back to its children (apps/web/src/components/translated.tsx:12: t(k) === k ? children : t(k)), so the Russian homepage renders that link in English.

*Production risk.* Low and self-limiting by design — the fallback chain (ru -> en -> key at i18n-provider.tsx:40, plus T's children fallback) means a missing key degrades to English rather than showing a raw key, except for the t() call sites that have no children fallback (login-form.tsx, register-form.tsx, social-auth.tsx), where a missing key would render a literal identifier like 'login_btn' as a button label on the auth form.

*Suggested test.* A ~15-line apps/web/src/lib/messages.test.ts: assert Object.keys(messages.en) and Object.keys(messages.ru) are set-equal; then scan src for /(?<![\w.])t\("([a-z0-9_]+)"\)/ and /k="([a-z0-9_]+)"/ and assert every captured key exists in messages.en. That is enough to have caught home_calc_more and costs nothing to maintain.

*Verified absent by.* All four factual assertions independently verified. (1) `rg -n home_calc_more apps/web/src` returns exactly one hit - apps/web/src/app/page.tsx:109, the `<T k="home_calc_more">` call site - and zero hits in apps/web/src/lib/messages.json, so the key is absent from both dictionaries. (2) Key parity holds: node-parsed messages.json reports en=500, ru=500. (3) The fallback chain is as described: apps/web/src/components/i18n-provider.tsx:37-41 `return dictionary[key] ?? fallback[key] ?? key;` and apps/web/src/components/translated.tsx:12 `{t(k) === k ? children : t(k)}`, so the RU homepage renders that link's English children. (4) messages.json is only ever read as a raw string in tests - apps/web/src/migration.test.ts:72 (join(root,'lib','messages.json')) and apps/web/src/public-product-truth.test.ts:13 (read('lib/messages.json')), both feeding toContain/not.toMatch greps; nothing parses it or asserts key parity or resolution. I also grepped for a bare-t() exposure: apps/web/src/app/login/login-form.tsx:35,38,39,41,45 use t("login_h"), t("f_email"), t("login_btn") etc. with no children fallback, confirming the identified blast radius. Severity low is accurate.

### 8.13 `infra` — Deploy/watchdog/infra test suites

This domain is the self-hosted CD pipeline: a root-adjacent watchdog on the prod host polls master, clones the exact SHA, runs the entire repo's test gate in isolation, applies commerce migrations, and blue-green cuts over the Rust engine, the commerce API/worker/content-studio, and the partner sales portal — then installs its own next version from the candidate it just tested. Two bash suites gate it (deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh, 499 lines; deploy/monitoring-config.test.sh, 212 lines), both run inside the gate at deploy/watchdog.sh:343-344. The honest picture is a sharp split: the ~430-line pure-helper library deploy/watchdog-lib.sh is genuinely well tested — retention selectors, the Drizzle append-only migration manifest, bounded retry, engine topology, and the ERR/EXIT trap semantics are all exercised behaviourally with real fixtures, and several tests encode non-obvious bash/systemd/PromQL failure modes that would each catch a real regression. Everything that actually mutates production is not. deploy/lib.sh (670 lines, 44 functions — the activation journal, atomic symlink swap, release validation, readiness gates) is never sourced by any test; deploy/rollback.sh, deploy/engine-bluegreen.sh, deploy/api-bluegreen.sh, deploy/deploy.sh, deploy/sales-deploy.sh, deploy/watchdog-migrate.sh and deploy/watchdog-backup.sh have zero behavioural coverage and are asserted on only by `grep -Fq` against their own source text. The two standalone e2e harnesses (deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh, tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh) are wired into no gate at all, and the rotation smoke harness has silently rotted into a script that cannot run.

**Working well here:**

- **Drizzle migration history is provably append-only, validated against the real repo migrations** — `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:56-98` — This is the best test in the domain. It copies the actual packages/db/migrations tree three times, builds a manifest from the baseline, then proves three distinct properties: a legitimately appended migration still passes wd_manifest_is_append_only but changes the digest (lines 63-80); appending a byte to an already-applied SQL file is rejected (lines 82-86); and mutating a historical journal entry's `when` is rejected (lines 88-98). Because the fixture is the real migration tree, the manifest generator's own invariants (contiguous idx, monotonic when, unique tags, no unjournaled SQL, no symlinks — watchdog-lib.sh:307-320) are re-validated on every gate run. A hand-edited or renumbered migration cannot reach the production commerce database with this in place.
- **Release retention cannot delete something that is live** — `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:116-177` — Three separate scenarios against a real directory tree with controlled mtimes: keep=1 selects exactly the two oldest unprotected releases and never current/previous/not-a-release (lines 143-159); an explicitly protected SHA — how watchdog.sh passes live PID-resolved releases and recorded component baselines at watchdog.sh:245-248 — survives even when counting would reach it (lines 161-169); and keep=0 with no protected list still selects only 3 of 5, i.e. current/previous are structurally safe (lines 171-177). Deleting the release backing a running engine is unrecoverable without a rebuild, and this pins it down.
- **Candidate retention uses test-completion age, not workspace age, and refuses unsafe targets** — `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:11-54` — Six fixtures cover the genuinely tricky cases: a symlinked SHA directory pointing outside the root, a malformed non-SHA name, an exact-boundary mtime (strictly-older semantics), and — the subtle one — two directories whose .tested marker mtime disagrees with their directory mtime in opposite directions (lines 38-43), asserting that the marker wins. That is the difference between reclaiming a 24-hour-old passed build and deleting one that just went green.
- **ERR/EXIT trap semantics of the quarantine handler are proved behaviourally, including the subshell case** — `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:334-380` — Six executed cases: success and deliberate `exit 0` must not quarantine; a wd_die before a candidate is selected must report PRECANDIDATE and quarantine nothing; a wd_die or a plain failing command after selection must quarantine; and a wd_die inside a `if ! ( ... )` condition subshell must be caught by the caller so the post-admission rollback at watchdog.sh:608-611 can recover instead of quarantining from inside the subshell. These are real bash semantics that are easy to get wrong and impossible to reason about by inspection, and getting any of them wrong either quarantines every green SHA or silently quarantines nothing.
- **Caddy secret carry-over is render-idempotent and leaks no placeholder** — `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:431-455` — The awk renderer is run twice — once against a synthetic live config, once against its own output — and both results are asserted identically: no basic_auth block survives, no bcrypt hash survives, the forward_auth line is present, each secret appears the exact expected number of times (including the case-differing X-Admin-Key at line 450), and no `<..._PLACEHOLDER>` remains. Idempotency matters because this runs on every Caddy-touching infrastructure commit; a non-idempotent renderer would progressively corrupt the live config or, worse, ship a placeholder as a live admin key.
- **Monitoring alerts, metrics and runbooks are closed loops** — `deploy/monitoring-config.test.sh` — For every deployment and Codex metric it asserts three things at once: the collector (or engine http.rs) exports it, a provider-scoped Prometheus rule consumes it, and every alert carries a MONITORING.md anchor that actually resolves to a section in MONITORING.md. It additionally asserts the two provider-availability alerts are gated on `claude_api_codex_enabled{provider="openai"} == 1` so a disabled provider does not page. This class of test catches the most common observability rot — an alert whose metric nobody emits, or a page with no runbook — which is invisible until the incident.
- **Two encoded production incidents kept from regressing** — `deploy/monitoring-config.test.sh:29-35 and 173-193` — The first pins the PromQL `and` operand shape used in final_verify_admin_panel (watchdog.sh:514), asserting the aggregated form is present and the unaggregated form is absent — vector matching would otherwise silently return empty while every series is healthy, i.e. a verification that can never pass. The second pins that the collector unit still drops capabilities, that deployment state is written 0644, and that reads are guarded with `-r` — the exact CapabilityBoundingSet/0640 interaction recorded in project memory. Both are specific, non-obvious, and would each be reintroduced by a plausible edit.

<a id="infra--lib-activation-journal-untested"></a>
#### `lib-activation-journal-untested` — deploy/lib.sh — the activation journal that unwinds a half-applied cutover — is never sourced by any test

**CRITICAL** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `deploy/lib.sh:470-670`

*Not covered today.* deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:6 sources only deploy/watchdog-lib.sh. deploy/lib.sh (670 lines, 44 functions) is sourced by deploy.sh, rollback.sh, api-bluegreen.sh and engine-bluegreen.sh but by no test. The gate's only contact with it is `bash -n` (watchdog.sh:340-342). Specifically untested: capture_release_link's refusal to journal an unexpected link (lib.sh:496-499), journaled_link_is_unchanged's TOCTOU re-check before activation (lib.sh:528-541 / 551), set_journaled_release_link's path_is_direct_release gate (lib.sh:550), restore_target_link/restore_absent_link's temp-file handling (lib.sh:565-597), restore_activation_links' reverse-order unwind and per-link failure accounting (lib.sh:599-626), and activation_abort's committed/active state machine (lib.sh:628-650).

*Production risk.* This is the code that decides whether an aborted deploy leaves production pointing at the old, working release or at a partially-swapped state. A bug in restore_activation_links (for example the reverse-index loop at lib.sh:603 silently skipping a changed link, or the ACTIVATION_CHANGED bookkeeping at lib.sh:556-561 marking a link unchanged when it was mutated) converts a recoverable failed deploy into a manual, console-only recovery on a host that bills real money. path_is_direct_release (lib.sh:112) is also the only thing preventing current from being pointed outside the release root.

*Suggested test.* A hermetic deploy/lib.test.sh sourcing deploy/lib.sh against mktemp -d roots: (1) capture two links, change one, call restore_activation_links, assert the changed link is restored and the unchanged one is untouched; (2) capture an absent previous link, create it, restore, assert it is absent again; (3) assert set_journaled_release_link dies when the link was mutated between capture and activation (the preflight TOCTOU guard); (4) assert it dies when pointed at a path outside the root or at a non-release path; (5) drive begin_activation with a failing command and assert the ERR trap restored every link and invoked the recovery callback exactly once.

*Verified absent by.* `grep -rn 'lib\.sh' --glob '*.test.sh' --glob '*.test.ts' .` returns only deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:5-6 (which sources watchdog-lib.sh) and deploy/monitoring-config.test.sh:202 (a grep of watchdog-lib.sh). deploy/lib.sh is sourced by no test. Every cited line number is exact: capture_release_link refusal deploy/lib.sh:498, journaled_link_is_unchanged deploy/lib.sh:528-541, set_journaled_release_link's path_is_direct_release gate deploy/lib.sh:550 and TOCTOU re-check deploy/lib.sh:551, ACTIVATION_CHANGED bookkeeping deploy/lib.sh:556-561, restore_absent_link deploy/lib.sh:565, restore_target_link deploy/lib.sh:582, restore_activation_links reverse loop deploy/lib.sh:599-626, activation_abort deploy/lib.sh:628-650, path_is_direct_release deploy/lib.sh:112. Gate contact is only `bash -n` over deploy/*.sh at deploy/watchdog.sh:340-342. The happy-path half of this code (set_journaled_release_link) runs on every production deploy, so the risk is not confined to failure paths.

<a id="infra--rollback-path-untested"></a>
#### `rollback-path-untested` — The automatic rollback path — the only recovery once traffic is committed — has zero behavioural coverage

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `deploy/rollback.sh:81-229 (invoked from deploy/watchdog.sh:539-571)`

*Not covered today.* deploy/rollback.sh is never executed by any test in any mode; its only coverage is three source-text greps (deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:317-324, asserting rollback_engine/rollback_backend are referenced in watchdog.sh and that install-watchdog.sh copies controller/rollback.sh) — not one, as claimed. The described untested internals are real and the line citations are exact. Severity is high rather than critical because attempt_rollback is invoked best-effort (`rollback_engine || true` / `rollback_backend || true` at deploy/watchdog.sh:640,668) and both callers then wd_die and quarantine, so a rollback.sh defect degrades an already-failed deploy into a manual recovery rather than silently corrupting a healthy one; rollback.sh also inherits the same journal/abort unwind from deploy/lib.sh (rollback.sh:207-212).
