*Production risk.* attempt_rollback (watchdog.sh:539) runs at exactly one moment: the new engine or commerce release has already been admitted, the old slot drained, and final verification has failed — so production is serving an unverified release with real customer traffic and real money moving through /v1/messages. If rollback.sh mis-selects a target, dies on an argument it should accept, or leaves current pointing at a half-swapped release, the outcome is an outage or a silent revert to the wrong build with no automatic second chance. The watchdog then quarantines and stops, so no subsequent commit can fix it either.

*Suggested test.* Add a hermetic bash suite (deploy/rollback.test.sh, wired into deploy/watchdog.sh next to watchdog-lib.test.sh:343) that builds a fake release root in mktemp -d with current/previous symlinks and marker files, overrides COMMERCE_RELEASE_ROOT/ENGINE_RELEASE_ROOT/DEPLOY_LOCK_FILE/ENGINE_POSTGRES_ENV via env, and runs `rollback.sh --api-only --dry-run` and `--engine-bluegreen --dry-run`. Assert: current/previous are unchanged after a dry run; the selected target equals the previous link; an explicit SHA that has no release directory is rejected before any link mutation; --engine-only with a non-empty engine-postgres.env dies with the documented message; and passing both --engine-only and --api-only is rejected.

<a id="infra--sales-migrations-unguarded"></a>
#### `sales-migrations-unguarded` — Sales-portal PostgreSQL migrations bypass the append-only manifest and the pre-deploy backup entirely, and the script that applies them is untested

**HIGH** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `deploy/sales-deploy.sh:121-125`

*Not covered today.* The manifest/backup gap is real, but 'the script that applies them is untested' overstates it: the gate does build and run packages/sales-db/dist/migrate.js against a disposable PostgreSQL database (deploy/watchdog.sh:325-326) and then runs the sales-db integration suites against that migrated database (deploy/watchdog.sh:327-329, e.g. packages/sales-db/src/payout-batch.integration.test.ts, commissions.test.ts), so broken or invalid sales DDL fails the gate. The accurate narrower claim: sales migrations are exempt from the append-only manifest (wd_migration_manifest hard-codes packages/db at deploy/watchdog-lib.sh:243,255,324, and the append-only tests at deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:56-98 only build fixtures from packages/db/migrations), and a sales-only commit deploys with no fresh validated pre-deploy dump because deploy_sales (deploy/watchdog.sh:677-688) never calls BACKUP_RUNNER — unlike deploy_engine (deploy/watchdog.sh:632). An edited/deleted historical sales migration still replays cleanly on a fresh disposable database and is therefore not caught.

*Production risk.* packages/sales-db/migrations contains payout_batches, commission_deposits and promo_codes — the partner program that pays real USDT-BEP20 on-chain. An edited or deleted historical migration in that tree is applied to production with no append-only rejection and no fresh validated dump taken first, while the identical class of change against packages/db is refused before any DB command runs (watchdog.sh:638-641). The symlink rollback at sales-deploy.sh:149-158 explicitly does not reverse the migration, so a destructive DDL there is unrecoverable except from the last hourly rotation dump.

*Suggested test.* Two changes, both testable. First, generalise wd_migration_manifest to take a migrations root and add a second manifest/baseline for packages/sales-db, then extend the existing deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:56-98 fixture block to cover the sales tree with the same append/edit/journal-tamper trio. Second, add an integration assertion in deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh that deploy/watchdog.sh's deploy_sales invokes BACKUP_RUNNER before SALES_RUNNER — and back it with a fixture that runs sales-deploy.sh against a mktemp release root with a stubbed migrate.js, asserting the current symlink is left untouched when the migration exits non-zero.

<a id="infra--bluegreen-controllers-untested"></a>
#### `bluegreen-controllers-untested` — Both blue-green cutover controllers are covered only by occurrence-count greps

**HIGH** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `deploy/api-bluegreen.sh:320-474 and deploy/engine-bluegreen.sh:98-139`

*Not covered today.* api-bluegreen.sh (650 lines) has exactly two assertions in the whole repo: deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:294 (a default URL string) and :295 (`[[ $(grep -Fc 'balancer_is_ready' ...) -ge 6 ]]`). engine-bluegreen.sh has none — no test file mentions it. The recovery functions stop_failed_target_after_old_ready, restart_old_slot_on_current_loudly, recover_with_old_slot, recover_cutover, cutover_abort (api-bluegreen.sh:320-464) and engine-bluegreen.sh's recover/abort_cutover/commit_cutover (lines 98-139) are the paths that run when a cutover fails, and none is exercised.

*Production risk.* A regression in the cutover state machine either leaves both slots serving (double-admit — for the engine this means two writers against the fenced PostgreSQL authority) or neither (a full outage of api.apitoken.sale or backend.apitoken.sale). wd_engine_topology_is_steady is tested and would detect the double-admit after the fact (watchdog-lib.test.sh:220-233), but only after the controller already produced it — and the repair path reconcile_engine_runtime (watchdog.sh:424-437) simply re-runs the same untested controller.

*Suggested test.* Extract the slot-decision predicates (other_port, slot_unit, slot_serves_current_release, and the target-selection logic that picks which of 8787/8788 to build into) into a sourceable pure section, then table-test them in a bash suite: for each of (active=8787 ready, active=8788 ready, neither active, both active) assert the chosen target port and the chosen recovery action. Complement with a systemctl/curl shim on PATH so wait_for_slot and recover_with_old_slot can be driven end-to-end against fake units in a temp dir.

*Verified absent by.* `grep -n 'api-bluegreen\|engine-bluegreen' deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh deploy/monitoring-config.test.sh` returns exactly two lines: deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:294 (a default URL string) and :295 (`grep -Fc 'balancer_is_ready' ... -ge 6`). deploy/engine-bluegreen.sh appears in no test file. Cited recovery functions verified to exist: deploy/api-bluegreen.sh:320 stop_failed_target_after_old_ready, :333 restart_old_slot_on_current_loudly, :344 recover_with_old_slot, :387 recover_cutover, :450 cutover_abort; deploy/engine-bluegreen.sh:98 recover, :121 abort_cutover, :132 begin_cutover, :139 commit_cutover. The mitigations the claim itself names check out: wd_engine_topology_is_steady is behaviourally tested at deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:220-233 and consumed at deploy/watchdog.sh:418, and the repair path re-runs the same untested controller (deploy/watchdog.sh:424-437).

<a id="infra--migration-and-backup-runners-untested"></a>
#### `migration-and-backup-runners-untested` — The irreversible production migration applier and the backup that is its only safety net have no tests

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `deploy/watchdog-migrate.sh:36-67 and deploy/watchdog-backup.sh:30-65`

*Not covered today.* Neither script is executed by any test — correct. But the safety predicates watchdog-migrate.sh delegates to are unit-tested and run in the gate: wd_migration_manifest / wd_manifest_is_append_only / wd_manifest_digest are exercised over real fixtures including an edited historical SQL migration and an edited journal entry (deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:56-98), and the caller re-asserts the committed digest after the runner returns (deploy/watchdog.sh:714-715), which detects a manifest that was not committed for the tested migration. The ordering hazard the claim describes (manifest committed before a failed migration) is additionally structurally prevented by `set -e` at deploy/watchdog-migrate.sh:2 — the node migrate at :59-61 aborts the script before the cp at :63. The genuine residual gap is all of deploy/watchdog-backup.sh (staleness assertion :33, pg_restore render :45-46, per-database probe loop :58-65) and watchdog-migrate.sh's marker/digest preconditions at :25-38 and setpriv de-escalation at :59.

*Production risk.* Commerce migrations are forward-only and irreversible by design (DEPLOYMENT.md non-negotiables). If the manifest is committed before a migration actually succeeds, or the backup is preserved without the pg_restore render actually reading the archive, the pipeline believes it has a recovery point it does not have — and the next migration proceeds on that belief. A silently stale or truncated dump is indistinguishable from a good one until the moment it is needed.

*Suggested test.* Add a fixture-driven suite that shims `systemctl`, `docker` and `node` onto PATH and runs watchdog-backup.sh against a temp BACKUP_ROOT. Assert: a dump whose mtime predates backup_started is rejected as stale; a pg_restore shim exiting non-zero prevents the .pre-deploy-<sha>.dump from appearing and prevents the .complete marker; the complete marker is written only after every existing database validated; and a pre-existing complete marker short-circuits with exit 0. For watchdog-migrate.sh, shim the migrate.js and assert the DB_MANIFEST is left at its old digest when the migration exits non-zero, and that the pending-migration.sha file survives that failure.

<a id="infra--no-config-validation-in-gate"></a>
#### `no-config-validation-in-gate` — The gate validates no configuration it ships: no `caddy validate`, no `systemd-analyze verify`, no `visudo -c`

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `deploy/watchdog.sh:336-344`

*Not covered today.* The factual core is correct — deploy/watchdog.sh:336-344 runs only `git diff --check`, `bash -n` over deploy/*.sh and the two bash suites, with no caddy validate / systemd-analyze verify / visudo -c. But two of the three named artifacts are guarded elsewhere, so the risk is narrower. (a) The Caddyfile render pipeline is behaviourally tested, not just grepped: deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:439-455 renders deploy/Caddyfile twice through render-caddy.awk against a live fixture and asserts secret carry-over, idempotence and no leftover placeholders; and an invalid Caddyfile is rejected by `install-caddy.sh --check` (deploy/watchdog-infrastructure.sh:42) before it is ever installed (deploy/install-caddy.sh:28) — so the outcome is a quarantine after controllers are refreshed, not a broken Caddy. (b) The sudo policy is deliberately not auto-applied (deploy/install-watchdog.sh:45-51 stages it only), install-sudoers.sh validates with `visudo -c -f` before installing and restores on failure (deploy/install-sudoers.sh:50,86,90-100), and that guard is asserted at deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:402. The real residual gap is systemd unit validity: units are installed at deploy/install-watchdog.sh:73 and daemon-reloaded/restarted at :190-194 with no systemd-analyze verify anywhere except the manual step in DEPLOYMENT.md:291.

*Production risk.* This is the self-delivery hazard specifically. deploy/watchdog-infrastructure.sh:40 runs install-watchdog.sh — which overwrites every controller, the watchdog itself, and all systemd units, then daemon-reloads and restarts services — and only afterwards, at line 42, does it run install-caddy.sh --check. A Caddyfile that fails validation therefore quarantines the SHA after the new, unvalidated controller set is already live. A malformed systemd unit is worse: it is installed and daemon-reloaded before anything notices.

*Suggested test.* Extend deploy/monitoring-config.test.sh (or a new deploy/config-validate.test.sh wired at watchdog.sh:344) to run, guarded on the tool being present: `caddy validate --adapter caddyfile --config` on the *rendered* output already produced at watchdog-lib.test.sh:442; `systemd-analyze verify` over systemd/*.service and *.timer; and `visudo -c -f deploy/sudoers.d/95-apitoken-deploy` with the same no-warnings rule install-sudoers.sh:52-55 applies. Separately, reorder watchdog-infrastructure.sh so install-caddy.sh --check runs before install-watchdog.sh, and assert that ordering with a grep-free behavioural fixture.

<a id="infra--quarantine-state-machine-untested"></a>
#### `quarantine-state-machine-untested` — The quarantine / retry / already-processed state machine is never driven end to end

**MEDIUM** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `deploy/watchdog.sh:666-760 and deploy/watchdog-control.sh:42-51`

*Not covered today.* The claim's source line numbers are stale by ~58 lines (main() is deploy/watchdog.sh:725; the rejected.sha short-circuit is :776-781; wd_require_ancestor calls are :771-774; the already-processed fast path is :805-812; the `rm -f -- "$REJECTED_FILE"` after a passing gate is :820; watchdog-control.sh:42-51 is correct). Coverage is also better than 'one grep for a string': the quarantine *write* decision is behaviourally tested with a fixture that replicates the exact `trap fail ERR EXIT INT TERM` handler and asserts pre-candidate vs candidate quarantine, exit-0 exemption, plain command failure, and non-quarantine from inside a condition subshell (deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:334-388), and the quarantine marker's collector-readable mode is asserted at deploy/monitoring-config.test.sh:190. What is genuinely untested is the *read* side: the rejected.sha short-circuit, the already-processed fast path, wd_require_ancestor (deploy/watchdog-lib.sh:423-430 — zero references in any test file), and watchdog-control.sh's retry SHA-mismatch refusal.

*Production risk.* If the quarantine short-circuit regresses, the watchdog re-attempts a known-bad SHA every timer tick, repeatedly rebuilding, re-migrating and re-cutting-over production. If it regresses the other way, a good SHA stays blocked forever and every subsequent commit is undeliverable — including the commit that would fix it. If wd_require_ancestor stops firing, a force-pushed master silently deploys a history rewrite over production.

*Suggested test.* Add a fixture harness that sources watchdog.sh with a guard so main() is not auto-invoked (or extract main's gating into a testable function), points STATE_ROOT/SOURCE_REPO at a mktemp git repo, and drives: quarantined SHA == candidate → exit 0, no build; quarantined SHA != candidate → proceeds; processed == candidate with no component changes → takes the reconcile-only path; and a non-ancestor processed baseline → dies with the history-rewrite message. Also test watchdog-control.sh retry with a mismatched SHA and with no quarantine present.

<a id="infra--smoke-harness-rotted"></a>
#### `smoke-harness-rotted` — tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh cannot run at all, and nothing would have noticed

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh:25 and :29-31`

> Same defect as [`only-real-http-harness-is-ungated`](#rust-forward-proxy--only-real-http-harness-is-ungated) in `rust-forward-proxy`, found independently from another file. That entry carries the fuller treatment and the severity to triage at.

*Not covered today.* Both breakages are real and verified — tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh:25 calls `sub add`, which does not exist (crates/server/src/main.rs:244-282 defines only AddFile at :247), and lines 29-31 set an http loopback CLAUDE_API_UPSTREAM without CLAUDE_API_ALLOW_INSECURE_LOOPBACK_UPSTREAM=1, which validate_upstream rejects (crates/server/src/config.rs:104-140) and ev_upstream turns into a panic (crates/server/src/config.rs:148). The script is dead. But the productionRisk is overstated: the two invariants it claims to guard are covered by in-gate Rust unit tests — crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2266 route_spreads_sessions_across_fleet (200 distinct sessions must land on >=3 of 4 subscriptions with inflight simulation) and crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2062 route_pins_session_to_home (10 repeats of one session stay on one home), plus crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2078 for shared-affinity pinning — all executed by `cargo test --locked --workspace` at deploy/watchdog.sh:333-334. The upstream-validation half is also unit-tested at crates/server/src/config.rs:575. So a clustering regression would not ship undetected; what is missing is only the end-to-end HTTP-level reproduction of AUDIT.md:285-291.

*Production risk.* The documented regression guard for the anti-clustering invariants — even fan-out across the subscription fleet and cache-first session pinning — does not exist in practice. AUDIT.md:285-291 presents concrete pass numbers as if they were reproducible. A regression that clusters all traffic onto one subscription would burn that subscription's 5h window and rotate the whole pool into cooling, degrading service for every customer, with no automated detection. Compounding this: deploy/watchdog-lib.sh:371 classifies tests/* as an engine path, so editing this dead file triggers a full engine blue-green production deploy while the file itself is never executed.

*Suggested test.* Repair and wire it: replace `sub add --token` with `sub add-file --token-file` writing a 0600 temp file, add CLAUDE_API_ALLOW_INSECURE_LOOPBACK_UPSTREAM=1 to the serve env, drop the `2>/dev/null` masks so a CLI-surface change fails loudly, and remove the fixed 9099/9797 ports in favour of ephemeral ones. Then invoke it from deploy/watchdog.sh next to the two bash suites (line 343), gated on the debug binary the gate's `cargo test` already builds. If it is to remain manual, delete tests/* from wd_path_is_engine so a dead harness stops triggering production engine deploys.

<a id="infra--stage2-e2e-unwired"></a>
#### `stage2-e2e-unwired` — The strongest money-correctness harness in the repo is wired into no gate

**LOW** · verdict `ADJUSTED` · `deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:165-200`

*Not covered today.* deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh is indeed referenced by no gate (`grep -rn 'test-stage2-e2e'` hits only DEPLOYMENT.md:161 and docs/STAGE2_POSTGRES_AUTHORITY.md:126). But the premise that the authority path is otherwise near-uncovered and that the pg.rs test 'self-skips' is wrong for the gate: deploy/watchdog.sh:331-334 provisions a dedicated engine database and exports CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL into `cargo test --locked --workspace`, so crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1932 stage2_fault_matrix runs on every candidate. That test covers settle idempotency including a conflicting-amount replay (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2322-2332), outbox drain exactly-once (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2364-2365), epoch fencing of a stale writer (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2420-2421), concurrent capacity leases not overselling under a 9-thread barrier (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2469-2495), single leader lease (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2506) and the SQLite importer's anonymous-hold refusal (crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1997-2003). The accurate residual gap is the cross-process layer only: SIGUSR1 pre-drain readiness flip with the listener kept up (deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:187-195), the SQLite singleton-lock rejection of a second process (deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:139-146), and the PostgreSQL role least-privilege assertions (deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:178-185).

*Production risk.* The registry authority path it covers is otherwise near-uncovered (a single #[test] in crates/registry/src/pg.rs that self-skips without CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL). A double-charge or lost-charge regression in reserve/settle, or a fencing regression that lets both blue-green slots admit traffic, would ship green. The gate already provisions a disposable PostgreSQL with a dedicated engine database (watchdog.sh:331-334, watchdog-test-db.sh:38-39), so the main blocker — a database — is already solved.

*Suggested test.* Port test-stage2-e2e.sh to run against the gate's existing disposable engine DSN instead of the production commerce-postgres container: replace the `docker exec $CONTAINER psql` calls with psql against CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL, drop the root requirement, and invoke it from deploy/watchdog.sh after the cargo test step. Keep the three highest-value assertions verbatim — the 2:2:2:0:0:0 authority totals, the pre-drain 503-with-live-listener check, and the duplicate-singleton rejection.

<a id="infra--gate-syntax-scope-narrower-than-docs"></a>
#### `gate-syntax-scope-narrower-than-docs` — The gate's shell syntax check silently misses files the contributor docs say are checked

**LOW** · verdict `CONFIRMED` · `deploy/watchdog.sh:340-342`

*Not covered today.* The gate runs `bash -n` over `find $candidate/deploy -type f -name '*.sh'`. DEPLOYMENT.md:154 and CONTRIBUTING.md:27 both instruct contributors to run `bash -n deploy/*.sh deploy/apitoken-db-dump`. deploy/apitoken-db-dump is a /bin/sh script installed to /usr/local/lib/apitoken-watchdog/apitoken-db-dump (install-watchdog.sh:55) and executed by claude-api-backup.service; it has no .sh extension and is therefore never syntax-checked by the gate, only content-grepped (monitoring-config.test.sh:25-27, 199-201). Nothing outside deploy/ is syntax-checked either — tools/, systemd/ and tests/ are excluded.

*Production risk.* A syntax error in apitoken-db-dump passes deploy/tests green and breaks the hourly backup unit — the recovery point that watchdog-backup.sh validates and preserves before every migration. The failure surfaces as a stale dump, which watchdog-backup.sh:33 would then correctly reject, halting all deploys until an operator notices.

*Suggested test.* Change the find at watchdog.sh:342 to select by shebang rather than extension (`find $candidate/deploy $candidate/tools -type f -perm -u+x` filtered on a `#!/...sh` first line), and add an assertion in deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh that `bash -n deploy/apitoken-db-dump` succeeds, so the gate and the documented pre-push checklist agree.

*Verified absent by.* Gate scope read at deploy/watchdog.sh:340-342 — `find "$candidate/deploy" -type f -name '*.sh'`, nothing outside deploy/ and nothing without a .sh suffix. deploy/apitoken-db-dump line 1 is `#!/bin/sh` (no extension), installed at deploy/install-watchdog.sh:55 and executed by systemd/claude-api-backup.service:15 (ExecStart=/usr/local/lib/apitoken-watchdog/apitoken-db-dump). Its only test contact is content greps at deploy/monitoring-config.test.sh:25-27 and :199-201. Docs instruct the wider check at DEPLOYMENT.md:154 and CONTRIBUTING.md:27 (`bash -n deploy/*.sh deploy/apitoken-db-dump`). No shellcheck runs anywhere (`grep -rn shellcheck` finds only `# shellcheck` directives; .github/workflows contains only indexnow.yml). The stated containment is also correct: deploy/watchdog-backup.sh:33 rejects a stale dump, so the failure is fail-closed rather than silent — low severity is right.

---

## 9. Test-quality issues

Problems with tests that exist. These do not appear in any coverage number, and several are worse than a missing test because they read as protection that is not there.

### `rust-registry`

| Test file | Kind | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1934` | unreachable-or-skipped | The only test covering the 2518-line money authority begins `let Ok(url) = std::env::var("CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL") else { eprintln!(...); return; };`. A missing database is reported as a PASS. `cargo test -p registry` on any developer machine, in any CI other than the prod watchdog, and in any future runner that renames or drops that env var, is green with literally zero authority coverage — the crate's own CLAUDE.md:57 lists plain `cargo test -p registry` as the verification command. The only place the variable is set is deploy/watchdog.sh:333. There is no counter-assertion anywhere (no `#[should_panic]` sentinel, no required-var check, no assertion that the suite executed) that would distinguish 'authority verified' from 'authority not exercised'. Preferred shape: gate on a `postgres-tests` cargo feature the watchdog passes, or keep the skip but add a second always-on test that fails when the variable is absent *and* a marker file indicating a CI environment is present. |
| `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1933` | duplicated | stage2_fault_matrix is a single 585-line #[test] (pg.rs:1933-2517) covering roughly fifteen logically independent scenarios — SQLite import, topup idempotency, key policy CRUD, two thread races, reserve/settle/cancel lifecycle, crash-boundary drain, overdraft floor, epoch fencing, reconcile, pool-state CAS, capacity leases, leader election, invariant sweep. Consequences: the first failing assertion hides every scenario after it, so a break at pg.rs:2020 silently removes all concurrency and fencing coverage from the run; no scenario can be run in isolation to debug; and there is no per-scenario name in the failure output. It also opens with TRUNCATE of eleven tables (pg.rs:1940-1943), which is safe only because it is the sole PostgreSQL test — the moment a second one is added, `cargo test` parallelism will have them wiping each other nondeterministically, with no serial guard or per-test schema to prevent it. |
| `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2303` | brittle-snapshot | Because everything shares one account and one test body, later assertions hard-code balances produced by earlier ones: Some(400) at 2306, Some(750) at 2324, Some(450) at 2347, (650, 350, 0) at 2373, Some(550) at 2429. Inserting a scenario anywhere in the middle forces every downstream magic number to be recomputed by hand. This is not merely cosmetic — it is a plausible structural explanation for why so much of pg.rs is untested: adding a case is disproportionately expensive. Extracting a fixture helper (fresh owner + account + key with a stated balance) per scenario would make each case independently readable and cheap to add. |
| `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1981` | weak-assertions | The import_sqlite assertions check only (subscriptions, accounts, keys) == (1,1,1) and the three money totals. report.ledger_rows, report.usage_rows and report.pool_rows are computed by the importer (pg.rs:1790, 1845, 1893) and never asserted, and no imported row is read back from PostgreSQL for field-level comparison — the fixture's pool_state cooling_until=123 (pg.rs:1973) is written and then never checked. A regression that imported zero usage_events or zero pool_state rows would pass, which is exactly the class of defect the omitted-columns gap describes. |
| `crates/registry/src/lib.rs:2853` | weak-assertions | ledger_prune_is_disabled_without_consumer_watermarks asserts only that prune returns 0 when no ledger_consumer_checkpoints row exists. With no checkpoint row the inner JOIN produces no candidates regardless of the id predicate, so the test passes even if `l.id <= c.last_ledger_id` were inverted or the kind filter dropped. It proves the JOIN exists, not that the watermark is honoured. The money-critical half of the property — acked rows prune, unacked rows survive — is asserted on neither the SQLite nor the PostgreSQL implementation. |
| `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:2186` | weak-assertions | The spawned race threads call .unwrap() on connect and on reserve_request inside the closure (pg.rs:2187-2199, 2257-2279), and the parent then calls join().unwrap(). A genuine failure inside a worker surfaces as a panic-in-thread plus an opaque 'Any { .. }' from the join, losing the assertion context. Since these two tests are the crate's only real concurrency coverage, a flaky or newly-broken race is exactly where a legible failure message matters most; returning Result from the closure and asserting in the parent would preserve it. |
| `deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:1` | unreachable-or-skipped | This is the only end-to-end coverage of the authority — the real CLI cutover through import_sqlite, `db verify-postgres`, two engine slots billing concurrently against one PostgreSQL, the 2:2:2:0:0:0 authority-totals assertion (line 173-176), the engine role least-privilege and commerce-isolation checks (line 178-185), and SIGUSR1 pre-drain — and it is never executed automatically. deploy/watchdog.sh:340-344 runs only `bash -n` over deploy/*.sh plus watchdog-lib.test.sh and monitoring-config.test.sh; the script appears only as a manual instruction in DEPLOYMENT.md:161 and docs/STAGE2_POSTGRES_AUTHORITY.md:126. It also requires root (line 6) and a named docker container (line 12), so the unprivileged CI user in prepare_and_test_candidate could not run it as written. In practice this means its assertions have decayed into documentation. |

### `rust-pool`

| Test file | Kind | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1873, :1937, :1962` | weak-assertions | All three calibrating tests choose numbers where the observation equals the plan prior exactly ($5/0.10 = 50 with prior5 = 50; $12/0.04 = 300 with prior 300; $5/0.10 = 50 with prior 50). Because baseline == obs, the EMA at lib.rs:230 collapses to the same value as a naive replacement, as a pure clamp, and as 'keep the baseline'. The assertion `(cap - 300.0).abs() < 1e-6` therefore proves only that a sample was accepted, not that it was blended — while the comment two lines above the code it covers states blending is the point of the fix. Pick observations that differ from the prior so the 0.7/0.3 weighting produces a distinctive number. |
| `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1890` | weak-assertions | In calibration_rejects_implausible_or_concurrent_bootstrap_samples, the first sample is rejected by two independent conditions at once: `ours_explains` fails (du 0.00126 < d*baseline*0.5 = 6.0) and `plausible_cap(0.0315, 300)` fails. The test asserts only cap == 0, so it cannot tell which guard fired and would still pass if CALIB_MIN_PRIOR_FACTOR/CALIB_MAX_PRIOR_FACTOR were deleted entirely. Split into two cases, each isolating one guard. |
| `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2062, :2206, :2215, :2228, :2247, :2266, :2533` | unreachable-or-skipped | These tests exercise Pool::route(u64), which the crate's own CLAUDE.md calls legacy and which has no caller anywhere in the repository (verified: `grep -rn '\.route('` finds only these tests and axum's HTTP router). Production routes through peek_affinity_home_with_warm + route_affinity + pick (crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1084-1142). The behaviours look covered but the covered code cannot regress in production, while the code that can has two dedicated tests. |
| `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2516` | over-mocked | routing_uses_live_spend_between_headers reaches into `p.inner.write()` and hand-writes cap5h_usd/cap7d_usd rather than driving calibration through set_util/record_spend. The assertion it makes (strict `<` at the reserve boundary) is valuable, but the fabricated state means the test cannot notice if the real calibration path stops producing those caps, and it will silently rot if the Live fields are restructured. |
| `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:2266` | weak-assertions | route_spreads_sessions_across_fleet loops 200 sessions but only asserts `seen.len() >= 3` out of 4 subscriptions — satisfied within the first handful of iterations. It also calls mark_used without ever releasing, so after ~24 sessions every subscription is over the envelope and the remaining ~176 iterations run place_best's untested Reserve::FULL fallback with nothing asserted about the result. Either assert a distribution (e.g. each subscription receives at least 200/4/2 sessions with slots released between rounds) or shorten the loop and assert the fallback explicitly. |
| `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:92` | slow-or-flaky | MAX_INFLIGHT_CELL is a process-global AtomicI64 and cargo runs the crate's tests in parallel threads of one process. Nine tests derive their expectations from max_inflight() at runtime, so the moment any test calls set_max_inflight (lib.rs:97, used in production at crates/server/src/main.rs:858) the suite becomes order-dependent and intermittently wrong. set_max_inflight itself — including its `v.max(1)` clamp — has no test, so there is no safe place to cover it today. |
| `crates/pool/src/lib.rs:1798, :2303` | weak-assertions | soonest_ready is only ever checked with is_some() / > 0, never for the value. It is returned to paying clients as Retry-After via crates/forward/src/proxy.rs:1157 and :1501, so 'returns the minimum remaining cooling across roster subscriptions, and ignores dormant rows outside the roster' deserves an exact-value assertion with two subscriptions cooled for different durations. |

### `rust-forward-proxy`

| Test file | Kind | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs` | tautological | `local_err_body_is_anthropic_error_envelope` (proxy.rs:1924-1938) never calls `local_err`, `err_response` or `err_retry`. It builds the JSON envelope itself with an inline `serde_json::json!({"type":"error","error":{...}})` at line 1930 and then asserts that literal has the fields it just wrote. It is a test of `serde_json`. The actual response builder at proxy.rs:407-413 could change its envelope shape, drop the top-level `"type":"error"`, or emit the wrong content-type and this test still passes. Its own doc comment (1926-1927) additionally claims it verifies that Retry-After is set only for retryable reasons — it contains no Retry-After assertion at all, so the comment documents coverage that does not exist. Fix: call `local_err(reason, None)` and `local_err(reason, Some(5))`, then assert on the real `Response` — status, `content-type: application/json`, the deserialized body, and presence/absence of the `retry-after` header. |
| `crates/forward/src/affinity.rs` | unreachable-or-skipped | `redis_shares_opaque_affinity_across_processes` (affinity.rs:1483) is `#[ignore]`d behind a hard-coded `redis://default:test-affinity@127.0.0.1:16379/15`. The only automated gate runs `cargo test --locked --workspace` (deploy/watchdog.sh:334) with no `--ignored`, and no Redis service exists in deploy/watchdog-test-db.sh. It is the sole coverage for ~350 lines of Redis L2 logic and the sole assertion of the 'Redis stores only opaque digests' privacy invariant (1604-1612). The test itself is genuinely well written — concurrent claim with a single-winner assertion, cross-store resolution, and a full KEYS/GET/ZRANGE dump scanned for raw account names, session IDs, prompt text and subscription emails — which makes it worse, not better: real coverage exists on paper and delivers nothing. It also carries the only `assert_eq!(third.stats().redis_hits, 1)` in the file, so even the L2 hit path is unverified. |
| `tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh` | unreachable-or-skipped | Never invoked by anything. The only references in the repository are AUDIT.md:290, which calls it a 'регресс-guard' and reports concrete pass numbers, and its own header comment. `prepare_and_test_candidate` in deploy/watchdog.sh does not run it, and there is no Makefile or package.json script that does. Its companion `tests/mock_upstream.py` is also structurally limited: `_hit` unconditionally sends 200 (mock_upstream.py:47) and `do_GET` unconditionally sends 403, so no 429, no 5xx, no Retry-After and no `text/event-stream` can ever be produced — the harness cannot reach the rotation branches its filename implies, even when run manually. The documentation therefore overstates the guarantee twice over. |
| `crates/forward/src/affinity.rs` | weak-assertions | `redis_outage_fails_open_to_local_affinity` (affinity.rs:1404-1424) points the store at 127.0.0.1:1 and asserts `store.stats().redis_errors >= 1`. The `>= 1` is unfalsifiable in the useful direction — it cannot distinguish 'we tried Redis once per operation and fell back cleanly' from 'we retried the dead connection on every call and burned the 5ms timeout each time', which is a real latency regression on the hot path. Because the connection never establishes, the test also exercises only the `connection()` error branch (affinity.rs:680-710); `redis_claim`, `redis_resolve`, `redis_remember` and `redis_rebind` return early and their bodies never execute. The fail-open property it pins is worth having, but it should also assert an exact error count and that the `redis_retry_after` backoff (affinity.rs:155) suppresses repeated connection attempts within the window. |
| `crates/forward/src/nodetls.rs` | weak-assertions | `header_name_case_is_byte_exact_cc` (nodetls.rs:81) is named and documented as verifying the wire representation 'БАЙТ-В-БАЙТ', but it connects over `http://127.0.0.1:{port}` (line 105), so the TLS layer — GREASE suppression, the Bun cipher ordering, curve list, OCSP/SCT extensions and ALPN, i.e. everything the module's doc comment claims a JA3 match for — is entirely bypassed. The half it does test is excellent. The naming and placement, however, create a false sense that the JA3/JA4 claim in nodetls.rs:1-4 is under test, which makes a wreq upgrade look safer than it is. It also asserts substring presence via `req.contains(...)`, so a duplicated header would pass. |
| `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs` | duplicated | The three `LocalErr` tests (proxy.rs:1870, 1902, 1924) all iterate the variant set and all reason exclusively about `LocalErr::parts()`; none of them calls `local_err()` (proxy.rs:521), the function that actually constructs what a customer receives. `local_err_maps_to_authentic_anthropic_triples` (1902) restates the `parts()` match arms as a literal table, which is legitimate pinning for the 529/402 contracts and worth keeping. But the net effect of three tests is that the mapping table is pinned three times over while the response-construction path — status code application, `retry-after` header emission via `err_retry` (418), and the `.unwrap()` at proxy.rs:413 — is covered zero times. Consolidating 1924 into an assertion over real `Response` objects would remove the duplication and close the gap in one edit. |

### `rust-billing-meter`

| Test file | Kind | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| `crates/metering/src/lib.rs:926` | unreachable-or-skipped | `sse_abort_before_message_delta_charges_by_utf8_bytes` and `sse_abort_unicode_and_web_search_are_not_undercounted` (:944) feed `usage_from_sse` a transcript containing `content_block_delta` and `content_block_start` events and assert 4000 output tokens and 1 web-search request. The only production caller (meter.rs:203) can never supply those events because `retain_sse_usage` (meter.rs:166-174) keeps only message_start/message_delta/error. Both tests pass while the anti-abuse behaviour they name is dead code — false confidence on an explicitly money-critical invariant documented in crates/metering/CLAUDE.md. |
| `crates/registry/src/pg.rs:1933` | unreachable-or-skipped | `stage2_fault_matrix` is the only test that ever executes the settlement SQL that `forward/src/billing.rs`'s PostgreSQL writer drives, and it `return`s after an eprintln when `CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is unset (:1934-1936). Locally `cargo test --workspace` is green with zero authority coverage; only the prod watchdog exports the DSN (deploy/watchdog.sh:333). It is also one monolithic test, so a failure anywhere in it hides everything after that point. |
| `crates/forward/src/billing.rs:1370` | over-mocked | `canceled_sqlite_reserve_handoff_releases_key_allowance` hand-constructs the handoff atomic already in `RESERVE_HANDOFF_CANCELED` and pushes a raw `WriteCmd::Reserve` into the private `writer` channel. It therefore verifies one writer arm in isolation, never the real trigger — `ReserveHandoffGuard::drop` transitioning COMMITTED→CANCELED and dispatching `CancelReserve` (billing.rs:82-105) — nor the reply-send-failure branch (:436-444), nor `reserve_request`'s `claim()` handshake (:1015-1018). The assertions themselves are strong (balance, reserved_nano, key allowance, final state); the setup bypasses the concurrency the machine exists for. |
| `deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh:167` | weak-assertions | The only end-to-end money test asserts a count tuple `2:2:2:0:0:0` (settled reservations, charge ledger rows, done outbox, active leases, pool inflight, reserved_nano) and never asserts a charge amount, even though the fixture makes it exactly computable (mult_bp=2000, 10 in / 5 out on claude-sonnet-4 → 21000 nano). It also covers only the non-streaming path (the mock upstream returns JSON, :77-89) and requires root plus docker, so it is not part of the watchdog gate (deploy/watchdog.sh:316-344) — a pricing regression would pass it. |
| `crates/metering/src/lib.rs:992` | weak-assertions | `client_pays_9_percent_of_real_api` states as its premise 'Бизнес-правило владельца: клиент платит 9% ... независимо от модели' and asserts mult_bp=900. Production multipliers are 4000/3500/3000/2500/2000 per PRICING.md:38 and the e2e fixture uses 2000. The arithmetic is fine and it does add the only `cost_with_multiplier` coverage, but the test is documenting a pricing policy the system no longer uses, which is exactly the kind of comment a future reader trusts when changing rounding. |
| `crates/server/src/http.rs:1225` | brittle-snapshot | `embedded_admin_panel_exposes_all_operational_workflows_without_secrets` stands in for coverage of the new provider-spend read path with `assert!(ADMIN_PANEL_HTML.contains("period.providers"))` — a substring match on an HTML template. It passes if the handler at http.rs:712 returns an error and the panel renders an empty split, and it breaks on any cosmetic rename. The actual aggregation (billing.rs:1169 → registry `spend_by_provider`) is never invoked. |

### `rust-codex`

| Test file | Kind | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs` | weak-assertions | The fake app-server (runner.rs:783) logs only the stdin JSON-RPC frames it receives (`printf '%s\n' "$line" >> "$log_file"`, runner.rs:797). It never records its argv or environment. Consequently the tests can assert what was sent over JSON-RPC — baseInstructions, developerInstructions, dynamicTools — but cannot observe the other half of the prompt-isolation invariant: the ten `--config include_*=false` / `project_doc_max_bytes=0` / `mcp_servers={}` flags (process.rs:601-615) and the `env_clear()` allowlist (process.rs:588-597). `full_transport_preserves_prompt_boundary_events_and_exact_usage` (runner.rs:978) reads as a prompt-boundary test but its assertions cover only the JSON-RPC params, so the invariant it appears to guard is in fact unobservable by the harness. Two extra lines in the fake script (`printf 'argv=%s\n' "$*"` and an env dump) would close this. |
| `crates/forward/src/codex/api.rs` | weak-assertions | `all_public_errors()` (api.rs:2100) is a hand-maintained array literal of ProcessError/AdmissionError variants, not a compiler-enforced exhaustive match. Adding a new `ProcessError` variant that carries upstream text into `ApiError::message` compiles cleanly and both `public_errors_never_leak_internal_architecture` and `public_errors_keep_openai_shaped_status_and_type_pairs` keep passing while the leak ships. The tests are otherwise excellent; making the list derive from a `match` on a constructed value (or adding an `#[deny]`-style exhaustiveness helper) would make staleness a compile error. Separately, the leak scan covers only `error.kind` and `error.message` (api.rs:2180) — `error.param` and `error.code` are never scanned. |
| `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs` | slow-or-flaky | The pool tests spawn real `/bin/sh` children and depend on wall-clock behaviour: `wait_for_method` (runner.rs:964) polls a file 100 times at 10ms for a total 1s budget before panicking, and `dropping_an_inflight_turn_sends_interrupt` (runner.rs:1157) needs task-abort -> Drop -> `runtime.spawn` -> stdin write -> child append to complete inside it. The fixture comment at runner.rs:877-879 already acknowledges CI scheduling pressure and raises `startup_timeout_ms` to 10s for that reason, while `request_timeout_ms`/`turn_timeout_ms` stay at 2s. Since `cargo test --locked --workspace` (deploy/watchdog.sh:334) is the single gate that admits a SHA to production, a flake here quarantines a good commit. Consider making the guard tests deterministic by awaiting an explicit signal from the fake child rather than polling with a fixed budget. |
| `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs` | duplicated | Three near-identical `CodexModel` + `CodexConfig` fixtures exist: runner.rs:731/774, api.rs:2220/2242, chat.rs:1026. Each had to be edited by hand when the in-flight refactor renamed `codex_home` -> `homes` and added `health_probe_interval_secs` (visible in the diff for all three). The chat.rs copy additionally sets `startup_timeout_ms: 1, request_timeout_ms: 1, turn_timeout_ms: 1` (chat.rs:1034-1036), which is harmless for its parse-only tests but is a trap for any future test in that module that touches the transport — it will time out instantly and look like a product bug. A single shared `#[cfg(test)]` fixture module would remove both the churn and the trap. |
| `crates/forward/src/codex/runner.rs` | tautological | `reasoning_summary_streams_but_raw_chain_of_thought_does_not` (runner.rs:1121) asserts that after three updates the channel is closed (`updates_rx.recv().await.is_none()`), i.e. that `item/reasoning/textDelta` produced no update. But `TurnUpdate` has no raw-reasoning variant at all, so 'raw chain of thought does not stream' is guaranteed by the enum rather than by the `"item/reasoning/textDelta" => {}` arm the test means to pin. The test then asserts the opposite of its name at the data layer — `result.output[0]["content"][0]["text"] == "private chain of thought"` (runner.rs:1149-1152) — because redaction actually happens later in `normalize_output_item` (correctly tested in api.rs:2389). The name overstates what this layer verifies; the honest assertion here is that the raw text reaches `CodexTurnResult` and must be stripped downstream. |

### `rust-server`

| Test file | Kind | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/engine-client/src/index.test.ts` | tests-the-mock | All 13 tests supply their own `fetch` stub and assert against fixtures the test author wrote (e.g. index.test.ts:75-88 constructs the exact account JSON that engineAccountSchema then validates; :99-118 constructs the key-issue and policy responses; :218 constructs `{account, mult_bp, updated:1}` and then asserts setAccountMultiplier accepts it). The assertions therefore confirm the schemas agree with the test file, not with crates/server/src/admin.rs. The request-side assertions (bodies, URLs, header omission at :57-69, bigint preservation at :7-24) are genuinely valuable and should be kept — it is specifically the response-shape assertions that carry no information about the real engine. |
| `crates/server/src/http.rs` | brittle-snapshot | `embedded_admin_panel_exposes_all_operational_workflows_without_secrets` (http.rs:1220) asserts substring presence against a 58 KB embedded HTML blob, including a hard-coded `data-admin-panel-version="8"` (:1222), Russian UI copy `"Пароль (минимум 8)"` and `"Аккаунты движка"` (:1249, :1262), and raw JavaScript source fragments `"(values.first\|\|'').length<8"` and `"idempotency_key:idempotencyKey"` (:1261, :1264). Any cosmetic edit, copy change, minification or version bump fails the build, while a panel that is completely non-functional still passes. The negative assertions in the same test (:1257-1259, that no CONTROL_KEY / COMMERCIAL_ADMIN_KEY / SALES_ADMIN_KEY string is embedded) are the genuinely load-bearing part and should be split into their own stable test. |
| `apps/api/src/auth.service.test.ts` | over-mocked | The engine is injected as `{ createAccount, creditAccount } as unknown as EngineClient` (auth.service.test.ts:85, engine-provisioning.test.ts:10), so the mock satisfies neither the real class shape nor its validation. `creditAccount: async (account: string) => ({ account, balance_nano: "4000000000", ... })` (auth.service.test.ts:30, :168) skips EngineClient's own pre-flight checks entirely, and the assertions are of the form `expect(creditAccount).toHaveBeenCalledWith(...)` (:253, :326) — they verify the call was made, never that the engine would accept it. In particular nothing asserts that the `signup-bonus:${user.id}` ref satisfies the engine's provider-qualified-ref rule (crates/server/src/admin.rs:330); it happens to, by coincidence of formatting. |
| `crates/server/src/metrics_store.rs` | weak-assertions | `insert_and_read_back` (metrics_store.rs:124) is the file's only test and covers one of four public functions. It reads back 5 of the 25 inserted columns, so a column-order regression in the 25-placeholder INSERT (metrics_store.rs:85-102) — which would silently write util into the balance column — is only caught for the handful of fields sampled. The test also writes to a PID-named path in the system temp dir and only removes it on the success path (:154), so a failing run leaves a stale file that a re-run then reuses via `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, making the next failure harder to reproduce. |
| `crates/server/src/poller.rs` | unreachable-or-skipped | The four tests call `pool::now()` and construct `Live` values relative to real wall-clock time (poller.rs:637, :663, :679, :689). They pass today because the intervals are large, but they are time-dependent by construction rather than by injected clock — the same pattern that later becomes an intermittent CI failure. More significantly, they are the crate's only poller coverage, so `poll_loop`, `persist_loop`, `persist_state_cas`, `billing_recovery_loop`, `ledger_prune_loop`, `metrics_loop` and `owner_heartbeat_loop` — seven `pub async fn` totalling roughly 400 lines — remain entirely unexercised, and their signatures (`AuthorityConfig` by value, real DB connect inside `spawn_blocking`) provide no seam to change that. |
| `crates/forward/src/proxy.rs` | weak-assertions | `ct_eq_is_correct` (proxy.rs:1798) tests the constant-time byte comparison primitive but nothing above it. The three functions this crate's `/admin/*`, `/pool`, `/capacity` and `/metrics` gates actually call — `authed` (proxy.rs:286), `control_authed` (:314) and `readonly_authed` (:330) — have no tests, including `control_authed`'s loopback fallback at :322-325 which grants full control-plane access when both key lists are empty. Testing the primitive while leaving the policy untested gives the appearance of auth coverage without any. |

### `commerce-api`

| Test file | Kind | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| `apps/api/src/auth.service.test.ts` | unreachable-or-skipped | Every Postgres-backed suite in this domain is wrapped in describe.runIf(Boolean(process.env.TEST_DATABASE_URL)) — auth.service.test.ts:21, account.service.test.ts:10, admin-operations.service.test.ts:9, admin-accounts.service.test.ts:13, checkout.service.test.ts:30 and :102. Without that variable the suite reports success with the entire auth, provisioning, api-key and billing surface unexecuted; what remains are ~30 pure schema/helper tests. Only the production watchdog supplies it (deploy/watchdog.sh:328). A contributor running `pnpm test` locally gets a green board that proves almost nothing, and CI outside the watchdog would too. A hard failure when the variable is unset in CI (or an explicit vitest.setup that starts a container) would make the coverage honest. |
| `apps/api/src/auth.controller.test.ts` | over-mocked | All five controller test files construct the controller directly (e.g. `new AuthController({ register, resetPassword } as unknown as AuthService, ...)` at auth.controller.test.ts:27) and pass fabricated request/reply/RequestAuth objects. The reply is `{ header: vi.fn() }` and no assertion is ever made on what was written to it, so cookie issuance is executed but unobserved. Consequently the tests validate zod schemas and delegation, not the HTTP behaviour their describe blocks claim ('authentication provider status', 'authenticated profile updates'). The same shape recurs in account.controller.policy.test.ts, admin-operations.controller.test.ts and admin-accounts.controller.test.ts. |
| `apps/api/src/totp.service.test.ts` | tests-the-mock | makeService() (totp.service.test.ts:7-26) hand-rolls a database whose query() dispatches on SQL substrings — 'totp_secret = $2', 'totp_enabled = true', 'totp_secret = NULL' — copied from packages/db/src/totp.ts:22/30/38. The mock is a lenient re-implementation: it drops the real UPDATE's `AND totp_secret IS NOT NULL` guard (totp.ts:30), so a regression that enables TOTP without a secret would still pass here. Any rewording of the SQL in packages/db silently re-routes the mock rather than failing. The otplib and AES-GCM parts of these tests are genuinely real and worth keeping; the storage layer should be exercised against the migrated test database like the neighbouring suites. |
| `apps/api/src/account.service.test.ts` | tests-the-mock | FakeEngine hardcodes `account: "acct_alice"` in both the key-issue response (account.service.test.ts:194) and the /keys listing (:227) regardless of which account the client requested, and returns the single issued key for every account. The cross-user assertions still hold because findOwnedApiKey runs against real Postgres and short-circuits first — but it means the fake cannot express 'this key is no longer on this account', which is precisely the situation account.service.ts:259-262 was written to defend against, and no test can be written for renameApiKey's ownership re-verification without first making the fake account-aware. |
| `apps/api/src/admin-operations.controller.test.ts` | over-mocked | fakeOperations() (admin-operations.controller.test.ts:72-84) enumerates eight service methods but omits revokeSignupBonus, the one route on this controller that irreversibly debits customer money (admin-operations.controller.ts:83-98). The fake and the real service have silently diverged, and the omission is what makes the missing test invisible. |
| `apps/api/src/auth.controller.test.ts` | weak-assertions | The test named 'rejects attempts to submit another user id' (auth.controller.test.ts:68-80) passes { displayName, userId } and expects BadRequestException. The rejection comes solely from updateProfileSchema being .strict() (packages/contracts/src/index.ts:200); the controller never reads body.userId and would ignore it regardless. The test does pin schema strictness — worth keeping — but its name implies an authorization check that lives in SessionAuthGuard, which has no test at all, so it reads as coverage of the identity invariant when it is not. |
| `apps/api/src/auth.service.test.ts` | duplicated | Five near-identical ConfigService literals are constructed inline (auth.service.test.ts:32-38, 86-92, 170-176, 236-241, 288-294), each re-listing SESSION_TTL_SECONDS, AUTH_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY and the two TTLs. The OAuth suite at :236-241 omits EMAIL_VERIFICATION_REQUIRED entirely, so it silently relies on `undefined === true` being false rather than declaring the flag under test — exactly the kind of accident a shared fixture factory (`makeAuth({ verificationRequired })`) would prevent, and it matters because that flag gates whether registration issues a session at all. |

### `commerce-db`

| Test file | Kind | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/db/src/migrate.test.ts` | brittle-snapshot | Lines 56-66 loop over a hardcoded list of three migration filenames asserting the literal substring '"current_tier" BETWEEN 0 AND 5' is present and 'BETWEEN 0 AND 4' is absent. It is pinned to a fixed snapshot of history: a future migration 0022 that re-narrows the constraint passes untouched, and the negative substring assertion would also trip on an unrelated column named current_tier. It also does not generalise — the structurally identical hazard for referral_floor_bps (0014 sets BETWEEN 0 AND 9000, 0018 widens to 9500, and pricing.ts:202 validates 0..9500 in TypeScript) has no equivalent guard. Better: assert the live constraint on the migrated disposable database, or derive the bound from B2C_PRICING_TIERS.length. |
| `packages/db/src/migrate.test.ts` | unreachable-or-skipped | The file name suggests it guards migration safety, but it does not enforce the never-edit/rename/reorder/delete rule that MIGRATIONS.md calls out as absolute. That rule is enforced only by bash (wd_manifest_is_append_only, deploy/watchdog-lib.sh:337, called at deploy/watchdog.sh:638) and tested only in deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh:83-98. A TypeScript-only contributor reading packages/db has no signal that the rule is machine-enforced, and there is no TS-side assertion that migrations/meta/_journal.json entry count matches the .sql file count. |
| `packages/db/src/refund-eligibility.integration.test.ts` | weak-assertions | The test at line 101 is titled 'not_paid for a pending (activated but unpaid) checkout, not_found for unknown' but both assertions (:107, :108) expect 'not_found' — its own comment at :106 concedes the pending case is 'treated as not_found'. Consequently the reason:'not_paid' branch (payments.ts:80-82) and the reason:'already_refunded' branch (payments.ts:77-79) are never asserted anywhere, despite already_refunded being the guard against paying a refund twice. The title actively misleads a reader into thinking both states are covered. |
| `packages/db/src/refund-eligibility.integration.test.ts` | slow-or-flaky | The spend() helper at line 57 generates ledger_entry_id with Math.floor(Math.random() * 1e9) while engine_account_id is the constant 'acct_refund'. pricing_usage_events has UNIQUE (engine_account_id, ledger_entry_id) (migrations/0005_elite_aqueduct.sql:95), so this is a nondeterministic key in a suite whose failure quarantines a production SHA. Use a deterministic counter. |
| `apps/api/src/admin-operations.controller.test.ts` | tests-the-mock | Line 77 registers businessInvites: vi.fn() and the controller test asserts the mock was invoked. Combined with the fact that packages/db's real createBusinessInvite/lockBusinessInvite/setBusinessPricing have no test at all, the only 'coverage' of the B2B discount-invite feature is an assertion about a stub. apps/api/src/engine-provisioning.test.ts:40-45 has the same shape for b2b account provisioning. |
| `packages/db/src/pricing.unit.test.ts` | duplicated | Not a defect in itself — the four cases are good — but this 29-line file plus antifraud.unit.test.ts and auth.test.ts constitute the entire always-running test surface of a 5,777-line package. Every remaining assertion sits behind describe.runIf(TEST_DATABASE_URL). The effect is that a local `pnpm --filter @claude-api/db test` reports a green suite of pure string/BigInt helpers while zero lines of the money path execute, which makes local pre-push verification of pricing changes structurally impossible. |
| `packages/db/src/auth.test.ts` | weak-assertions | Twelve lines, two cases, covering only initialDisplayName. It sits in a file named auth.test.ts alongside auth.ts (287 lines) whose real risk surface — createEmailUser's multi-table transaction and its EmailAlreadyRegisteredError mapping (auth.ts:121-124), resolveAuthSession's revoked/expired/disabled filtering (auth.ts:190), consumeAuthRateLimit's window rollover (auth.ts:38-53) — is covered only incidentally from apps/api. The filename overstates what is verified here. |

### `payments-worker`

| Test file | Kind | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/payments/src/cryptomus.test.ts` | tests-the-mock | signWebhook at :139-142 computes the expected signature as JSON.stringify(body).replace(/\//g,"\\/") then md5(base64+key) — character-for-character the same transform as phpCompatibleJson at cryptomus.ts:231-233 and sign() at :190-193. The assertion at :56-61 therefore proves only that the implementation agrees with itself. The identical duplication appears again at apps/api/src/checkout.service.test.ts:220-222. It does validate the hash construction, but the actual interop risk — that our re-serialization differs from Cryptomus's PHP json_encode of the bytes they signed — is structurally outside what this fixture can express. No captured real payload exists anywhere in the repo (there is no fixtures directory). |
| `packages/payments/src/digiseller.test.ts` | weak-assertions | "rejects tampered checkout tracking" (:74-86) uses checkout_sig="00", which is 1 byte against a 32-byte HMAC, so decodeTracking returns at the length guard (digiseller.ts:219) and the timingSafeEqual branch is never executed with a same-length forgery. Replacing the comparison with an unconditional match would not fail this test. The test's name promises signature verification; it delivers a length check. |
| `packages/payments/src/platega.test.ts` | weak-assertions | :139-140 asserts only `.toThrow()` with no message or error-type argument for both the not-JSON and the missing-id cases, so a PlategaError and an unrelated TypeError are indistinguishable. Compare :185, which has the same problem for the Rapira rate rejection. The sibling Cryptomus tests do assert messages (cryptomus.test.ts:61), so the convention exists and is just not applied here. |
| `apps/api/src/checkout.service.test.ts` | unreachable-or-skipped | Both webhook describe blocks use `describe.runIf(Boolean(connectionString))` (:30, :102) keyed on TEST_DATABASE_URL. The same pattern gates packages/db/src/payments.integration.test.ts, email.integration.test.ts and pricing-free-first.integration.test.ts. Locally `pnpm test` reports green with the entire money path skipped and no warning; only deploy/watchdog.sh:328 supplies the DSN. This mirrors the known engine-side hazard in crates/registry (stage2_fault_matrix returning early when CLAUDE_API_TEST_DATABASE_URL is unset). A skipped-suite summary line, or a hard failure when NODE_ENV=ci and the DSN is absent, would make the gap visible to whoever is actually running the tests. |
| `apps/api/src/auth.service.test.ts` | over-mocked | EngineClient is substituted with object literals cast `{ createAccount, creditAccount } as unknown as EngineClient` (:around the createAccount/creditAccount stubs), and the same pattern appears in apps/api/src/engine-provisioning.test.ts and admin-operations.service.test.ts. The cast defeats structural typing, so these suites keep passing after a signature change in packages/engine-client, and the assertion `expect(createAccount).toHaveBeenCalledOnce()` verifies the stub was invoked rather than that a correct request reached the engine. Combined with the absence of any client/engine contract test, both sides of the Control API boundary can drift with a fully green gate. |
| `apps/worker/src/email-template.test.ts` | brittle-snapshot | :15-17 assert on `font-family:'JetBrains Mono'`, `background:#3767f0` and `logo-mark-light\.png` — presentation details with no behavioural meaning that will fail on any brand refresh, training the team to update assertions reflexively. The behavioural assertions in the same file (subject text, per-purpose copy isolation at :19 and :30, and token escaping at :33-37) are exactly right and should be what the file consists of. |
| `packages/engine-client/src/index.test.ts` | weak-assertions | "manages keys by a non-secret stable identifier" (:90-144) chains issueKey, listKeys, replaceKeyPolicy and disableKey through one fetch stub that dispatches on URL suffix. Because a single stub serves four different endpoints, a client that requested the wrong path would often still match a branch and return a plausible payload, and the per-call assertions mostly use `requests.at(-1)`. Splitting into four tests with per-endpoint stubs would make each contract independently falsifiable; as written the suite's most contract-sensitive test is also its least isolated. |

### `sales`

| Test file | Kind | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/sales-db/src/payout-batch.integration.test.ts` | unreachable-or-skipped | All five sales-db integration suites open with `describe.runIf(Boolean(connectionString))` where connectionString = process.env.TEST_SALES_DATABASE_URL (payout-batch.integration.test.ts:12-16, payout-periods.integration.test.ts:7/:17, analytics.integration.test.ts:7/:10, reconcile.integration.test.ts:7/:10, partner-lifecycle.integration.test.ts:7/:9). That variable is set only by the production watchdog gate (deploy/watchdog.sh:328). On any developer machine `pnpm --filter @claude-api/sales-db test` reports green while executing zero of the domain's money assertions — the only real coverage in the package. Every money invariant in the sales domain is therefore invisible until a commit reaches the prod host. A hard failure when the variable is absent (or a documented `pnpm test:integration` with a compose-provided DSN) would make the gap loud instead of silent. |
| `packages/sales-db/src/payout-batch.integration.test.ts` | weak-assertions | The test named "createPayoutBatch inserts rows, blocks a second in-progress batch..." (:55-75) performs the two createPayoutBatch calls sequentially and awaits the first. The advisory lock at payout-batch.ts:134 exists specifically for the SIMULTANEOUS case — its own comment says the `SELECT 1 FROM payout_batches` check alone does not block concurrent prepares — so removing the lock leaves this test green. The test name and the code comment both claim protection the test does not exercise; that is worse than no test, because it reads as covered. |
| `packages/sales-db/src/payout-batch.integration.test.ts` | weak-assertions | Line 114 is `void setBatchStatus;` — the function is imported at :7 purely to satisfy the unused-import check and is never called or asserted. Three further exports of the module under test are not imported at all: finalizeStuckSendingBatches (the anti-DoS release for batches stuck in 'sending'), getMaxOutstandingNonce (the nonce reservation that prevents reuse), and acquirePayoutSendLock/releasePayoutSendLock (the cross-process send lock). The suite's import list reads as comprehensive coverage of payout-batch.ts while three money-critical exports have none. |
| `packages/sales-db/src/payout-batch.integration.test.ts` | brittle-snapshot | Line 97 does `UPDATE payout_batches SET status='sent' WHERE id=$1` with the comment "free the in-progress lock" — the test reaches around the invariant it asserts twenty lines earlier in order to set up its next scenario. It also hard-codes schema internals (the payout_status enum cast at analytics.integration.test.ts:53 and payout-periods.integration.test.ts:58, direct commission_entries inserts everywhere), so an expand-only migration that renames a column breaks four test files rather than one fixture. |
| `packages/sales-db/src/payout-periods.integration.test.ts` | duplicated | Four suites each re-declare their own near-identical `partner()`, `earn()`, `deposit()` and `payout()` fixture helpers with slightly different column sets (payout-periods.integration.test.ts:32-61, payout-batch.integration.test.ts:27-42, analytics.integration.test.ts:22-54, reconcile.integration.test.ts:21-32). More importantly, every one of them inserts commission_entries with hand-written SQL rather than going through recordReferredSpend, so the entire due-list / cabinet / batch money story is asserted against rows the production writer never produced. A defect in how real commissions are shaped — wrong level, wrong applied_bps, wrong created_at, entries attached to the wrong usage event — would be invisible to all four suites. Extracting one shared fixture module that writes through recordReferredSpend would close both the duplication and the bypass. |
| `apps/sales-api/src/config.test.ts` | weak-assertions | The suite asserts defaults for eleven env vars but not one of the fourteen PAYOUT_* knobs (config.ts:41-54) that govern real on-chain spending — chain id 56, the USDT contract address, gas price, confirmations, and PAYOUT_ENFORCE_WINDOW defaulting to true. A typo in the default USDT contract or a flipped window default would ship green. It also does not assert the relationship between SALES_MIN_PAYOUT_USD (:38) and PAYOUT_MIN_USD (:50), which are two names for the same business rule read by different call sites. |
| `apps/sales-api/src/payout/chain.unit.test.ts` | over-mocked | testChain() (:5-15) is constructed with `readRpcUrls: []` and `sendRpcUrl: "https://bsc.invalid"`, which is honest for the offline properties actually tested — but it means the read-RPC fallback loop (chain.ts:70-80), assertNetwork's chain-id guard (:83), and checkReceipt's confirmation-depth check (:147-151) can never be reached from this file. The file's name (`chain.unit.test.ts`) and its 4/6 passing helper tests give the module an appearance of coverage while its three network-facing decisions — including the one that stops the sender from broadcasting to the wrong chain — are untouched. |

### `web`

| Test file | Kind | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| `apps/web/src/migration.test.ts` | brittle-snapshot | All 282 lines execute zero application code — every assertion is readFileSync + toContain/not.toContain over .tsx and .css source text. It pins exact CSS declarations including whitespace and property order: ':248 ".nav-links{display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:space-evenly"', ':250 "grid-template-columns:repeat(2,134px)"', ':256 ".pricing-intro{display:grid;grid-template-columns:repeat(2,minmax(0,1fr));gap:22px;align-items:stretch}"', ':262-263, :266-267'. Any prettier run, property reorder, CSS minifier, or 134px -> 136px tweak turns the production deploy gate red for a purely cosmetic change. The churn confirms it: 37 commits touch this file, including two whose messages are explicitly snapshot maintenance ('test(web): update migration snapshot for flex B2B card + full footer', 'test(web): align migration assertions with profile cleanup'). The cost is real and the protection is near-zero: because it only checks that a substring exists somewhere in a file, it cannot distinguish live code from dead code, cannot detect a broken conditional, and cannot fail when a component renders incorrectly. Notably its most valuable assertion — ':129 expect(dashboard).toContain("app ym-hide-content"), the guard that stops Yandex webvisor from DOM-recording API keys and balances — is verified only by substring presence, so a rename of the dashboard root className while a stale string survives elsewhere in the 1,626-line file passes. Recommendation: keep only the genuinely behavioural invariants and re-express them as assertions on rendered output (renderToStaticMarkup is already used successfully in provider-logos.test.ts), and delete the CSS-declaration pins outright — they duplicate what a visual-audit script is for. |
| `apps/web/src/lib/money.test.ts` | tests-the-mock | Not a mock issue but the same category of wasted signal: 3 of the file's 8 assertions (:6-8) test nanoToUsd, which has no production caller anywhere in apps/web — verified repo-wide; the only other nanoToUsd in the repo is an unrelated local function at apps/api/src/admin.service.ts:185. The function that actually renders the customer's balance is formatNanoUsd at apps/web/src/app/dashboard/dashboard.tsx:1569 and it has zero tests. So the file gives the appearance of money-formatting coverage while the live formatter is unprotected. Either delete money.ts:1-9 as dead code or make the dashboard use it, then move the tests to the live path. |
| `apps/web/src/app/auth/callback/oauth-callback.test.ts` | tautological | The entire file (9 lines) tests oauthFeedbackIsSuccess, defined at oauth-callback.tsx:11-13 as `return phase !== "error"` over a two-member union, by passing both members. The assertion restates the implementation with no independent expectation. The helper appears to exist only to give the module something exportable and testable, while the behaviour that matters — oauth-callback.tsx:22-30, where api.me() confirms the session, trackSuccessfulLogin fires and router.replace('/dashboard') runs, and where a rejection must set phase 'error' rather than leave the user on an apparently-succeeding screen — is untested. Replace with a jsdom test of the effect, following auth-entry-guard.test.ts. |
| `apps/web/src/public-product-truth.test.ts` | weak-assertions | Same readFileSync-and-grep technique as migration.test.ts. The negative copy assertions (:20-24, :29-41) do carry genuine value — they prevent advertising key controls the product does not implement, which is a legal/compliance concern — but they are enforced by regexes over concatenated source text, so they pass if the forbidden claim is phrased differently, and they mix that real invariant with fragile presentational pins in the same file (':40 expect(styles).toContain(".model-pricing-mobile{display:grid")'). Note the honesty invariant is already expressed better and per-locale at apps/web/src/lib/learn.test.ts:74-95 against parsed article content rather than raw file text; this file's :20-27 largely duplicates it. |
| `apps/web/src/components/provider-logos.test.ts` | brittle-snapshot | Asserts a literal SVG path fragment ('M41.4395 69.3848', :21) and exact width/height attribute strings (:11-12). Any re-export of the official GitHub Invertocat asset with different path precision fails the deploy gate without any visual regression. The valuable parts of this file are the aria-hidden and intrinsic-ratio assertions; the path-data pin is noise. |
| `apps/web/src/migration.test.ts` | duplicated | migration.test.ts:161-179 re-pins the B2C tier table by grepping pricing-tiers.ts for literal fragments ('{ code: "builder", label: "Builder", messageKey: "tier_builder", discountPercent: 65'), which apps/web/src/lib/pricing-tiers.test.ts:6-12 already pins properly by importing the module and comparing structured values. The grep version is strictly weaker (breaks on formatting, cannot see the actual runtime value) and doubles the edit cost of any tier change. Meanwhile neither of them checks the duplicate tier table at cost-calculator.tsx:115-121, which is the copy customers actually read. |
| `apps/web/vitest.config.ts` | unreachable-or-skipped | apps/web/vitest.config.ts:4 sets include: ["src/**/*.test.ts"], so any test written as *.test.tsx is silently collected by nothing and reports no failure. No .test.tsx currently exists, so this is a trap rather than an active hole, but it is exactly the kind of silent skip that hides a component test someone adds later — the natural extension when writing JSX-heavy React tests. Either widen to src/**/*.test.{ts,tsx} or add an assertion that the collected file count matches the number of *.test.* files on disk. Related: apps/content-studio has no vitest config at all and relies on defaults. |

### `infra`

| Test file | Kind | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh` | tests-the-mock | Lines 338-363 build a trap fixture that is a hand-written re-implementation of watchdog.sh's fail() handler, and lines 366-380 assert against that copy. The comment at line 334 calls it 'a behavioural check of the exact handler shape, rather than trusting the greps above', but the greps at 327-332 are the only link to the real function — and `grep -Fq '(( rc == 0 ))'` matches anywhere in the 812-line file. The real fail() also does things the fixture omits entirely: stopping the test database (watchdog.sh:95-97), writing rejected.sha (line 107), and dispatching the per-phase GitHub commit statuses (lines 116-124), none of which is exercised. Sourcing watchdog.sh with a guard and invoking the real fail() would close this. |
| `deploy/monitoring-config.test.sh` | tautological | Lines 10-12 construct role_sql with a printf copied verbatim from deploy/install-monitoring.sh:88, then assert that the copy expands to `DO $$ BEGIN ...` and contains no `\$\$`. The test never reads install-monitoring.sh. Reintroducing the exact quoting bug the comment describes — changing line 88 of the installer — leaves this test green. It should extract the line from the installer source (or invoke the installer's SQL-emitting function) rather than re-declare it. |
| `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh` | weak-assertions | Line 295 asserts `[[ $(grep -Fc 'balancer_is_ready' "$ROOT/deploy/api-bluegreen.sh") -ge 6 ]]` — an occurrence count over a 650-line file. It passes if the identifier appears six times in comments or in dead code, and it says nothing about whether the balancer readiness gate is actually consulted at the six decision points that matter. This is the entirety of the semantic coverage for the commerce blue-green controller. |
| `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh` | weak-assertions | Roughly 60 assertions are bare commands relying on `set -e` with no failure message: lines 248-253, 257-300, 423-429, 444-455, 492-497. When one fails the suite exits 1 printing nothing, and the watchdog then quarantines the SHA reporting only 'command failed at line N' of watchdog.sh (not of the test). The file already has wd_die available and uses it for its better assertions — the split is arbitrary, and every silent one costs an operator a re-run to locate. |
| `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh` | brittle-snapshot | Line 428 pins `data-admin-panel-version="8"` in crates/server/src/admin-panel.html. The same literal is hard-coded in the production verifier at deploy/watchdog.sh:494. Bumping the admin panel therefore requires a synchronised edit across three files in one commit; getting it wrong quarantines the SHA on a host where the fix must arrive as a new commit. The version should be read from one source (e.g. the test deriving it from admin-panel.html and asserting watchdog.sh greps the same value) rather than duplicated as a literal. |
| `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh` | brittle-snapshot | Lines 248-300 are ~35 literal greps and exact-count assertions over deploy/Caddyfile (e.g. `[[ $(grep -Fc 'reverse_proxy 127.0.0.1:3000 127.0.0.1:3001') == 1 ]]` at line 280, and `grep -Fc 'exclude http.handlers.reverse_proxy.health_checker.active' == 1` at line 290). Several encode genuine incidents and deserve to stay, but as a set they are a text snapshot of a config file that is never parsed by Caddy in the gate: benign reformatting fails them, and a semantically broken Caddyfile passes them. |
| `deploy/monitoring-config.test.sh` | brittle-snapshot | Line 53 asserts `[[ $(grep -Fc 'network_mode: host' "$ROOT/observability/compose.yaml") == 8 ]]` — an exact count with no statement of which services must be host-networked. Adding a ninth observability container fails the gate for a reason the message does not explain (there is no message). Lines 41-51 similarly pin eight full image digests; that pinning is correct and valuable, but the count assertion adds brittleness without adding a property. |
| `deploy/monitoring-config.test.sh` | weak-assertions | Lines 206-210 grep observability/ for `(password\|secret\|token)\s*[:=]\s*[A-Za-z0-9_-]{24,}`. The character class excludes `+`, `/` and `=`, so any base64 secret fails to match, as does any credential shorter than 24 characters or assigned via a different key name. It is defence in depth rather than a credential scanner, and reads stronger than it is. |
| `tests/rotation_fanout_smoke.sh` | unreachable-or-skipped | Executed by no gate and, as of the current tree, unrunnable: line 25 invokes a `sub add --token` subcommand that no longer exists (crates/server/src/main.rs:245-282 has only AddFile), with output masked by `>/dev/null 2>&1`; and lines 29-31 start the server with an http loopback upstream without CLAUDE_API_ALLOW_INSECURE_LOOPBACK_UPSTREAM=1, which panics at crates/server/src/config.rs:148. AUDIT.md:290 still cites it as a regression guard. |
| `deploy/test-stage2-e2e.sh` | unreachable-or-skipped | A high-value exactly-once-billing and fencing e2e that runs only when an operator manually invokes it as root against the production docker host (referenced only by DEPLOYMENT.md:161 and docs/STAGE2_POSTGRES_AUTHORITY.md:126). It is not in deploy/watchdog.sh's gate, so in practice it runs approximately never — while the gate already provisions a disposable engine PostgreSQL that would satisfy most of its requirements. |
| `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh` | duplicated | Caddyfile assertions are split across both suites with no shared helper — watchdog-lib.test.sh:248-300 covers vhosts, admin auth and upstreams while monitoring-config.test.sh:63-69 covers the Grafana auth-proxy headers and metrics block on the same file. Someone editing the Caddyfile has to discover two suites in two files to know what is pinned, and the two can drift into contradictory expectations. |
| `deploy/watchdog-lib.test.sh` | weak-assertions | The sudoers alias-usage loop at lines 407-411 checks each declared Cmnd_Alias with a substring `grep -Fq` against `grep -E '^deploy ALL=' -A2` output. The -A2 window happens to fit the current three-line grant at sudoers.d/95-apitoken-deploy:138-140; adding a fourth continuation line silently drops the last aliases from the check. Substring matching also means a future alias pair sharing a prefix would satisfy each other. install-sudoers.sh:52-55 already treats visudo warnings as fatal for exactly this reason — running `visudo -c -f` on the committed policy in the gate would subsume this hand-rolled check. |

---

Every meaningful source unit the audit reached, with its coverage classification. `strong` = a realistic regression would be caught; `partial` = the happy path or a subset is covered; `thin` = tests exist but would not catch a realistic regression; `none` = no test executes this code. Ordered worst-first within each area.
